# Research: Design-Review Eval

**Feature**: `014-design-review-eval` | **Date**: 2026-09-29 | **Spec**: [spec.md](spec.md) | **Base**: `main` at `be01974`

This file records why the spec says what it says. Its sources:

- **The audit of 2026-09-28** (§2). Five readers audited `swreview benchmark run`, `score` and `compare` against an eval-health checklist, one dimension each: cases, harness, grader, mechanism, and metrics and entry. They raised 79 findings. A second, adversarial reader re-checked 46 of them offline, reproducing each probe, and confirmed, re-rated or refuted each.
- **The anchor drafting of 2026-09-28** (§3). One reader drafted R-1 to R-8, read-only, from the engineers' debriefs of 2026-09-26 and 2026-09-28 and the owner's ruling of 2026-09-23, in neutral wording.
- **Two planning passes of 2026-09-28 and their cross-check** (§4). One planned decision 4A (the scorer), the other decision 6A (parity with the pane); both were read-only and tests-first. Between them they forced 23 lower-level choices, which decision 11A settled by their recommended defaults.
- **A four-lens review of the spec on 2026-09-29** (§5). Its lenses were the Spec Kit quality checklist (issues `CL-nn`), the constitution (`C-nn`), the code's facts (`Fnn`), and statistics and testability (`Snn`). The owner answered four of its questions (13A to 16A); the rest are settled by defaults D1 to D14.
- **A re-review of the second draft on 2026-09-29** (§5), by two readers (issues `RR-An`, `RR-Bn`). Round 1 of the third draft applied its fixes: defaults D15 to D17; revisions to D1 to D4, D6 to D12 and D14; and every mechanism moved out of the spec into §8.
- **A second re-review of the third draft on 2026-09-29** (§5; issues `RR2-1` to `RR2-7`). Round 2 applied its fixes: the private slice as its own study, one fictional profile for every committed case, the identical-arms null, the drop shapes, a bound on "no 10-point loss", fit matching on drawing documents, and one builder of ingested drawing sheets.
- **A third re-review of the third draft on 2026-09-29** (§5; issues `RR3-1` to `RR3-6`). Round 3 applied its fixes: one precedence below the floor; golden probes for every scored column and matching rule; false alarms of code-only calls as code origin, whoever calls the check; the scripted-answer refusal narrowed to intersecting ids; a testable definition of a local absolute path; and reference-level matching for joints, alignments, fits and stacks, which replaces round 2's generator constraint.
- **A fourth re-review and owner decisions 17A to 19A, 2026-09-29** (§1, §5; issues `RR4-1`, `RR4-2`, and 19 minor). Round 4 recorded the decisions (D15 and PP-8 become 17A and 18A; 19A, a product change, answers RR4-1), defined the simulated drop (RR4-2) and settled the minor issues; the owner then confirmed its pending narrowings as decisions 20A to 23A (RR4-m1). §5's disposition tables name where each round's fixes sit.
- **The plan's owner questions and its three review rounds, 2026-09-29** (§9). The owner answered questions 1, 2, 3 and 5 of §9.7 as decisions 24A to 27A and kept the defaults of 4, 6 and 7; question 8 stayed open that day. The plan's review fixes are RF-1 to RF-81 (§9.9 to §9.11). The same day the owner took decisions 28A (the committed event streams compressed, answering RF-78's size) and 29A (the tasks generated before the owner's plan review); RF-82 to RF-84 apply 28A (§9.12). On 2026-09-30 the owner answered question 8 with its option B as decision 30A (§1), and RF-85 and RF-86 settled two points the tasks had left open (§9.13).
- **The owner's review of the plan, 2026-10-06** (§1, §9.14). Done in CLAUDE.md's SMALL CHANGE form (31A: review the plan and tasks with CLAUDE.md's procedure, then commit the documents and start Phase 3, over 31B, skip the review and start, and 31C, trim the scope to slices 1 to 4 first; 32A: SMALL CHANGE, one question per section, over 32B, BIG CHANGE, up to four per section): one question per review section, Architecture, Code quality, Tests and Performance, numbered 33 to 36, each with lettered options, the recommended one first. The owner took option A in each section, decisions 33A to 36A; RF-87 to RF-91 apply them (§9.14).

Rules this file follows:

- Line numbers are at `be01974`. They drift; every task re-verifies before editing. Paths are under `reviewer/src/swreview/` unless another root is given.
- The debriefs and the handover files carry company data and are held outside the repository. They are cited by basename only, and nothing is copied from them.
- Designs are named by role: the big assembly, the small assembly. Every example value is fictional (`FICT-`).
- An owner decision (§1) is normative. A default (§4, §5) is reversible and names its alternatives. Where a review replacement conflicts with a decision or a default, the decision or default wins, and §5 says so. Where a later default changes an earlier one, both entries say so.
- Points no decision or default settles are marked **to resolve in plan.md**.

---

## §1 Owner decisions log

Decisions 1A to 12A were taken in the eval interview of 2026-09-28, 13A to 16A on 2026-09-29 from the spec review's questions, 17A to 19A the same day from the re-reviews', 20A to 23A the same day, confirming the fourth re-review's pending narrowings (RR4-m1), and 24A to 27A the same day from the plan's owner questions 1, 2, 3 and 5 (§9.7), whose options they list, and 28A and 29A the same day, from RF-78's repository-size arithmetic and the tasks step, and 30A on 2026-09-30 from the plan's owner question 8 (§9.7), whose options it lists, and 33A to 36A on 2026-10-06 from the owner's review of the plan, and 37A to 41A on 2026-10-07 from five questions the implementation raised. That review was done in CLAUDE.md's SMALL CHANGE form (31A, 32A): one question per review section, numbered 33 (Architecture), 34 (Code quality), 35 (Tests) and 36 (Performance), each with lettered options and the recommended option first, which the owner took in all four. The chosen option is always the owner's. For 1A to 12A the interview recorded the choice; the alternatives listed are the ones the audit and the planning material had put on the table for that question, reconstructed from those sources rather than quoted. For 13A to 23A the alternatives are the reviews' own proposals, named by the issue or default that raised them. For 33A to 36A the options recorded are the one the owner took and the plan as it stood before it; the review's other lettered options are not recorded here. For 37A to 41A the options recorded are the ones offered, the recommended option first; question 38 offered two. 47A and 48A were answered on 2026-10-08, from two questions the implementation raised: option A is recorded in the owner's words, and the other options are reconstructed from the reasons A gives, rather than quoted.

### 1A. Providers (2026-09-28)

- **Question**: which providers the eval may call, including any future grader.
- **Options**: A: the product's providers only, OpenAI (default) and Gemini; any future judge is OpenAI or Gemini, never Claude. B: a grader or judge on a provider the product does not ship.
- **Chosen**: A. The eval measures the product, whose models are OpenAI and Gemini only.

### 2A. The flow under evaluation (2026-09-28)

- **Question**: which flow the eval measures.
- **Options**: A: the agentic design review of a package, graded against answer keys (`benchmark run`, `score`, `compare`). B: the replay gate, which replays recorded rounds through a scripted provider; the audit found it cannot see a change in model behaviour (replay-gate-freezes-model, §2). C: the deterministic checks alone against golden outputs, which the golden suite already covers.
- **Chosen**: A.

### 3A. The first study and its headline (2026-09-28)

- **Question**: what the first study compares, and which number decides it.
- **Options**: A: the Task Pane defaults (checks-first pre-run, withheld pre-run tools, payload slimming, history pruning, dropped prior reasoning, parallel tool calls) against all levers off; the headline is recall on defects only the model can find, plus false alarms; recall on defects code finds is a regression guard. B: one lever at a time against the all-off baseline, as the existing harness is built (`specs/005-llm-efficiency/contracts/ab-harness.md`). C: one lever-off arm per pane default, measured from a pane baseline (the audit's pane-defaults-adopted-on-estimates suggestion). D: overall recall as the headline.
- **Chosen**: A. The pane defaults were adopted on offline replay estimates (`agent/settings.py:505-514`); no measured run has checked their quality. Overall recall is dominated by code-produced defects (recommended-set-saturated-by-code, §2), so it cannot be the headline. **Later** (17A): only model-origin false alarms decide; code-origin ones are shown.

### 4A. The scorer (2026-09-28)

- **Question**: how the scorer's defects are fixed.
- **Options**: A: in place, tests first, with the behaviours of User Story 1. B: a second scorer beside the old one, keeping old scorecards comparable. C: label the quality columns "not meaningful" and fix them later (the audit's interim for cover-blind-tap).
- **Chosen**: A. Old scorecards are refused and re-scored (SP-11, §4).

### 5A. Where the cases come from (2026-09-28)

- **Question**: the source of the case set.
- **Options** (the audit's inventory of sources, §2): A: a committed seeded-case generator, plus a private held-out slice of real designs whose keys the owner writes and which stays outside the repository. B: promote golden fixtures to benchmark packages, each with a hand-written key. C: write keys for the committed fictional fixtures (mechanical, drawings, sitting) and the scrubbed replay recordings. D: scrub one handover dump per distinct design and commit it with a key.
- **Chosen**: A. The builders behind B and C become the generator's building blocks (§7). **Later** (round 2, FR-056; confirmed by 23A): the private slice is its own study, never in the pane study's verdict.

### 6A. Parity with the pane (2026-09-28)

- **Question**: how `benchmark run` comes to run what the pane ships.
- **Options**: A: `benchmark run` accepts the pane's configuration through the resolver `review` already uses, and records it (User Story 2). B: label every benchmark scorecard "pane levers off" and never quote it as the product's (the audit's interim). C: approximate the pane with four `--lever` flags; this cannot express the model view or the profile, and the baseline arm forbids it.
- **Chosen**: A.

### 7A. The anchors (2026-09-28)

- **Question**: which real cases anchor the set.
- **Options**: A: the eight real cases R-1 to R-8, and the big assembly's three analyst-found defects taken as real (M4 screws in M5x0.8 tapped holes, a 0.75 mm dowel offset, interference groups of about 250 mm³). B: only cases with the seat engineer's own verdict; this drops R-4, whose verdict is the owner's ruling, and the three analyst-found defects, which have no engineer verdict.
- **Chosen**: A.

### 8A. What the key grades beyond matched defects (2026-09-28)

- **Question**: whether the key grades the pilot's non-defect failures, and by what.
- **Options**: A: forbidden findings, expected coverage and question rules, all graded by code, with no judge. B: add a judge (OpenAI or Gemini under 1A) for prose, such as R-2's drawing advice. C: matched defects only.
- **Chosen**: A. Grading the prose of R-2's advice is deferred; its structure is graded by code.

### 9A. Scripted answers (2026-09-28)

- **Question**: how behaviour between turns is observed.
- **Options**: A: cases carry scripted engineer answers, played through the answer path the pane uses; the final state is graded. B: one unattended turn, as today (`agent/runner.py:1815-1848`); R-1, R-3, R-6 and the dropped-reasoning lever stay unobservable. C: credit an open evidence request as flagged, with no answer turn (the audit's option a in unresolved-answer-scored-as-miss).
- **Chosen**: A.

### 10A. Set size (2026-09-28)

- **Question**: how large the set must be.
- **Options**: A: large enough to detect a 10-point drop in model-judgement recall: about 34 held-out model-produced defects at 3 repetitions. The figure comes from the audit's noise-floor arithmetic: a ±10-point 95% half-width needs n·R ≥ 100 defect-trials. B: the audit's unpaired 80%-power figure, about 130 defects at 3 repetitions (about 390 trials per arm). C: a smaller set, with its wider noise floor stated.
- **Chosen**: A. **Revised 2026-09-29 by 14A**: 34 is the floor, and the size is set from a pilot (§6).

### 11A. The 23 planning choices (2026-09-28)

- **Question**: the two planning passes forced 23 lower-level choices; decide each, or take the planners' recommended defaults.
- **Options**: A: take the recommended defaults, recorded with their alternatives and reversible (§4). B: answer each before planning.
- **Chosen**: A. Five change behaviour a user would notice: five origin buckets rather than three (SP-1, now seven: D9); a scorer version that refuses scorecards scored under the old rule (SP-11); `review --pane-defaults` also running the explanation pass (PP-1); explanations held equal, and stated, on both arms of the pane study (PP-2); and the pane study's decision row guarding the call counts of the fit and axial-stack checks (PP-8; narrowed by 18A: shown, never deciding).

### 12A. Delivery (2026-09-28)

- **Question**: the delivery shape and order.
- **Options**: A: one feature, in order: the scorer; parity with the pane; the key extensions and scripted answers; the generator; the report and runner robustness; each part test-first. B: a separate feature per part. C: robustness first.
- **Chosen**: A. Robustness (User Story 7) still holds before any paid study, whatever its place in the order. **Later** (14A; round 1): the simulator (User Story 8), the pilot and the cost gate follow robustness and precede the paid study.

### 13A. An unresolved finding on a defect expected demonstrated or suspected (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (CL-03, C-5, F2): what an unresolved finding scores when it matches a defect whose expected status is demonstrated or suspected, or that states no status. Model-judgement defects (a fit with no tolerance, a drawing reading) often end unresolved, so the answer moves the headline directly. Today an unresolved finding never credits anything (`benchmark/scorecard.py:261-263`).
- **Options**:
  - A: every known defect MUST state its expected status. An unresolved finding on a defect expected demonstrated or suspected is counted as *flagged below expected*, in its own column, and does not count toward headline recall. The approved 4A rule stays: an unresolved finding on a defect expected unresolved credits it as flagged and counts toward recall; a demonstrated or suspected finding on a defect expected unresolved credits it and counts as a status mismatch.
  - B (CL-03): an unresolved finding credits any defect as flagged and counts toward recall; on a defect not expected unresolved it is also a status mismatch.
  - C (F2): also credit, as flagged, an evidence request or an unresolved coverage item on the defect's parts.
  - D: no change; an unresolved finding never credits.
- **Chosen**: A. A lever that makes the model hedge, answering unresolved where the evidence supports a verdict, must show as a loss; under B it would score as found. C is not taken: D8 makes every committed defect reachable through findings instead. A flagged-below-expected finding is not a false alarm (C-5).

### 14A. Set size and repetitions (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (S1, C-1, CL-12, CL-13): whether 34 held-out model-produced defects at 3 repetitions can detect a 10-point drop. The review's simulations say only if about three defects in four are found on every run or on none (§6). SC-005's ±10-point 95% interval gives about 50% power against a 10-point drop, not 80%.
- **Options**:
  - A: 34 held-out model-produced defects is the floor, not the plan. Before the paid pane study, a small paid off-arm pilot measures each held-out model defect's detection rate and how the defects of one package move together. Repetitions grow first, then cases, until simulated studies detect a true 10-point drop in model-judgement recall at least 80% of the time, with identical arms called a loss at most 5% of the time. The owner approves the resulting size and cost at the cost gate (likely 6 to 10 repetitions if detection rates are spread).
  - B (S1's rewrite of 10A): keep 34 at 3, and state the assumption that the per-defect paired difference has a standard deviation of at most 0.20.
  - C (C-1): fix a larger plan now: about 80 to 100 defects at 3 repetitions, or 6 to 9 repetitions at 34.
  - D: keep 10A and SC-005 as written.
- **Chosen**: A. The first draft's SC-005 (a ±10-point interval) becomes a simulated criterion at the approved size: the recall rule on the main figure detects a true 10-point drop at least 80% of the time under both drop shapes, and the whole verdict calls identical arms a loss, or a true 10-point drop "no 10-point loss", at most 5% of the time each (D17; since round 2 the power, the drop shapes and identical arms are the spec's Terms; D17's reading of identical arms is 21A). A planned standard error of at most 3.6 points (about ±7) is the arithmetic approximation (§6). SC-010's simulator prints its inputs, which §8.2 lists. **Later** (round 4, RR4-m15; confirmed by 22A): FR-055 caps repetitions at 10, or the owner's figure at the cost gate, before cases grow (§6).

### 15A. Held out is decided per graded item (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (CL-14, C-9, S9): what may be held out, when feature 013's prompt and checklist were written from the pilot sittings on the small assembly (the R-1 to R-3 behaviours: `specs/013-engineer-first-review/spec.md:11`; `agent/checklist_v1.yaml:19-21, 60, 90-91`), and 5A calls the private real designs held out.
- **Options**:
  - A: decide per graded item. Anything the prompt or checklist was written around, meaning every anchor R-1 to R-8 behaviour and every debrief item, is tagged *tuned-on* and graded as a regression guard, never counted in held-out recall. Everything else in a case, including the big assembly's three analyst-found defects, may be held out. Each held-out item records the prompt and checklist digests it was held out against, and loses held-out status in the same commit a prompt or checklist change cites it.
  - B (C-9, S9): decide per case. A case whose anchor led to a prompt or checklist change is development-only, and the 34 are counted on other cases only.
  - C: the spec as drafted: a held-out case "may not be one the prompt or checklist was written around", with no recorded digest and no rule for losing the status.
- **Chosen**: A. B would make both private real designs unusable as held out, since both were in the sittings. C gives no checkable record, and no protection against later tuning in a public repository where coding agents edit the prompt and can read committed keys. **Later** (25A): the stamp also digests every registered tool description the model reads, and an item loses held-out status in the commit of a prompt, checklist or tool-description change that cites it.

### 16A. Who produces a defect (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (F1, CL-01, S10): who produces a defect. The drafted list ("fastener, fit, interference, drawing and part-role judgement" as defects only the model can find) is wrong about the code. Under checks-first the pre-run judges every interference group and runs the joint map, hygiene, mass and drawing checks (`prerun.py:144-216, 705-745`; `tools/checks_mechanical.py:76, 297-337`); part roles are classified, and their question asked, by code (`checks/part_roles.py:659-720, 881-900`). A set weighted that way would plant code-produced defects and repeat the audit's rms-part failure.
- **Options**:
  - A: decide per defect, in its own package. Model-produced: fits and axial stacks the model must set up; drawing readings; fastener or alignment joints whose geometry is deliberately withheld, so only a check the model sets up can find them. Code-produced regression guards: interference; joint-map fastener and alignment defects; and any defect a pre-run or argument-free check can find from the package's evidence. The generator records, per model-produced defect, which evidence it withheld.
  - B (the spec as drafted): decide by family.
  - C (S10): code only for tools in the pre-run list; a defect found by any check the model must call, with or without arguments, is model-produced.
- **Chosen**: A. C would tag argument-free checks that the off arm's model must call (the all-off arm has no pre-run) as model judgement, which measures tool-call compliance (rms-key-measures-code, §2). A ties the tag to the evidence in the package, and the validation's code-only check (D8, FR-054) verifies it offline. **Later** (round 2, RR2-4): fits and axial stacks are generable as model-produced only through ingested drawing sheets, so FR-027 adds one builder (D14.1, §8.14).

### 17A. False alarms that decide (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (RR-A2, RR-B6, RR3-3; default D15 until now): which false alarms decide the pane study's verdict.
- **Options**: A: only model-origin false alarms, the model's own findings and those of checks the model chose to call; pre-run and regrade (code) false alarms are shown with a paired interval and named, never deciding. B (RR-A2's second option): every origin decides. C (RR-B6): every origin, anchor packages left out of the rule.
- **Chosen**: A, which was D15. The pre-run runs the argument-free checks on every on-arm run and the off arm only when its model calls them, so their false alarms measure checklist compliance, not judgement (fa-rate-rewards-ignoring-instructions, §2), on the generated cases too (against C). 20A reads "checks the model chose to call" as configured checks, a code-only call's false alarms being code origin whoever makes it (RR3-3); D15 keeps tuned-on hits out (15A).

### 18A. The fit and axial-stack call counts (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (RR-A19, CL-22, F10): whether PP-8's guard (11A: fit and axial-stack calls per run must not fall) decides the pane study's verdict. D1 had overridden it until the owner decided.
- **Options**: A (D1): shown per arm with a paired interval, named when the interval excludes zero, never deciding. B: PP-8 as 11A took it, a fall deciding.
- **Chosen**: A. The counts are a proxy for fit and stack judgement, which recall on model-produced fit and stack defects measures directly (3A, 16A); a deciding count spends the 5% budget. Lever studies keep the counter.

### 19A. Joint references on every path (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (RR4-1, a blocker against RR3-6): an unresolved fastener result carries no references (§8.12), so under FR-003 it matches no reference-keyed entry: cover-blind-tap's D-1 and every fastener defect expected unresolved are unreachable (FR-054), and a hedged joint finding can never read flagged below expected.
- **Options**: A: a product change: every fastener-joint and hole-alignment result records its fastener and hole ids (the joint's references) on every path, unresolved included. B: the four argument-taking tools cite their step (SP-3's B) and the scorer reads references from the call's arguments. C: part-level matching for a result naming no references, and a generator rule against a second joint between the same parts.
- **Chosen**: A. A finding should point at the exact entity it judges (constitution Principle IV), for the engineer as for the scorer; B misses the joint map's own results, and C brings back the sibling credit RR3-6 removed. Consequences (§8.12): the tool results the model reads change slightly, and golden and replay fixtures regenerate in the same change (feature 008's decision 3A: fixtures follow the code). FR-061 reads the references per check: `fastener.unsupported` also records the hole id wherever its call names a hole (it is returned by the joint checks, `fastener.py:729-730, 903-904`), and `fastener.head_fit` keeps its one reference (§8.12).

### 20A. A code-only call is code origin (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (RR3-3; the round-4 refinement of 17A): whether a model's call of a tool the pre-run also calls, or of a check with no arguments, is model or code origin.
- **Options**: A: code origin whoever makes it; only configured checks (the model sets the arguments) are model origin. B: every call the model makes is model origin.
- **Chosen**: A. Such a tool gives the same findings whoever calls it; under B an off-arm model that runs the hygiene check is charged with "model" false alarms, which brings back fa-rate-rewards-ignoring-instructions (§2).

### 21A. What identical arms means (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (D17, round 4): the null of 14A's 5% bound, when the arms necessarily differ in the pre-run.
- **Options**: A: equal model behaviour in both arms, code-produced defects detected as each configuration does; both arms at the off arm's code rate is a printed stress case, not bounded. B: a literal A/A test of one configuration.
- **Chosen**: A. B is easier to state but does not test the question 3A asks.

### 22A. The repetition cap (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (RR4-m15): when the pilot-driven sizing of 14A stops adding repetitions and adds cases.
- **Options**: A: a cap of 10, which the owner may change at the cost gate. B: no cap. C: a cap of 6.
- **Chosen**: A (FR-055, §6, §8.2).

### 23A. The private slice (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (round 2, FR-056): the private real designs need the owner's real profile, the committed set the fictional one, and one study may not mix profiles (D5).
- **Options**: A: its own study, set and row, reported beside the pane verdict, never deciding it. B: its own study with its own verdict; a loss in either is a loss.
- **Chosen**: A. Most of its items are tuned-on (15A), and a verdict resting on two designs is mostly noise.

### 24A. Clean twins (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (§8.11; plan question 1, CS-4): one fully clean twin per core case, or one twin per removed family?
- **Options**: A: §8.11's rule B, one twin per core case with both seeds in their clean form, each removal keyed as a correct condition: about 47 packages, 94 × R paid reviews per study, no key-format change. B: rule A, one twin per removed family keeping the other defect, marked "counted elsewhere": about 65 packages, 130 × R, a new key marker.
- **Chosen**: A. Twins feed only the false-alarm rule, never recall power, so B's 18 extra packages per arm and repetition buy nothing the verdict reads; FR-028's "kept defects count only in their own case" holds with nothing to mark (CS-4).

### 25A. The held-out stamp covers the tool descriptions (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (plan question 2; amends 15A): should `held_out_against` also digest the tool descriptions the model reads?
- **Options**: A: the system prompt, the checklist and every registered tool description the model reads, so a description change forces the same cite-or-restamp decision. B: the prompt and checklist files only, as 15A says.
- **Chosen**: A. The tool docstrings state review policy (§2, prompt-encodes-cbt-answer), so tuning one on a held-out defect would leave the defect held out under B; such changes are rare and already pinned (`test_tool_payload`). 15A's rule reads: an item loses held-out status in the same commit as a prompt, checklist or tool-description change that cites it. Applied by RF-64 (§9.11).

### 26A. A context overflow is a behavioural stop (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (plan question 3; D2, FR-040): is a review that overflows the model's context window (OpenAI's `context_length_exceeded`, Gemini's input-token limit) behavioural or infrastructure?
- **Options**: A: behavioural, class `provider_stop` with its reason, as D2 reads the step and output limits. B: infrastructure, excluded and re-run. C: infrastructure first, behavioural if the resumed attempt overflows again.
- **Chosen**: A, extending D2. Two pane defaults shrink the context, so the off arm is likelier to overflow; excluding its overflowing runs would remove the off arm's worst runs, the survivorship bias D2 exists to prevent, and C pays again for a run likely to overflow again.

### 27A. The paid pilot's size (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (plan question 5; FR-055, 14A): how many repetitions the paid pilot runs.
- **Options**: A: 3 off-arm repetitions over the whole committed pane set. B: 2 (co-movement barely estimable). C: 5 (about 1.7 × the pilot's cost, tighter estimates).
- **Chosen**: A: the minimum that shows co-movement, at least 102 defect-trials; the sizing's conservative shock SD covers the rest (VD-8, VD-9).

### 28A. The committed event streams, compressed (2026-09-29)

- **Question** (RF-78's size, §9.11): in what form the committed study and pilot folders carry their event streams, which a re-score, `attempt_facts` and the report read. Plain, a study at the cap puts about 102 MB of streams into every checkout's working tree, up to about 513 MB at the big assembly's stream size, and the pilot about 15 MB (RF-78's arithmetic).
- **Options**: A: gzip-compressed, `events.jsonl.gz`, with each stream's sha256 digest recorded; the `.gitignore` exception targets the compressed form; readers (re-scoring, the report) read it transparently; the plain `events.jsonl` stays the working form while a run writes. B: the streams archived outside the repository with their digests committed: the committed folders stay small, but a re-score or a rebuilt report needs the archive, and a checkout without it reads withdrawn hits `null` and cost unknown, RF-78's loss. C: the plain `events.jsonl` committed, as RF-78 first decided.
- **Chosen**: A. About 12 MB per study at the cap in every working tree (39 MB at the big assembly's size), nine to thirteen times smaller than C; every committed study stays re-scorable and reportable from the repository alone, as B's are not; and the digest names each stream as the run wrote it, whatever compressed it. Applied by RF-82 to RF-84 (§9.12): an explicit `benchmark pack` step before a folder's commit, one resolver of a folder's stream beside the one reader, and the git rules and their tests.

### 29A. Tasks before the owner's plan review (2026-09-29)

- **Question**: whether the tasks are generated before the owner reviews the plan. The constitution's gate reads "the plan is reviewed before task generation" (`.specify/memory/constitution.md`, Development Workflow); the plan has had three review rounds (§9.9 to §9.11), and the owner has not yet read it whole.
- **Options**: A: generate the tasks now; the owner reviews the plan and the tasks together before implementation. B: the owner reviews the plan, then the tasks are generated.
- **Chosen**: A. The tasks are derived text: read beside the plan they show each slice's work and failing tests, and no code moves before the owner's review. The constitution's gate is met by the plan's three review rounds; the owner's own review of both precedes implementation. Owner question 8 (§9.7) then stayed open: slice 4's tasks followed its option B, as the plan did, to change if the owner answered otherwise; 30A (2026-09-30) answered it B, so they stand.

### 30A. Held-out cases take no answer turn (2026-09-30)

- **Question** (plan question 8; FR-022, FR-059, 9A, CS-9, RF-14): should held-out cases take an answer turn, so the verdict can see the pane default that acts only between turns (lever 14, dropped reasoning, `agent/settings.py:482-488`)? Under CS-9 every held-out defect sits in a core case with no case file, so each held-out review is one turn, and on OpenAI the verdict cannot see one of the six settings the arms differ in.
- **Options**: A: every core case and its twin (36 packages) scripts one answer to its likeliest question (the fit or the fastener item), worded so it states nothing about a planted defect: at most one answer turn per package run, and only when the model asks it; up to 72 × R more paid turns per study beside the 94 × R reviews, and 36 answers for the case author to write and the validation to run. B: CS-9 as is; the verdict names dropped reasoning as a setting it does not test, and the anchors show it with paired intervals (FR-059). C: a follow-up entry kind, one scripted follow-up message per core case: a driver path, a case-file field and paid turns, as question 4's B.
- **Chosen**: B: a held-out review that takes no answer turn is inert to lever 14 in the pane as well, so the verdict is not wrong about the reviews it measures, and B states the gap on every verdict; A's answers are the case author's words beside the planted defects, a risk to the held-out figure the study exists to protect, at up to 72 × R paid turns. CS-9 and RF-14 stand as the plan's rule (§9.13); slice 4's tasks stand as written (tasks.md T103).

### 33A. The pane's behaviour, checked at the gates and once on the seat (2026-10-06)

- **Question** (plan review, Architecture, issue 33): the eval edits about 22 product modules, the pane's live path among them (`chat/server.py`, `agent/runner.py`, the provider adapters' streams, `report/session.py`). What shows the pane's behaviour unchanged but for the evidence fields the plan allows?
- **Options**: A: the gates of slices 1, 2 and 6 re-run the add-in's page and host tests (from the repository root, `dotnet build extractor/SwReview.sln -c Release --nologo -v q` with zero warnings and `dotnet test extractor/SwReview.sln -c Release --no-build --nologo`, Windows) and a replay of a recorded pane session (the committed replay fixtures through the existing replay gate), asserting the pane's behaviour unchanged but for those evidence fields; and one workstation task after slice 6's gate and before any paid run, a smoke review of the small assembly in the Task Pane on the pilot workstation, written into the workstation handover, with no paid study until it passes. B (offered as 33B): land the product seams first as their own slice 0, seat-tested before any eval code (about 30 tasks re-sequenced, the scorer MVP delayed). C (offered as 33C): the plan as it stood: the add-in's tests at slice 2's D12 commit only (RF-28), the replay tests where a slice regenerates the fixtures, and no seat task ("Seat" is never needed).
- **Chosen**: A. Applied by RF-87 (§9.14): tasks.md T041, T072 and T220 carry the two checks; T221 is the seat task.

### 34A. One atomic writer (2026-10-06)

- **Question** (plan review, Code quality, issue 34): RF-74 left the product's three atomic-write copies (`report/attention_record.py:126-145`, `tokens.py:221-226`, `ir/loader.py:177-195`) beside the eval's `write_atomic`, a repetition justified only by a Complexity Tracking row.
- **Options**: A: `reviewer/src/swreview/atomic.py`'s `write_atomic(path, data: bytes)` becomes the only atomic writer: the three copies migrate, each with a failing-first test (an atomic replace, the exact bytes written, the temporary removed on failure), in the tasks right after the one that creates `atomic.py`, lane P; the attention record, written in text mode with no newline argument and so with CRLF on Windows today, is LF after it, a benign change called out; the vocabulary's bytes and the package's (written with `newline="\n"`) do not move. B (offered as 34B): migrate only the two copies whose bytes stay identical (`tokens.py`, `ir/loader.py`), the attention record keeping its text-mode copy with a recorded reason. C (offered as 34C): RF-74 as it stood: the copies kept, with their Complexity Tracking row.
- **Chosen**: A. Applied by RF-88 (§9.14): tasks.md T010 and T011; the Complexity Tracking row removed; RF-31 and RF-74 amended.

### 35A. A paid smoke run before the pilot (2026-10-06)

- **Question** (plan review, Tests, issue 35): the pilot, 141 paid reviews, is the first run against a real provider, so a wiring fault the offline suite cannot see (a field that reads unknown or trivial on real responses) would be found only after the pilot is paid for.
- **Options**: A: quickstart P0 before P1: on a machine with a key, 2 packages (one anchor with a case file, so the answer driver runs, and one core case) at 1 repetition per arm, about 4 real reviews; then every row is read: served model, non-zero usage, a priced cost (no "cost unknown"), run status, the answer log, finding origins and the transcript in the report; any wiring is fixed before P1. And one `-m live` test in `reviewer/tests/live/`, beside the existing live tests and skipped without a key, that drives one real benchmark attempt end to end and asserts the row's fields present and non-trivial: a test task in slice 6, lane B, written test-first and run only on demand, and a paid task for P0 before the paid pilot's. B (offered as 35B): record one real streamed review once and replay it offline through respx in the default suite, which checks real payload shapes on every run but not a real model's question-asking. C (offered as 35C): the plan as it stood: the pilot is the first paid run, and SC-008 is confirmed on it.
- **Chosen**: A. Applied by RF-89 (§9.14): tasks.md T171 (the live test) and T244 (P0); SC-008's paid half names P0.

### 36A. A projected wall clock and bounded concurrency (2026-10-06)

- **Question** (plan review, Performance, issue 36): `run_benchmark` reviews the packages of a set one at a time (`benchmark/runner.py:236`), so a study at the cap, up to 940 paid reviews, takes as long as their sum, and the cost gate's time is an estimate of that sum.
- **Options**: A: (1) the cost gate (`benchmark pilot`'s output and the approval record, slice 8) prints the approved study's projected wall clock from the measured per-review times of P0 and the pilot, at the chosen concurrency; (2) slice 6 adds `benchmark run --jobs N` (default 1): bounded package concurrency within one run, each package keeping its own folder, stream and attempt worker; the run's shared records (`run-status.json` and anything else written per run) written under a lock or per package, so concurrent attempts cannot interleave; N recorded in run provenance, record-only and not must-match, since it does not change what is measured; the retry layer's rate-limit retries still counted; failing-first tests that packages run concurrently up to N and never more, that the shared record stays valid under concurrency, that N is recorded, and that the default 1 behaves exactly as today. B (offered as 36B): the projection alone, runs staying sequential, concurrency added later if the number is too big. C (offered as 36C): the plan as it stood: one package at a time, and the time estimated from the pilot's mean wall clock per package run.
- **Chosen**: A. Applied by RF-90 (`--jobs`) and RF-91 (the projection), §9.14: tasks.md T205 and T206, and T228, T229, T239 and T240; plan risk RK-18 (rate limits).

### 37A. T022's family rows on a family a key can hold (2026-10-07)

- **Question** (implementation, issue 37; tasks.md T022; AK-4): T022's two rows on a `fastener.*` entry, row 1's "`fastener.*` against `fastener` and `fasteners.x`" (US1-2) through `matching.matches` and row 6's "`fastener` never against `fastener.*`" through `matching.entries_overlap`, cannot be built: the test builds its entries with `answer_key_model`, whose AK-4 refuses `fastener.*` on a known defect and on a correct condition, since the family covers the reference-keyed fastener checks. Forbidden entries, the one kind that may hold it, arrive in US3 (T080, T084), and T083's forbidden rows held neither row. How should the rows land?
- **Options**: A: T022 pins the rule on a family a key can hold under the same root, `fastener.head.*`, which covers no reference-keyed check: `fastener.head.*` against `fastener.head` and `fastener.heads.x`, and `fastener.head` never against `fastener.head.*`; the literal `fastener.*` rows join T083's forbidden-entry tests, where such an entry exists. B: build the `fastener.*` entry with `model_construct`, bypassing validation, and keep the literal rows in T022: a shape no loaded key can hold, since a forbidden entry names `checks`, not `check`. C: drop both rows from T022 and test them as written with forbidden entries in US3.
- **Chosen**: A: the rule is pinned in slice 1 on data a key can hold, and the contract's own example is pinned once it can be reached. Applied in tasks.md T022 (rows 1 and 6) and T083.

### 38A. The event reader's line ends, as at `be01974` (2026-10-07)

- **Question** (implementation, issue 38; tasks.md T012, T013; §9.14's implementation follow-ups): the follow-up that made a line cut inside a multibyte UTF-8 character an unreadable line read the stream as bytes, so a lone carriage return no longer ended a line, as text mode's universal newlines had at `be01974`. `EventSink` never writes one (JSON escapes control characters), so the difference is very unlikely to be seen. Keep the byte splitting or restore the old?
- **Options**: A: restore universal-newline splitting exactly, a cut character still stopping the replay at its own line, with a lone-carriage-return test for the plain and the packed stream; it keeps T012's "behaves as at `be01974`". B: keep the byte splitting and note the difference in one sentence here.
- **Chosen**: A. `agent/events.py`'s `_open_stream` opens the stream in text mode again, as T013 did, with `errors="surrogateescape"`, so a byte that is not UTF-8 reads as a lone surrogate and a line holding one is the unreadable line; `test_event_lines.py` pins a lone carriage return ending a line, and counted in the line's number, in both forms (§9.14, implementation follow-ups).

### 39A. The unknown-role golden stays at schema 1.3.0 (2026-10-07)

- **Question** (implementation, issue 39; tasks.md T024, T027; RF-2): T024's `joint-unknown-role` golden was to hold a tolerance source the joint map's resolver reads, so that `hole.position_stack` returns its unknown-role result too, inside the golden corpus's schemas 1.0.0 to 1.3.0 (`PRE_1_4_0_SCHEMA_VERSIONS`, `tests/unit/test_ir_golden_fixtures_load.py`, which gates every golden not named `standards-*`). Every source `ResolverLookup.holds_any_source` reads is a 1.5.0 member (a model dimension, a model annotation, a Hole Wizard class) or a 1.4.0 drawing record, and `run_joint_checks` runs the stack only when a source exists, so no package the gate admits can carry one. How should the golden land?
- **Options**: A: the package stays at 1.3.0 inside the gate, which is not widened; the golden pins `hole.nominal_alignment`'s unknown-role result naming every instance of the joint and the stack check's "no tolerance source" skipped coverage row, as `alignment_case` returns them, and `hole.position_stack`'s unknown-role result is pinned by T024's unit test in `test_joint_alignment.py` only. B: declare the package 1.5.0 and exempt it from the gate by name, as `standards-*` is by prefix, so it can carry a tolerance source. C: other.
- **Chosen**: A. The gate stays the one rule it is, and no 1.5.0 member is written into a package that declares 1.3.0; the stack's unknown-role path is the same `_unknown_role` the nominal check's golden pins, and its unit test pins it word for word. Applied in tasks.md T024 and T027.

### 40A. `check_interference_group` cites its own step (2026-10-07)

- **Question** (implementation, issue 40; tasks.md T032, T033; `scoring.md` 5.3; SP-3): `check_interference_group` records its findings with no step id (`tools/checks_interference.py:120`), although its contacts cite their step (`:161`), so by `scoring.md` 5.3's rule 5 a pre-run interference finding would read `configured_check`, model origin, and count in the deciding false-alarm rule. How to fix?
- **Options**: A: a product fix in its own commit: `check_interference_group` passes its step id, as its contacts do; the contracts' list of the tools whose findings cite no step amended; the big-assembly replay fixture regenerated, slice 1's replay gate allowing the small token change; landing before T032 and T033. B: patch the origin rule, with no product change: a step-less interference finding reads code origin, which cannot tell a pre-run finding from a model-called one. C: exempt and accept: pre-run interference findings read as model origin in the pane study.
- **Chosen**: A. `check_interference_group` passes `tool_result_ids=[context.current_step_id]` on its findings, as on its contacts, test first (`test_tools_checks_interference.py`: every finding the tool writes cites the step in flight, through the registry too, and a pre-run interference finding and a model-called one each cite their own step); SP-3 and `scoring.md` 5.1 name every finding writer that cites no step; the big-assembly replay fixture is regenerated with 008 `contracts/replay.md` section 8's command, then the pane fixture. The measured consequence is in §9.14's implementation follow-ups and in 008 `contracts/replay.md` section 9's row "Feature 014 owner decision 40A"; tasks.md T041 compares against that row.

- **Question** (implementation, issue 41; tasks.md T032's test (5)): test (5) wants a `code_only_call` finding the checks-first review cannot produce: the pre-run guard answers a repeated `check_hygiene`, and hygiene finds nothing there. How should it be tested?
- **Options**: A: the off arm supplies it: the checks-first review asserts `prerun` and `configured_check` and that the guarded `check_hygiene` repeat adds nothing; the end-to-end `code_only_call` comes from the same package's off arm, where the model's scripted `check_rms_part` and `check_mass_material` write findings that read `code_only_call`; T032's text amended. B: a new hygiene-defect variant: keep the wording, and add a variant package with a hygiene defect so that an off-arm `check_hygiene` writes a finding. C: data-built sessions only: drop `code_only_call` from test (5), which tests (1) and (2) cover.

In test (5) the checks-first review's findings read `prerun` and `configured_check` (the scripted `check_hole_alignment` on `hole:1` and `hole:2`), and the model's scripted `check_hygiene` repeat is answered `already_run` by the pre-run guard and adds no finding; the end-to-end `code_only_call` findings come from the same package's off arm (every lever off), where the model's scripted `check_rms_part` and `check_mass_material` write them. Applied in tasks.md T032's test (5), whose text is amended with T032.

B: exempt the field by name from the must-match loop, so one arm may carry it and the decision row is still refused for it.

### 55A. `coaxiality_undrawn`'s twin carries no correct call (2026-10-08)

Keep the nominal as written, or record the anchor's text as normalised?

### 64A. A case and its twin lay the same document ids (2026-10-09)

- **Question** (implementation, issue 64; tasks.md T132, T135, T138, T146, T147, T151; `generator.md` 4): a planted screw of another thread or length has a document of its own, laid with the screws, before the ingested drawings, so every drawing of the case takes an id one higher than its twin's. In `kalo-1`, `tessa-1` and `pelin-1` the fit's references then name other drawing documents in the case's key than in its twin's, and the twin tests of T146 (5), T147 (the set summary's twin column) and T151 fail as written. Lay the ids alike, or compare a twin with its case otherwise?
- **Options**: A: a case and its twin lay the same document ids: the design modules of `kalo`, `tessa` and `pelin` lay the planted screw document after the drawings (or otherwise keep every shared document id equal), with a regression test that every committed twin carries its case's document ids, so the twin tests hold as written. B: leave the ids apart and compare a twin with its case by what its documents are rather than by their ids.
- **Chosen**: A. Applied in `spec.set_aside` and `spec.laid_after`: a design sets a planted screw's document aside while it lays its drawings, already renumbered as the document after them, its manifest entry, instances and gaps re-pointed, and appends it after them; a document not among the package's last, or one another row names, is refused naming it, and one that does not follow the drawings when appended. `kalo` now lays its base screw document first, as the other designs lay theirs. The regression test, `test_every_committed_twin_carries_its_case_s_document_ids` in `test_benchmark_case_generator.py` (every document a twin lays, its case lays under the same id, and each twin condition carries its defect's documents, parts and references, as `generator.md` 4 has it), found the same shift in `kalo-2`, `okta-2`, `rusk-1`, `lori-1` and `venta-1`, each a drawing defect whose `document_ids` named a document one higher than its twin condition's; it is fixed the same way in `okta`, `rusk`, `lori` and `venta`, the rule being 64A's and the list of three the modules then known. Tests `test_set_aside_lays_a_case_s_own_document_after_the_drawings`, `test_set_aside_with_nothing_to_set_aside_changes_nothing`, `test_set_aside_refuses_a_document_that_is_not_among_the_package_s_last`, `test_set_aside_refuses_a_document_another_row_names` and `test_laid_after_refuses_documents_that_do_not_follow_the_drawings`; tasks.md T132, T135, T136, T138, T140, T141, T142 and T146.

### 66A. The denylist scan of the generated cases, and the words it found (2026-10-09)

- **Question** (implementation, issue 66; tasks.md T150; `generator.md` 8): T150's denylist half read every string value of every generated file, schema values and check ids among them, matched as names by substring, and gap reasons, the extractor's sentences, by substring too; beyond those, three words of the generated prose matched. Narrow the reading, exempt the words, or reword them?
- **Options**: A: the scan leaves schema values and check ids out (the `source`, `kind`, `hole_type`, `check` and `item` fields, as feature 010's scan does) and reads gap reasons as sentences, by whole word; the three remaining words are reworded in `seeds.py` and the design files, never exempted and never by weakening the scan; no denylist token is printed, logged or committed, and the list's path is written nowhere. B: exempt the words that match.
- **Chosen**: A. The prose is reworded in `seeds.py` (the fit kinds' forms) and in the designs `kalo` (`kalo-empty`'s question rule), `cover` (`C-1`), `brun`, `davo` and `lori` (the assembly sheets' notes) and `rusk` (the summaries of one part and its drawing); the scan is `test_benchmark_cases_are_fictional.py` (T150), which lands with the committed set and names the file, never a token, when it finds one; tasks.md T150.

### 65A. Test 5's second half on a key holding no correct condition (2026-10-09)

- **Question** (implementation, issue 65; tasks.md T149; `scoring.md` 7; `answer-key.md` 9 test 5): `test_committed_answer_keys.py` test 5's second half held every key with no extension list to the seven columns the key-extensions slice leaves unassessed, `NOT_ASSESSED_WITHOUT_EXTENSIONS`; the generated core cases' keys hold no correct condition either, so their two condition columns read `null` too, with "the key holds no correct condition", which `assert_oracle_and_null` already expects and the second half did not. Keep those keys in the second half, or read it only on keys holding a condition?
- **Options**: A: keep every key with no extension list, and expect the two condition columns as not assessed, with their reason, when the key holds no correct condition. B: read the second half only on keys holding a correct condition.
- **Chosen**: A. Applied in `test_committed_answer_keys.py`'s `test_sc_001_a_key_with_no_extension_list_reads_the_new_columns_not_assessed`, its expectation `NOT_ASSESSED_WITHOUT_EXTENSIONS` plus `scorecard.NO_CORRECT_CONDITION` on `scoring_probes.COLUMNS_BY_KEY_LIST["correct_conditions"]` when the key holds none; tasks.md T149.

### 67A. The committed pilot set is keyed (2026-10-09)

- **Question** (implementation, issue 67; tasks.md T115, T151; `cases.md` 4.1 and 6.1): T115's acceptance test, `test_benchmark_sets.py`'s `test_the_committed_pilot_set_loads_as_a_keyless_set`, read the hand-written `pilot.json`, which named no key; the generator writes `pilot.json` with the committed set, every entry naming its key (`generator.md` 7), so the test fails on the committed set as the contract has it. Move the test, or keep `pilot.json` keyless?
- **Options**: A: the test becomes one that `pilot.json` is keyed and each entry's key is `benchmarks/answer_keys/<package_id>.json`, renamed to say so. B: write `pilot.json` without keys.
- **Chosen**: A. Applied in `test_benchmark_sets.py`'s `test_the_committed_pilot_set_is_keyed_each_entry_s_key_its_package_s_answer_key` (each entry's key the package's answer key, read by `key_path` with no directory given); tasks.md T115 and T151.

### 81A. A module-level sentence may name the stream files (2026-10-14)

The number follows feature 015's 68A to 80A, since the owner's decisions are numbered across features.

- **Question** (implementation, issue 1 of slice 5; tasks.md T159 and T160; `scoring.md` 11 item 4; RF-29, RF-83): T159's structural half holds every reference to `EVENTS_FILE_NAME` or `PACKED_EVENTS_FILE_NAME` under `benchmark/` to an import or a function that calls `replay_events` or `event_stream`, with no module exempt in this slice. `benchmark/scorecard.py`'s `NO_EVENT_STREAM` (`:138-141`), the key-extensions slice's `not_assessed` reason for a package with no stream, names both files in a module-level sentence and reads nothing; as worded, the test could never pass after T160, though `scoring.md` 11 asks only that no other *reader* exist. Allow it, narrow the rule, or reword the sentence?
- **Options**: A: keep the rule and the empty module exemption as written, and add one named allowance, `("scorecard.py", "NO_EVENT_STREAM")`: a module-level sentence that may name the files, the test failing if that constant ever calls anything or is used as a path. B: narrow the rule for every module: a reference that only formats a string is no read. C: write the file names into the sentence literally (restating two constants, and editing lane S's file beyond T168). D: exempt the whole of `scorecard.py`.
- **Chosen**: A. Applied in `test_event_lines.py`'s `NAMED_SENTENCES` and `test_every_named_sentence_is_a_sentence_that_reads_nothing` (one module-level assignment whose value calls nothing, its name never an operand of `/` nor an argument of a call that opens, resolves or reads a path), beside `test_no_module_under_benchmark_reads_a_stream_but_through_the_one_reader`; tasks.md T159.

## §2 The 2026-09-28 audit

The audit's severity scale: *misleading*, a flaw that can make a conclusion point the wrong way; *noise*, a flaw that adds variance or limits how far a result carries over, without flipping it; *info*, a fact the eval designer needs; *fine*, sound as it is. Each entry below gives the finding's id, the auditor's rating then the verifier's, what it found, its evidence at file and line, and the verifier's verdict. Entries are grouped by the verifier's rating.

What the audit established, in one list: the only benchmarked package scores 0 of 2 for any reviewer, including none; the scorer credits sibling rules; a finding that contradicts a correct condition is never a false alarm; every `rms-part` defect is produced by argument-free code; `benchmark run` cannot run what the pane ships; a cut-short or provider-stopped review is scored like a finished one; nothing measures recall finely enough to see a 10-point change in the model.

### Misleading after verification (the findings that drove the feature)

- **cbt-defects-unreachable** (cases; misleading, confirmed misleading). Neither known defect of cover-blind-tap can be matched; the key's own expected behaviour scores 0 of 2 plus a false alarm. Evidence: `benchmarks/answer_keys/cover-blind-tap.json:8` (D-1 expects unresolved); `benchmark/scorecard.py:261-263` (unresolved findings are counted and skipped); `benchmarks/packages/cover-blind-tap/package.json` (0 holes, 0 fasteners); `tools/checks_fastener.py:219-226` (unknown id without a fastener row and a hole row); `tools/session.py:512-527` (the drawing finding carries no component ids); `benchmark/scorecard.py:138` (component sets must intersect). Verdict: worse than stated. The joint map cannot place a joint without holes and writes skipped coverage (`tools/checks_mechanical.py:141-147, 171-172`), so even the prefix route is closed. Rebuilding from the golden variant is not enough: its holes carry a null usable thread depth, so D-1 would stay unresolved and uncredited.
- **cover-blind-tap-unscoreable** (grader; misleading, confirmed). The same from the grader's side. D-2 names `cmp:0001` (`cover-blind-tap.json:10-15`), while its only writer binds the drawing through `drawing_locations` (`findings.py:53`; the other `drawing.*` ids are coverage only, `checks/drawing_context.py:119-138`). Six recorded scorecards each show 0 matched, 2 missed, 9 to 12 false alarms. No gate decision is computed today (the pilot set holds nothing out: `cli.py:2226-2245`, `benchmark/adoption.py:258-270`), but once another package joins, a correct drawing finding in the on arm reads as NEW FALSE ALARM (`adoption.py:297-307`).
- **d2-model-finding-unmatchable** (mechanism; misleading, confirmed). The only model-judgement defect in either key can never match, and the correct finding counts as a false alarm, so a better model scores worse. Reproduced with the real tool on the committed package (`tools/session.py:511-527`, `findings.py:372`, `benchmark/scorecard.py:138-139`; `tools/context.py:250-259`). No scoring test builds a finding the way the tool does (`reviewer/tests/unit/test_scorecard.py:131-135`).
- **d1-unreachable** (mechanism; misleading, confirmed). D-1 is unreachable; the recall ceiling on the only set is 0 of 2. Worse than stated: the tool context indexes only the package's holes and fasteners, both empty (`tools/context.py:187-193`), and even with fasteners bottoming stays unresolved while thread depth is unknown, so the only route to a match is the over-claim the key forbids.
- **null-beats-code-only** (mechanism; misleading, confirmed but not independent). A null run (`review --provider fake`) scores 0 valid, 2 missed, 0 false alarms; a code-only run (`--pane-defaults`) adds 9 `rms.*` false alarms. A plain model-versus-model `benchmark run` declares no study and is never refused (`cli.py:2287-2289`).
- **prefix-sibling-credit** (grader; misleading, confirmed). Prefix matching applies to every key entry, so a sibling rule on the same part credits a defect it did not find (`benchmark/scorecard.py:132-137`, no `*` test; `:22-25`, the docstring's intent; `benchmarks/answer_keys/rms-part.json:34-51, 70-87`). One `rms.quarantine.chamfers_before_fillets` on `cmp:0002` matched D-6, D-7 and D-8; `fastener.thread_match` matched cover-blind-tap's D-1. 8 of the 20 rms-part defects can be credited without their own rule firing, and D-15 without the suppress-test evidence it needs (`rms-part.json:92`). Narrowed because the model cannot choose check ids and one RMS call grades every rule of a part (`tools/rms_checks.py:111-137`), but rule 1 (`adoption.py:229-240`) can still hide the loss of a single rule.
- **correct-conditions-excuse-false-alarms** (grader; misleading, confirmed). A finding that contradicts a correct condition is excused, or credited through a shared prefix, never a false alarm (`benchmark/scorecard.py:267-277`, `:31-35`; pinned at `reviewer/tests/unit/test_scorecard.py:216-217`). `rms.refs.direction` on `cmp:0002` credits D-9 and `rms.refs.quarantine_has_no_children` on `cmp:0003` credits D-20; the other 16 conditions drop silently, so rule 2 (`adoption.py:286-307`) under-counts. Its cases-side twin, correct-conditions-shield-false-alarms, was re-rated noise: no real fit finding can match cover-blind-tap's only condition (`tools/checks_fit.py:67`).
- **recommended-set-saturated-by-code** (cases; misleading, confirmed). The contract's recommended minimum set (build rms-part, hold out one package: `specs/005-llm-efficiency/contracts/ab-harness.md:356-360`) would be 20 code-produced defects of 22 (91%), found before the model's first turn under pane settings (`agent/settings.py:502-521`; `prerun.py:24-26, 145-155`). A lever that damages fastener, fit or drawing reasoning loses no matched defect and reads "no quality loss".
- **p3-min-count-not-enforced** (cases; misleading, confirmed). The P-3 refusal checks only that some package is held out (`cli.py:2239`), while the contract refuses fewer than a configured minimum (`ab-harness.md:115-120`). Worse than stated: with cover-blind-tap held out, recall computes as 0.0 rather than none, passes the NO_RECALL refusal (`adoption.py:264-269`), and ADOPT becomes possible with no quality resolution.
- **noise-floor** (mechanism; misleading, confirmed). Recall cannot see a 10-point change in the model even with rms-part (`benchmarks/sets/pilot.json:4`; `benchmark/scorecard.py:431-435`; `benchmark/adoption.py:76`, three repetitions). n·R = 6 gives ±41 points; 66 gives ±12; 42 (the independent match classes) ±15; the one model-dependent defect ±58. A ±10-point half-width needs n·R ≥ 100, 34 defects at 3 repetitions: the source of 10A. The unpaired 80%-power figure near p = 0.5 is about 392 trials per arm. Correction: the handover's 27 dumps resolve to 9 distinct designs.
- **fa-rate-rewards-ignoring-instructions** (mechanism; misleading, confirmed). With checks-first off, a model that follows the checklist (`agent/checklist_v1.yaml:57-58`) and calls the RMS tools on cover-blind-tap brings in 9 code-authored false alarms; one that skips them brings none. The package has no feature tree and no gap marking it unread, so the tools grade an empty tree; the one guard (`tools/context.py:91-128`) is consulted only under lever 4 (`tools/registry.py:721-723`).
- **gemini-service-stop-silent** (harness; misleading, confirmed). A Gemini SAFETY, RECITATION or OTHER stop ends the package quietly and scores like a finished review, while the OpenAI equivalent raises and aborts the set (`agent/providers/gemini_provider.py:127-145`, `:470-481, 578-589`; `agent/providers/openai_provider.py:638-654`; `report/session.py:323-331`, no closeout for an `error` end). The blocked-turn error's docstring claims the runner records unresolved coverage (`gemini_provider.py:185-188`); the code does not. Latent: every recorded study provenance is OpenAI.
- **unresolved-answer-scored-as-miss** (harness; misleading, confirmed). D-1's expected answer (unresolved, with an evidence request) cannot score, and the single-turn scaffold never answers the request (`agent/runner.py:1842-1848`); a lever that moves the model from suspected to unresolved reads as DEFECT LOST (`adoption.py:229-240, 291-297`).
- **benchmark-cannot-express-production-config** (metrics; misleading, confirmed), with **parity-efficiency-levers** (merged into it), **parity-model-view** and **parity-standards-profile** (each confirmed). `benchmark run` has no pane-defaults, model-view or profile option (`cli.py:2248-2285`) and `_review_fn` forwards none (`:2192-2223`), while the pane passes all of them and `explain_findings=True` (`chat/server.py:2119-2138`); the baseline arm forbids any lever (`agent/settings.py:756-766`; `benchmark/compare.py:380-387`). The levers can ride a named study's arms, but the model view and the profile cannot be expressed, and a study-less "pane control" run is refused by compare. The model view is recorded per session (`agent/runner.py:1621`), not in provenance. Without a profile, part roles are absent, no standards tool is offered and no part-roles question is asked (`agent/runner.py:470-487, 1610-1612, 1682-1686`), which moves the false-alarm count. A lever verdict reversing between the two baselines is plausible, not demonstrated.
- **no-cost-cache-blind-gate** (metrics; misleading, confirmed). No cost is computed, and the adoption gate reads total tokens, cached and uncached alike (`adoption.py:329-352`; `agent/providers/__init__.py:162-171, 233`). Overstated in parts (history pruning is a model-view setting; the gate is framed in tokens), but levers that cut round trips mostly remove cached prefix tokens, so the token reduction overstates the cost reduction and can cross the 20% threshold.

### Noise after verification

- **correct-conditions-shield-false-alarms** (cases; misleading, re-rated noise): see correct-conditions-excuse-false-alarms above.
- **prefix-match-collapses-rms-key** (cases; misleading, re-rated noise) and **prefix-match-collapse** (mechanism; noise, confirmed). The 20 rms-part defects fold into 12 (prefix, component) buckets, and 11 synthetic findings score 20 of 20 (`benchmark/scorecard.py:122-124, 268-271`). Noise because the model cannot author `rms.*` findings and one call grades a part's rules together (`tools/rms_checks.py:80-108`). Exact matching needs cover-blind-tap's D-1 rewritten as `fastener.*`: its notes treat bottoming and engagement as one outcome.
- **rms-key-measures-code** (mechanism; misleading, re-rated noise). All 20 rms-part defects come from argument-free code checks (`tools/rms_checks.py:80, 242`; `prerun.py:144-152`; D-15 needs the package's suppress-test run, `checks/rms/part.py:1090-1092`): the right regression guard for efficiency levers, no resolution for model studies. The basis of FR-006 and 16A.
- **no-run-status-in-scorer** (harness; misleading, re-rated noise). The per-package score has no run status (`benchmark/scorecard.py:143-208`). Noise because a raised provider error leaves no provenance and gates nothing (`benchmark/runner.py:236-255`; `cli.py:2303-2333`), while step-limit and truncated turns write an unresolved closeout item (`agent/runner.py:1451-1453`). The verifier's point that those budget outcomes belong to the configuration is the basis of D2.
- **abort-on-first-error-no-resume**, **rerun-appends-events**, **retries-unrecorded-asymmetric**, **no-wall-clock-ceiling**, **served-model-from-config** and **served-model-not-recorded** (noise, confirmed; the basis of User Story 7). A provider error stops the set: no per-package guard, error record or resume (`benchmark/runner.py:236-255`; `cli.py:260-269`). A re-run into one folder appends a second session with duplicate sequence numbers, and time to first finding then times the older one (`agent/events.py:99, 108, 115-117`; `benchmark/scorecard.py:345-346`). OpenAI's SDK retries twice with jitter, Gemini gets one attempt, and nothing records attempts (`agent/providers/openai_provider.py:290`; `agent/settings.py:253-263`). Elapsed time is never bounded (`benchmark/runner.py:242-252`), and a manual kill leaves nothing to score (`agent/runner.py:1434`). Usage records the requested model id, and compare checks two copies of configuration (`openai_provider.py:456-465`; `gemini_provider.py:458-466`; `compare.py:356-367`).
- **benchmark-config-not-pane-config** (harness) and **levers-change-model-view** (mechanism; both noise, confirmed). The parity facts from the harness's side, where the all-off baseline is deliberate (`agent/settings.py:511-514`); the model view is recorded per session but not in the run's provenance; `gemini_explicit_cache` is read nowhere (`:439`).
- **adoption-rules-vs-stochastic-defects** (mechanism; noise, confirmed). For a defect found with p = 0.8, three off-runs disagree with probability 0.48, and at least one of three on-runs misses a defect some off-run found with probability 0.49 (`adoption.py:214-240, 76`). More repetitions make CONTROL UNSTABLE more likely (0.8^R + 0.2^R: 0.52 at 3, 0.33 at 5). It fails safe, never a false ADOPT. The basis of D1.
- **no-oracle-null-tests** and **metric-scope-and-dupes** (grader; noise, confirmed). Every scoring test builds a synthetic key; the one real-key test checks content and is skipped without the native package (`reviewer/tests/conftest.py:70-72`); the CLI fixture's drawing defect has empty component ids (`reviewer/tests/unit/test_cli.py:2396-2417`). Recall is over held-out packages and the false-alarm rate over all (`benchmark/scorecard.py:431-440`); the matching loop is written twice (`:267-271`, `:303-307`); a repeated RMS call doubles valid findings, which can pile up in an off arm, since the pre-run guard answers repeats only with checks-first on (`prerun.py:1446-1449`).
- **d1-gold-ambiguous** (cases; noise, confirmed). If the 14 in the callout "4X M6x1.0 - 6H ↧ 14" is the drill depth, usable thread is at most 14 mm, less than the 18.5 mm protrusion, and D-1 is demonstrated; if it is the thread depth, D-2's premise (thread depth missing) is false. The product's rule is a house policy, not ground truth (`benchmarks/packages/cover-blind-tap/notes.md:14-17`; `reviewer/scripts/make_cover_blind_tap_pdfs.py:7-10`; `tools/checks_fastener.py:213-214`). An input to D8: the regenerated case seeds the callout without the ambiguity.
- **distribution-shape-mismatch**, **parity-package-shape**, **parity-bridge-tools**, **parity-explanations-default-off** (noise, confirmed). The only package is a schema 1.0.0 PDF-ingest package with no features, mates, holes or drawing records, while 26 of 27 production dumps (schema 1.0 to 1.6) carry feature trees. The three live-bridge tools are offered only with a bridge (`tools/registry.py:149-158, 934-935`). The pane runs the explanation pass and `benchmark run` defaults it off (`chat/server.py:2138`; `cli.py:2258-2265`); its usage carries no purpose tag (`agent/runner.py:1335-1338`).
- **cache-share-not-compared**, **latency-boundaries**, **per-call-breakdown-not-surfaced** (noise, confirmed). Cache share renders "unknown" while its containment is unverified (`compare.py:813-817`); per-round latency includes SDK retries (`openai_provider.py:434-451`); tool time is recorded per step but summed nowhere.

### Info after verification

- **prompt-encodes-cbt-answer** (cases; noise, re-rated info). The prompt, checklist and tool docstrings state the product's unknown-thread-depth policy (`agent/prompts/system_v1.md:11-14, 76`; `agent/checklist_v1.yaml:37-39`). It is a policy, not a paraphrase of the key, but cover-blind-tap cannot count as held out: the basis of tagging every one of its items tuned-on (FR-010).
- **part-level-granularity** (grader; noise, re-rated info). RMS findings match at part level (`checks/rules/report.py:224-228, 312`); nothing checks key ids against the package: the basis of FR-009's id resolution.
- **failed-round-usage-dropped** (metrics; noise, re-rated info). A round that fails mid-stream records no usage (`openai_provider.py:597-598`; `gemini_provider.py:541-565`).
- **crash-scored-as-negative** (grader; misleading, **refuted**, re-rated info). A raised provider error exits `benchmark run` before the saved set and provenance are written, so the run gates nothing (`benchmark/runner.py:236-255`; `cli.py:2303-2333`; `compare.py:39-41, 476-480`), and step-limit and truncated turns are the arm's own budget outcomes, pinned within a study (`compare.py:494-503`). The refutation's claim that the Gemini blocked-turn path raises is wrong (gemini-service-stop-silent; F17): it reports and returns ("Report - never raise", `agent/providers/gemini_provider.py:578`), so a Gemini provider stop does score as a finished review today.

### Raised but not re-checked

Rated fine or info by the auditors and not sent for verification. Those this feature relies on:

- **answer-key-isolation-sound**, **ground-truth-isolation-sound**, **ground-truth-provenance-human** (fine): the keys are human-seeded and out of the reviewer's reach by construction (`ir/loader.py:46-62`, called from `benchmark/sets.py:52` and `benchmark/runner.py:237-239`); no tool takes a free file path. Residual: `notes.md` sits in the package folder though no tool reads it.
- **source-golden-fixtures**, **source-mechanical-drawings-sitting**, **source-replay-fixtures**, **source-perf-generator**, **source-handover**, **source-attention-and-runs**, **programmatic-seeded-generation** (info): the inventory of case sources behind 5A and §7. The handover's 27 dumps cover a handful of designs, several near-duplicates; recorded sessions are model output, never gold.
- **rms-part-key-without-package**, **golden-rms-part-not-the-recipe**, **set-size-and-held-out**, **headline-null-and-code-measured** (info): the rms-part key has no package, and the golden fixture of that name is another design with other ids; the pilot set is one package, two defects, nothing held out, recall null.
- **source-not-split-in-scorer** (info) and **source-label-and-prerun-plumbing-fine** (fine): findings carry `source` (`findings.py:146`) and pre-run findings cite their steps, but no step carries a pre-run mark (`report/session.py:55`): the basis of D9.
- **tool-errors-malformed-maxsteps**, **isolation-and-determinism**, **reps-supported**, **usage-from-api**, **wall-clock-two-writers**, **parity-shared-entry**, **key-resolution** (fine): the runner records turn outcomes, isolates packages, supports repetitions, takes token counts from the providers' usage blocks, and calls the product's own entry point (`agent/runner.py:1497`).
- **trajectories-mostly-saved** (fine): all is on disk but the request as sent, the system prompt and reasoning items: the basis of D11. **parity-single-turn** (info): one opening turn is graded: the basis of 9A. **replay-gate-freezes-model**, **pane-defaults-adopted-on-estimates** (info): the pane defaults were adopted on replay estimates blind to model behaviour: the basis of 3A.
- **production-defaults** (info): the pilot reviews ran another OpenAI model id than the code's default, so a benchmark meant to represent them passes `--model`.
- **null-baseline-ties-real-runs**, **exceptions-json-unrecorded**, **context-overflow-unclassified**, **aggregate-omits-gemini-tool-tokens**, **opening-message-neutral** (info or fine).

### Corrections to the audit's summary (F20)

- "All 20 rms-part defects are produced by code that runs before the model's first turn" holds only with checks-first on. In the all-off arm the model calls the three argument-free RMS tools itself, and D-15 also needs a suppress-test run in the package.
- "A failed or cut-short review is scored like a finished one" is imprecise. A raised provider error stops the whole set, and `benchmark score` then fails on the first missing session. What is scored as finished is a cut-short turn or a Gemini provider stop.

---

## §3 Anchor cases

R-1 to R-8 were drafted read-only from the debriefs, the handover files of the two reviews, and the committed pane-findings documents. Handover files are cited by basename only. Under 15A **every anchor behaviour is tuned-on**: it is graded as a regression guard and never counted in held-out recall. The producer column follows 16A: it is decided per defect in its own package, so a seed that withholds evidence can move a defect from code to model, and the generator records what it withheld. Every seed below uses the builders of §7 plus the one builder of ingested drawing-sheet dimensions FR-027 adds, and is built and keyed against the repository's fictional example profile (`config/standards.example.yaml`), whose names replace the profile A names the existing builders write (FR-027; §8.14). The validation (D8, FR-054) checks each seed's producer and reachability. Joint, alignment, fit and stack entries also name their references (the fastener and hole, both holes, or the dimensions), so a seed may place sibling joints, hole pairs and fits beside a planted one (FR-003; §8.12; round 3, RR3-6).

### R-1: a bought vendor pin graded for modelling practice and hygiene

- **Kind**: false alarm. Family: part role (bought part).
- **What happened**: on the small assembly (2026-09-26 sitting), pre-run code graded a downloaded vendor dowel pin (two instances) for modelling practice and hygiene: four RMS findings (`rms.sketches.one_sketch_per_feature`, `rms.folders.present`, `rms.grouping.all_features_in_a_group`, `rms.params.global_variables_present`) and one `hygiene.revision_present`. The attention policy put the pin's "one sketch feeds four features" row first in Start here, above the custom plate's under-defined sketch.
- **Verdict and source**: rejected by the engineer: a bought or vendor part's modelling does not matter; ignore it for modelling practice, and ask when unclear (`findings-2026-09-26.md`, F1). Confirmed on 2026-09-28: the engineer answered the code-written part-roles question "All bought" for both pins, the pins' modelling and hygiene findings were withdrawn, and the rest of the tool seemed to work (`findings-2026-09-28.md`, ER-001 row; `docs/testing-feedback-2026-09-28.md:24-27`; `specs/013-engineer-first-review/spec.md:11, 19`).
- **Producer**: pre-run code (the RMS and hygiene checks); the part-roles question is asked by code (`checks/part_roles.py:881-900`).
- **Tuned-on**: yes.
- **Graded as**: forbidden `rms.*` and `hygiene.*` findings on the pin in the final state; a withdrawn forbidden hit in its own column (D14.4), because the complaint was about what was shown first; a question-count rule of exactly one part-roles question (spec FR-060); the scripted answer "All bought"; the plate's own findings survive the regrade (R-7).
- **Seed**: built as `tests/support/sitting.py` `sitting_package()` is, on `features.rms_package` with `AssemblySpec`, `PartSpec`, `InstanceSpec` and `MateSpec`, but in the example profile's names rather than profile A's (`sitting.py:8-9, 188-198`; §8.14). Give the pin's `PartSpec` two instances and a tree with `sketch_feature('Sketch1', consumers=...)` feeding four features, no `folder(...)` and no `equation(...)`. Place it in a folder the example profile's `bought_folder_names` names (`config/standards.example.yaml:136-137`) by `model_copy` of path, custom properties and configuration properties, as `sitting_package` does. Add a value to one of its `vendor_properties` (`:148-150`) and a file and configuration name of its catalogue shape (`:163-164`); for the hard variant, also a populated hygiene part-number property (`:100`) starting with a custom prefix (`:169-171`). The custom plate follows the fictional convention and has one `sketch_feature(raw_status=2)`. Clean twin: rename the pin to the custom convention, move it to the job folder and remove the vendor signals; its RMS findings are then real.
- **Hard because**: the findings are true of the geometry; only the part's role makes them irrelevant. The signals conflict: library folder, vendor property and catalogue configuration name say bought, while a house-shaped part number and a full data card say custom. Two instances double its reach, so ranking promotes it above the custom part.

### R-2: drawing advice as if a drawing had been read, and a request for a bought part's drawing

- **Kind**: false alarm. Family: drawing.
- **What happened**: same review. Code found a same-name drawing beside the assembly and the custom plate, but it was not open. The engineer confirmed a read-only open, which the seat refused because its switch was off, and the model never learned of the refusal. The model then asked for native drawings of the assembly, the plate and the vendor pin, and opened a second request for the same drawing. In a follow-up it gave five numbered "drawing feedback" items written as a drawing review, although no drawing had been read.
- **Verdict and source**: rejected by the engineer (`findings-2026-09-26.md`, F4 and F5; `specs/013-engineer-first-review/spec.md:11, 78, 97`). A vendor pin's drawing can never be supplied; drawings exist only for custom parts, in the same folder with the same name.
