Four of the five items restated that no drawing was extracted and the fifth was the refused version question; advice like that is worse than silence.
- **Producer**: model (the requests and the prose).
- **Tuned-on**: yes.
- **Graded as**: forbidden `drawing.*` findings (nothing was read); an unanswerable request for the pin's drawing; a repeat of an open question (the second request, refused by the product as already asked: spec FR-020); expected coverage on `drawing.manufacturing_inputs`: unresolved, which is the row code writes while candidates sit unattached (`checks/drawing_context.py:446-472`). A request naming the drawing item is closed by code since feature 013 and survives only as a refused call (D4); the repeat the key counts is of a request naming no item, refused as already asked (`tools/session.py:112-120`; §8.3). The prose is deferred (8A).
- **Seed**: `sitting_package()`, or a `mechanical.PackageBuilder` small assembly whose root and plate share one stem, wrapped in `drawings.DrawingBuilder(base)` with only `.candidate(root_id)` and `.candidate(plate_id)`: no `drawing_document`, drawing, sheet or view records, so nothing is readable, and no candidate for the pin. Script the engineer's "yes, open it"; offline, a confirmed read records no connection (`tools/drawings.py:241, 331`). Then a follow-up asking what the drawing should improve. Positive twin: `DrawingBuilder.drawing_document` plus drawing, sheet, view, a dimension on the pin hole with no tolerance, and `revision_table`; a finding about the untoleranced fit dimension is then legitimate.
- **Hard because**: the drawing exists, code named it and the engineer said yes, so the model assumes it has drawing context. The refusal reached coverage, not the model. Generic drawing advice is partly true in general; it must be scored against what was read, which was nothing. Asking for a bought part's drawing looks diligent but can never be answered.

### R-3: a line-to-line dowel passed as a floating fastener, then re-asked after "press fit"

- **Kind**: real condition missed; the design itself has no defect here.
- **What happened**: in both small-assembly reviews (2026-09-26 and 2026-09-28) the dowel pins sit line-to-line: a 3.0 mm pin face in a 3.0 mm hole whose Hole Wizard type is "clearance". Live interference found nothing. The joint check recorded a pass: axes 0.0 mm apart, within the clearance the joint allows as a floating fastener (`hole.nominal_alignment`). Fit stayed unresolved for lack of drawing limits (`interfaces.fit`), and after the engineer answered, the model asked again for numeric limits. The vendor pin's diameter tolerance was in the package as a configuration property no check read.
- **Verdict and source**: the engineer's answers contradict the pass's premise: "press fit" to the fit question on 2026-09-26, and "pin is oversize for 3mm hole" on 2026-09-28 (`findings-2026-09-28.md`, ER-002 row; `session.json` of the 2026-09-26 review, the ER-005 answer). The sitting's analysts recorded the floating-fastener pass (N3) and the unused tolerance (N5) (`specs/013-engineer-first-review/research.md:1162, 1164`); the spec lists the re-ask for numeric limits after "press fit" as a question that must not be asked (`specs/013-engineer-first-review/spec.md:57`).
- **Producer**: split. The floating-fastener pass comes from the joint map, which the pre-run runs: code. The press-fit reading needs the fit check set up by the model: model-produced.
- **Tuned-on**: yes (the re-ask and the fit question were written into feature 013).
- **Graded as** (corrected in round 1, RR-B11; round 2, RR2-3, RR2-4; round 3, RR3-6): variant A, a known defect `fit.size_only`, expected suspected, model-produced: the fit check writes suspected for an interference or transition fit and never demonstrated (`checks/fit.py:75-77, 112`), and `interfaces.fit` is the checklist item, not a check id. A fit finding carries only the drawing locations of its two dimensions (`tools/checks_fit.py:67`; `reviewer/tests/unit/test_tools_checks_fit.py:194`), so the entry names the drawing document that holds them, never a part document, and the bore's and shaft's references (`checks/fit.py:121, 170`); a fit on another pin, or the bore against itself, neither credits nor clears it (FR-003; §8.12). Variant B, `fit.size_only` expected unresolved: both diameters drawn with no tolerance, so the fit check writes unresolved naming the bore (`checks/fit.py:85-86, 98-100`), while the pin's oversize sits only as free text in a configuration property no check reads. A clearance result writes checked_within_scope, which clears the defect (FR-005). A question-count rule of at most one question on `interfaces.fit` (spec FR-060); the scripted answer "press fit"; after it, a follow-up naming `interfaces.fit` and no ids is a re-ask (spec US3-6; §8.3). A floating-fastener pass or an invented hole class is wrong; how a key grades that pass is open (§8.13).
- **Seed**: `mechanical.PackageBuilder` as `generate_fixtures.build_small_assembly` does: a plate `hole(hole_type='clearance', size='Ø3.0', end_condition='blind', ...)`; one pin component in it, with a coaxial `cylinder_face(diameter_mm=3.0)` at 0.000 mm and a mesh; no positive-volume interference. Both diameters are drawn on one ingested sheet of one drawing document (§8.14), since a bought pin has no drawing of its own; the golden `shaft-bore` fixture holds its bore and shaft on one drawing the same way. The anchor's two pins may both be seeded: each fit's entry names its own two dimensions, and one "2X" dimension drawing both bores gives one entry covering both fits (§8.12). Variant A: the bore drawn Ø3.0 with limits +0.010/0 and the shaft Ø3.0 with limits wholly above them (for example +0.020/+0.014), an interference fit. Variant B: both drawn Ø3.0 with no tolerance, and the pin's oversize as free text in a configuration property, set by `model_copy` after `build()` or with `standards.PartSpec(config_properties=...)`. No model dimension, model position tolerance or ISO 286 Hole Wizard class on the pin or the plate: any of them makes the argument-free joint map run `hole.position_stack` on the joint, which writes suspected or demonstrated for an oversize pin, a code-produced defect of another check (§8.14); `build_tolerances_assembly`'s model dimensions are therefore not reused here. Clean twin: a 3.0 mm pin in a 3.1 mm hole with slip-fit limits on the sheet, where the alignment pass is right and the fit check writes checked_within_scope, keyed as a correct condition on the drawing document.
- **Hard because**: nominal CAD hides the intent. A press fit modelled line-to-line shows no interference and aligns perfectly, so two code checks pass. The hole feature says clearance. The only evidence is free text on a vendor configuration and a terse answer. The right output is neither a pass nor a guessed tolerance, and once "press fit" is answered it must not be asked again.

### R-4: zero-volume size-for-size contacts reported as interference

- **Kind**: false alarm. Family: interference.
- **What happened**: small-assembly reviews with the pins resolved (2026-09-18, and the 2026-09-20 recording used for replay) produced two `interference.static` findings, demonstrated and medium, each 0.0 mm³, one between each pin and the plate. Both sat in Start here. On the big assembly, four of the ten Start-here slots across two evening runs were zero-volume contacts reported the same way.
- **Verdict and source**: the owner's decision of 2026-09-23, taken after reviewing those runs: size-for-size contacts are listed separately as contacts, never as interference findings; only positive-volume overlaps are findings. This is the owner's verdict, not the seat engineer's words; the engineer's later answers ("press fit", "pin is oversize") confirm the contact is the intended joint (`specs/010-mechanical-checks/spec.md:11, 17`; `specs/010-mechanical-checks/research.md:717`; `docs/roadmap-2026-09-22.md:27-28`; `docs/pane-findings-2026-09-18.md:175`; `docs/pane-findings-2026-09-20-review-gui.md:48`).
- **Producer**: code (the interference group check; the pre-run judges every group under checks-first, `prerun.py:156`). A regression guard for feature 010's contact list.
- **Tuned-on**: yes.
- **Graded as**: forbidden `interference.static` on the zero-volume pairs; the positive twin as a code-produced known defect, expected demonstrated with its volume. The contact must not turn into "clearance OK" either (see R-3).
- **Seed**: `mechanical.PackageBuilder`: a 3.0 mm pin face in a 3.0 mm hole at 0.000 mm, with `interference(pin, plate)` at the default zero volume, twice, as `build_small_assembly` does; one `interference(..., volume_mm3=None, is_possible=True)` row. Positive twin in its own package, since a known defect sharing the plate with the forbidden contacts would overlap them and be refused (spec FR-009): a second pin offset 0.75 mm in a 3.1 mm hole with `interference(pin2, plate, volume_mm3=1.237)`, as `build_big_assembly` does for its offset dowel (`reviewer/tests/fixtures/mechanical/generate_fixtures.py:444-445, 679`); it must give exactly one demonstrated interference finding carrying its volume. The joint map also judges the offset pin's alignment; the validation's code-only check (FR-054) shows whether that result must be keyed as a second code-produced defect or the offset kept inside the joint's allowance.
- **Hard because**: SOLIDWORKS reports coincident faces as interferences, and "demonstrated" with an interference check outranks everything in ranking. A real positive overlap may sit beside the contacts.

### R-5: lightweight pins leaving interference "checked, clean"

- **Kind**: real condition missed (a coverage verdict). Family: interference coverage.
- **What happened**: small-assembly reviews of 2026-09-19 and the evening of 2026-09-20, with both dowel pins lightweight. Live interference detection returned no groups, and the review recorded interference as checked. Fit and stack stayed unresolved. The "not read" sentence was only in coverage and in a header badge cut off by the pane's width. The same assembly with its pins resolved produced two pin/plate rows.
- **Verdict and source**: the engineer: lightweight components hollow out interference and fit; the engineer asked for a resolve step before the review. The 2026-09-20 debrief calls the empty result "the lightweight hollow, not a clean assembly" (`docs/pane-findings-2026-09-19.md:34, 112`; `docs/pane-findings-2026-09-20.md:238`; `docs/feature-request-resolve-lightweight.md:45`).
- **Producer** (corrected in round 1, RR-B8): offline, the model, through its own coverage claim. Without the bridge the pre-run writes `coverage.prerun.interference` as skipped ("the package reports no interference, so no group was checked", `prerun.py:993-995`); a "checked, clean" row comes only from a clean live detection (`prerun.py:1356-1370`), which needs the bridge. Offline, only the model's own `mark_coverage` can claim interference checked.
- **Tuned-on**: yes.
- **Graded as**: expected coverage on the `interference` item: unresolved or skipped, read with the report's own reader (§8.4). On the on arm the report reads not reached either way (the pre-run's skipped row, or finalisation's unresolved row); on the off arm, a model that marks interference checked reads checked, a coverage mismatch. With no interference rows no check can raise an interference finding, so the report's "issues" state cannot hide the claim. Expected coverage on `interfaces.fit`: unresolved, read through the fits-and-stacks goal that also holds `interfaces.stack` (§8.4). No fit known defect is keyed: the fit check (`fit.size_only`) reads only drawn dimensions (§8.14), and the case carries no drawing. Per-part expectations are not taken (§8.4); the unread pins show in the gaps the transcript carries.
- **Seed**: `mechanical.PackageBuilder` small assembly: the plate resolved with its 3.0 mm holes; both pins added with `component(pin, suppression='lightweight')`, for which the builder adds a "bodies were not read" gap each (`tests/support/mechanical.py:535-577`); the pin document created with `document(..., opened=False)` so its properties and mass are absent (`:471-527`); no interference rows. Twin: the R-4 package, where the contacts are listed and interference is checked.
- **Hard because**: the tool call succeeds and returns an empty list, so absence looks like a clean result. The only evidence of the gap is in gaps and coverage rows, and results change from run to run with SOLIDWORKS load state.

### R-6: empty holes correctly left alone, because the screws belong to another assembly

- **Kind**: correct negative. Family: fastener.
- **What happened**: in the 2026-09-28 review the custom plate carries M8 counterbored through holes (two Hole Wizard features, several instances) and a tapped hole, and the assembly holds no screws. The review raised no fastener or hole finding, asked once which fasteners go in the holes and, after the answer, kept the fastener checks unresolved because the package holds no fasteners.
- **Verdict and source**: accepted as correct by the engineer, who answered that the screws belong to a separate assembly, so the empty holes are by design (`findings-2026-09-28.md`, ER-004 row; `session.json` of the 2026-09-28 review, coverage "fasteners" unresolved and no fastener finding). The only correct negative with an explicit engineer statement.
- **Producer**: none for the negative; the question is the model's. The positive twin is a code-produced thread mismatch from the joint map.
- **Tuned-on**: yes.
- **Graded as**: forbidden `fastener.*` and `hole.*` findings on the empty holes; a question-count rule of at most one question on `fasteners` (spec FR-060); the scripted answer (the screws are in another assembly); no re-ask. Expected coverage on `fasteners`: unresolved or out of scope, since a key may list several statuses (spec FR-018); the recorded session has it unresolved.
- **Seed**: `mechanical.PackageBuilder`: plate `hole(hole_type='counterbore', size='M8', end_condition='through', ...)` at five points, plus `hole(hole_type='tapped', size='M6x1.0', thread='M6x1.0', thread_depth_mm=10.0, hole_depth_mm=12.0, end_condition='blind')`; no `screw_document` or `screw`; two dowel pins seated on their hole axes, since the forbidden `hole.*` entry names the plate and so covers the pins' joints too. Positive twin: `screw_document('SHC', 'M4-0.7', 12.0, ...)` and `screw(...)` seated in an M5x0.8 tapped hole, which must give a demonstrated thread mismatch; in its own package, since a known defect beside the forbidden entries on the same plate would overlap them (spec FR-009).
- **Hard because**: Hole Wizard names the intended screw, so empty holes look like missing hardware. Which assembly installs the screws is a scope question only the engineer can answer; the review must not invent a missing fastener, must not re-ask, and must accept a terse, misspelt answer.

### R-7: the custom plate's under-defined sketch, loose features and empty equations

- **Kind**: real defect found. Family: modelling practice on a custom part.
- **What happened**: every small-assembly run graded the custom plate with an under-defined sketch (`rms.sketches.fully_defined`, with `standards.part.sketches_fully_defined`), content features outside every group folder (`rms.grouping.all_features_in_a_group`) and an empty equation manager (`rms.params.global_variables_present`). On 2026-09-26 the sketch was named twice in one finding and the loose-feature count was 48; after the shared tree reading of feature 013 it is 22.
- **Verdict and source**: agreed by the engineer: "the real modelling issues on the custom plate" that the vendor pin pushed down (`findings-2026-09-26.md`, F1; `specs/013-engineer-first-review/spec.md:19, 133`; `specs/013-engineer-first-review/research.md:1161`).
- **Producer**: pre-run code (RMS; the standards family as well). A code-produced regression guard.
- **Tuned-on**: yes.
- **Graded as**: code-produced known defects: one under-defined-sketch finding naming the sketch once (RMS and standards either cross-referenced or both keyed); a grouping finding whose count equals the features an engineer can move; one global-variables finding. Every custom-part finding survives the bought-parts regrade.
- **Seed**: `features.rms_package(parts=[PartSpec(document_id='doc:2', name='FICT-7001', features=[sketch_feature('Sketch2', raw_status=2, consumers=('Boss-Extrude2',)), feature('Boss-Extrude2', 'Extrusion', parent_names=('Sketch2',)), ...about 20 more loose features], equations=())])`, with the absorbed sketch listed at two depths (the shape the shared tree reading handles). Mirror the tree in `standards.standards_package(documents=[standards.PartSpec(name='FICT-7001', features=...)])`. Clean twin: sketches fully defined inside `folder('1-Ref', ...)` and further `folder(...)` groups, with one `equation(..., is_global=True)`.
- **Hard because**: the defect is real but was buried under vendor-part noise. The absorbed sketch appears twice in the tree, so naive counting names it twice and inflates the loose features. Two families report one defect. It must survive the regrade.

### R-8: revision-missing findings caused by a profile that names the wrong property

- **Kind**: false alarm (configuration). Family: hygiene against the standards profile.
- **What happened**: the 2026-09-26 review was graded against the seat's placeholder profile, the repository's fictional example. It raised three low findings saying the assembly, the plate and the vendor pin carry no revision property under the profile's property name.
- **Verdict and source**: rejected by the engineer: the files carry their revision under a different property name, and every Standards and hygiene verdict on that seat had been graded against made-up rules (`findings-2026-09-26.md`, F6; `specs/013-engineer-first-review/spec.md:11`; `specs/013-engineer-first-review/research.md:1163`).
- **Producer**: pre-run code (hygiene, with the profile).
- **Tuned-on**: yes.
- **Graded as**: forbidden per-document `hygiene.revision_present` findings. **To resolve in plan.md**: the anchor's ideal is one unresolved line saying the profile is likely wrong because none of the N documents carries its property (the zero-match precedent of feature 010, User Story 7, scenario 3). Whether the product emits such a line for hygiene today decides whether the key can expect it, or grades the forbidden hits only; making the product emit it is outside this feature.
- **Seed**: `standards.standards_package(documents=[AssemblySpec(name='FICT-7000', properties={'Rev': 'A'}), PartSpec(name='FICT-7001', properties={'Rev': 'A'}), a fictional pin PartSpec with the same property])`, graded against the repository's fictional example profile, whose revision property (`DocRev`, `config/standards.example.yaml:57-60`) no document uses. That was the anchor's own configuration: the 2026-09-26 review was graded against that example. No case carries a profile of its own: a run takes one profile and a study refuses runs that differ in its digest (FR-011, US2-6), so such a case could not share a study with the others (FR-027; round 2, RR2-1). Positive twin, in its own package: every document carries `DocRev` but one, which gives one real finding. `generate_fixtures.house` is not used: it writes profile A's revision property (`tests/support/mechanical.py:108`).
- **Hard because**: the check is right against the profile it was given; the error is in the configuration. It shows only as the same failure on every document, each carrying a near-synonym property. A reviewer has to collapse N identical failures into one doubt about the rule.

### The big assembly's three analyst-found defects

Decision 7A takes these as real. They were found by analysts reading the big assembly's dump; the reviewer missed or underweighted them; no engineer has given a verdict. Under 15A they may be held out. Under 16A each is code-produced when its geometry is present; a model-produced variant must withhold the evidence the code check needs, and the generator records what it withheld.

- **AF-1: M4 screws in M5x0.8 tapped holes.** The big assembly's 68 screws were not recognised as fasteners at the time (only Toolbox parts counted), so an M4 screw in an M5 tapped hole went unreported (`docs/roadmap-2026-09-22.md:25-27`); the later probe counted two such screws (`specs/010-mechanical-checks/research.md:649-650`). Producer: code when the screw sits on the tapped instance's axis (the joint map's thread check). Model variant: the screw carries no placement the joint map can pair (the same probe found 11 of 68 real screws on no extracted axis), so the model must name the pair to `check_fastener_joint`. Seed: `PackageBuilder.screw_document('SHC', 'M4-0.7', ...)` and `screw(...)` against `hole(hole_type='tapped', size='M5x0.8', thread='M5x0.8', ...)`; the big mechanical fixture already seeds an M4-named screw in an M5 thread. The entry names the screw and its tapped hole, which its findings carry on every path under 19A, so other screws between the same parts neither credit nor clear it (§8.12).
- **AF-2: a 0.75 mm dowel offset.** The model checked unrelated hole pairs for alignment while five real candidates existed, one with a 0.75 mm dowel offset (`docs/roadmap-2026-09-22.md:28-29`). Producer: code when present (the joint map's alignment since feature 010: `specs/010-mechanical-checks/spec.md:54, 233`). Model variant: withhold what lets the joint map pair the holes, so the model must set up `check_hole_alignment`. With no tolerance that tool writes unresolved with the measured offset (`checks/hole_alignment.py:98-101`), so the model variant is keyed expected unresolved; it is expected demonstrated only when the seed draws a coaxiality tolerance on an ingested sheet (§8.14), which the tool reads through `resolve_dimension` (`tools/checks_fastener.py:357-359`), its finding then also citing that drawing (`:377`) (round 2, RR2-4). Seed: `build_big_assembly`'s 3.0/3.1 mm dowel pair offset by 0.750 mm (`reviewer/tests/fixtures/mechanical/generate_fixtures.py:444-445, 679`). The candidate pairs may sit between the same plates: the entry names the two offset holes, so a call on another pair, with or without the drawn tolerance, neither credits nor clears the defect, and the anchor's own failure, checking unrelated hole pairs, scores as missed (US5-16; §8.12; RR3-6).
- **AF-3: interference groups of about 250 mm³.** Positive-volume groups that the review underweighted beside the zero-volume contacts. Producer: code only (16A: interference is a code-produced guard; the group check judges every group). Seed: `PackageBuilder.interference(a, b, volume_mm3=...)` with a positive volume among zero-volume contacts; the big fixture's shape is 113 groups, 8 of them positive (`specs/010-mechanical-checks/tasks.md:35`; `specs/010-mechanical-checks/spec.md:21`).

### What makes a case hard

- A finding can be true of the geometry and still wrong for the review: whether it matters depends on the part's role, decided from conflicting signals.
- Nominal CAD hides design intent. A press fit modelled line-to-line shows no interference and aligns perfectly, while a zero-volume contact looks like interference: one joint invites two opposite errors.
- Absence of evidence reads as a pass: lightweight or unread components, a tool call returning an empty list, a drawing that exists on disk but cannot be read on this seat.
- Labels mislead: a dowel hole typed "clearance", a floating-fastener rule applied to a pin, Hole Wizard naming screws installed in another assembly.
- The engineer's terse answers ("press fit", "latest", "skip", "separate assembly") are final. They must be used, not re-asked, and not turned into invented numbers or tolerance classes.
- Plausible generic advice is partly true in general; every claim has to be tied to evidence actually read.
- Configuration errors look like design defects: the same failure on every document signals a wrong profile, not N defects.
- Duplicated tree rows inflate counts and make two check families report one defect.
- Ranking amplifies noise: instance count and representative choice put a vendor part first.
- Run-to-run variance: the same design gives different finding sets with SOLIDWORKS load state and with the model's choices.

### Gaps and candidates

- No engineer-agreed real defect in a judgement family (fastener, fit and axial stack, interference, drawing, part role) exists yet; the only agreed real defects are R-7's modelling defects. The analyst-found defects are taken as real by 7A, not by an engineer.
- Candidates to put before the engineer, none with a verdict: Standards on the plate's drawing reporting "no revision table" although SOLIDWORKS recorded one (analysts verified; `specs/013-engineer-first-review/spec.md:133`); the 48-versus-22 loose-feature count; `hygiene.duplicate_part_number` and `hygiene.duplicate_description` between the small assembly and its plate on 2026-09-28, which share a stem on purpose (probably a false alarm); `mass.density` passing for the steel pin and the aluminium plate (the engineer objected only to their ranking, F2); `interfaces.stack` left alone after the answers "none" (2026-09-26) and "skip" (2026-09-28).
- cover-blind-tap's keys are fictional, and the six-check key of the 2026-09-19 study is provisional, not gold (`docs/pane-findings-2026-09-19.md:165`).

### Seed caveats

- `PackageBuilder.document` writes empty configuration properties and fixes the path under the fictional root. Set folders and configuration properties after `build()` with `model_copy`, as `sitting_package` does, or use `standards.PartSpec(folder=..., config_properties=...)`.
- The sitting fixture already models the vendor pin, the unclear spacer, the shared same-name drawing candidate and the eight recorded questions, so it is the natural base for R-1 and R-2, in shape.
- The existing builders and fixtures write profile A's property names (`tests/support/mechanical.py:108-111`; `tests/support/sitting.py:8-9, 188-198`; `house`, `reviewer/tests/fixtures/mechanical/generate_fixtures.py:114-124`). Committed cases take the example profile's names instead (FR-027; §8.14).
- `PackageBuilder.model_dimension` and `DrawingBuilder.dimension` never reach the fit, stack or toleranced alignment checks; only ingested sheet dimensions do (§8.14).

---

## §4 The 23 planning defaults (decision 11A)

The two planning passes were read-only and tests-first. Their forced choices are listed in each plan's own order: **SP-n** for the scorer plan (decision 4A), **PP-n** for the parity plan (decision 6A). The list numbers here are authoritative; the scorer plan's own cross-references drift after number 11. Each entry: the question, the options, the default taken 2026-09-28 under 11A, why, and any later change.

### Scorer plan (decision 4A)

- **SP-1. Origin buckets.** Carried findings (lever 11a) and set-up findings (`record_provenance`, written before the first turn with no step) are neither pre-run, model-called nor model. Options: A: five buckets, prerun, model-called, model, carried (`carried_over_from` set), set-up (the provenance family, whose single writer is `record_provenance`); B: three, with carried and set-up folded into prerun; C: three, with carried and set-up counted nowhere. **Default A.** Why: nothing misfiled; B makes prerun non-zero with lever 5 off; C hides findings. **Later**: D9 widens this to seven values.
- **SP-2. Where the pre-run mark is set.** Options: A: after the call returns in `prerun.recorded_call(prerun=True)`, mark the steps appended during the call; only `_prerun_call` passes true (`prerun.py:1190, 1201`); B: thread a flag through five call layers to the session sink; C: a session-level list of pre-run finding ids. **Default A.** Why: one file, about five lines, exact, because `recorded_call` already captures the step index. B changes provider-facing signatures; C is a second list beside the findings. The plan said restated calls (`agent/runner.py:1258`, ids `restated_*`) stay unmarked because they happen only after an engineer answers. **Later**: scripted answers (9A) make them happen in benchmark runs; D9 gives them their own origin, so they are marked too.
- **SP-3. Findings that cite no step.** The argument-taking check tools record findings with no step id (the plan named three; at `be01974` four: `check_fastener_joint`, `check_hole_alignment`, `check_fit`, `check_axial_stack`, `tools/checks_fastener.py:283, 373`; `tools/checks_fit.py:67, 109`). Options: A: a code finding citing no pre-run step is model-called, with a test that every pre-run tool's findings cite their own step; B: make the tools cite their step. **Default A.** Why: B changes the bytes the model reads and moves token measurements. **Later** (round 3, RR3-3): D9 classes them as configured checks, and every other model-called check by the tool on its cited step (§8.5). **Amended 2026-10-07 (owner decision 40A, §1):** at `be01974` a fifth writer cited none, `check_interference_group` (`tools/checks_interference.py:120`), although its contacts cite their step (`:161`), so a pre-run interference finding would have read `configured_check` by `scoring.md` 5.3's rule 5; 40A makes its findings cite their step, as its contacts do. After it, the finding writers citing no step are exactly `check_fastener_joint` and `check_hole_alignment` (`tools/checks_fastener.py:283, 373`), `check_fit` and `check_axial_stack` (`tools/checks_fit.py:67, 109`), `record_drawing_finding` (`tools/session.py:512`, `source` `model`) and the provenance set-up records (`agent/runner.py:414`); a carried finding keeps its earlier review's ids (`carry_over.py:498`). Every other finding writer cites the step in flight.
- **SP-4. Document-scoped findings.** They carry their document only in provenance, never in `drawing_locations`. Options: A: overlap by `drawing_locations` only; B: `drawing_locations`, plus provenance document ids only when the finding has no component ids and no drawing locations; C: always union all provenance documents. **Default B.** Why: A never credits a key entry on a document-scoped check (the bug class 4A fixes); C over-matches, since a component finding's provenance includes its part document. FR-003 states B.
- **SP-5. Correct-condition ids.** Options: A: a required, unique id on every correct condition (C-1, ...), with contradicted and confirmed ids on the scorecard; B: list index; C: counts only. **Default A.** Cost: ids added to both committed keys (1 plus 18) and the fixtures.
- **SP-6. Two findings on one expected-unresolved defect.** An unresolved and a demonstrated or suspected finding both match. Options: A: credited and counted as a status mismatch, whatever the order; B: flagged. **Default A.** Why: order-independent and conservative; for D-1 a demonstrated bottoming without thread depth is the wrong claim. 13A keeps this rule.
- **SP-7. A checked-within-scope finding on a known defect.** Options: A: record it as cleared (reported; the defect stays missed unless something else credits it); B: keep ignoring it. **Default A.** Why: it exposes the product's worst outcome at the cost of one list. **Later**: the spec's Terms make a cleared defect not detected rather than missed (RR-A10); the plan said it has no effect on gates, and in the pane study the cleared guard is named and decides only where the simulation allows (D1, D17).
- **SP-8. Status mismatch scope.** Options: A: only the approved case (expected unresolved, credited by a demonstrated or suspected finding), with a demonstrated or suspected expectation recorded but not changing scoring; B: any difference between the finding's status and the expected one. **Default A.** **Later**: 13A requires every defect to state its expected status and adds *flagged below expected* for an unresolved finding on a defect expected demonstrated or suspected. Demonstrated against suspected is still not a mismatch.
- **SP-9. Validation scope.** Options: A: wildcard-aware collisions (exact against exact, `x.*` against `x.y`, nested wildcards), for defect/condition and defect/defect pairs; B: string equality, defect/condition only. **Default A.** Why: two defects one finding could both credit are the inflation 4A removes. **Later**: D7 extends the same test to forbidden entries.
- **SP-10. Wildcard boundary.** Options: A: `x.*` matches any check starting `x.` at any depth, never `x` itself and never `xs.y`; B: exactly one more segment. **Default A.**
- **SP-11. Old scorecards.** Options: A: a scorer version (absent reads as 1); scoring writes the new version and compare refuses anything else, naming the re-score command; B: document that runs must be re-scored. **Default A.** Why: a mixed study produces a false DEFECT LOST; re-scoring is cheap because the session is kept. **Later**: D14.2 adds the golden-pinned bump rule and refuses newer versions too.
- **SP-12. Producer.** Options: A: required on every known defect; B: optional, with an "untagged" recall group. **Default A.** **Later**: 16A defines it.
- **SP-13. Ledger reach.** Options: A: run rows gain flagged, status mismatch, contradicted, confirmed, findings by origin and false alarms by origin; the decision row gains recall by producer (reported, not gated); ledger schema 1.1 and the committed document regenerated; B: only the adoption fix plus flagged and contradicted; C: only the adoption fix. **Default A.**
- **SP-14. Lists or lists plus counts.** Options: A: id lists only, counts derived where rendered, the aggregate storing summed integers; B: the existing list-plus-count pattern. **Default A.** Why: one writer per number; a computed field is ruled out because the strict model would refuse it on reload. The existing twins are left alone.

### Parity plan (decision 6A)

- **PP-1. `review --pane-defaults` and explanations.** Options: A: explanations are resolved in the shared resolver for both commands (typed flag, else on with `--pane-defaults`, else off), and the pane's default moves into `PaneDefaults` for the chat server to read; B: only `benchmark run` infers explanations. **Default A** (named in 11A). Why: B gives the switch two meanings and leaves `review --pane-defaults` contradicting its help (`cli.py:459-461`). Cost: one bounded extra request for existing users, reversible with `--no-explain-findings`.
- **PP-2. Explanations in the pane study.** Options: A: held fixed and stated; each arm of the bundle study must type `--explain-findings` or `--no-explain-findings`, or the run is refused (exit 2), and compare's existing must-match stays; B: part of the bundle, on in the on arm only; C: an untyped setting follows the pane in both arms. **Default A** (named in 11A). Why: B charges the explanation tokens to the on arm; C is an implicit rule keyed on the study name; without A a mismatch is found only after the paid runs.
- **PP-3. How compare checks that the on arm equals the pane.** Options: A: against the typed provenance marker, with equality enforced at run time by the study-arm check and the session cross-check extended to the model view; the off arm against fixed constants; B: re-derive the pane defaults from the current code at compare time. **Default A.** Why: under B every committed pane study would be refused the day the pane defaults change.
- **PP-4. Telling the pane baseline from the plain baseline.** Options: A: `--study none --arm baseline --pane-defaults`, labelled "none (pane defaults)" from the typed marker, with its own decision row and sign-off key; B: `--study pane_defaults --arm baseline`; C: both keyed "none". **Default A.** Why: C mixes two distributions and makes sign-off carrying refuse duplicate keys (`compare.py:1094-1098`); B contradicts "a baseline's study is none" (`agent/settings.py:757-761`). D5 keeps it.
- **PP-5. An unusable `--standards-profile`.** Options: A: load it before the set and before any review; an unreadable or invalid profile exits 1 with one line; otherwise record the profile identity's sha256, the digest the standards report cites; B: hash the raw bytes and let each review grade standards "not evaluated", as `review` does. **Default A.** Why: a study that grades no standards in six paid runs is wasted. D5 keeps it for `benchmark run` only.
- **PP-6. Does `--pane-defaults` declare a study for the P-3 refusal?** Options: A: yes, like `--lever`; so do `--payload-slimming` and `--history-pruning`; B: no. **Default A.** Why: a pane smoke run on the pilot set would otherwise look like a measurement; the override still allows it and is recorded.
- **PP-7. Ledger columns.** Options: A: "model view" and "profile" columns after "explanations", with the committed header regenerated in the same commit; B: provenance only. **Default A.** Why: a pane-baseline row and a plain-baseline row are otherwise identical.
- **PP-8. The pane row's lever counter.** Options: A: the existing gate counter: fit and axial-stack calls per run must not fall, with the runs where they fell; B: lever 13's tool array per request; C: the name only. **Default A** (named in 11A). **Later** (18A): in the pane study the counts are shown with a paired interval, named when it excludes zero, never deciding. Lever studies keep the counter.
- **PP-9. Further must-match fields.** Options: A: the pane-defaults flag becomes must-match (exempt for the bundle study), and `bridge` is recorded but not must-match; B: only the model view and profile digest; C: `bridge` too. **Default A.** Why: `bridge` is always false from `benchmark run`. **Later** (round 1, RR-A11): kept; the spec records live tools and never makes them must-match, and D5's wording is corrected to match.

### The cross-check of the two plans

Every item below is **to resolve in plan.md** unless it says it is settled.

**Shared files.**
- `benchmark/compare.py`, the main collision. Both plans edit the run row and its builder, the run columns and line, the decision row and the docstring's refusal list (`:30-44, 156-196, 421-469, 653-715, 835-865, 899-932`); the scorer plan alone the ledger schema, run loading, the lever decision and the lever columns (`:75, 300-326, 226-273, 867-991`); the parity plan alone the lever counters, the session and arm checks, the study key and the counter (`:120-136, 329-397, 475-510, 548-560`).
- Tests: `reviewer/tests/support/studies.py` (scorer `:55-255`, parity `:120-164, 287-343`; every compare test of both plans runs through it); `reviewer/tests/unit/test_benchmark_compare.py` (scorer `:741`, parity `:411-430`; both need the committed-document byte identity, `:993-1019`); `cli.py` and `reviewer/tests/unit/test_cli.py` in separate regions.
- Documents: both regenerate the ledger block of `docs/llm-efficiency-options.md:164-186`, whose old-scorer quality numbers (`:188-220`) neither annotates, and rewrite `specs/005-llm-efficiency/contracts/ab-harness.md` sections 4 (`:194, 216-229`) and 9.
- Listed by neither: `benchmark/adoption.py` (what `decide()` reads changes); `handoff.py:425` (settled by D14.9); `specs/001-agentic-design-review/contracts/cli.md:37`, stale under PP-1.

**Landing order.** The scorer first, then parity rebased onto it (12A); parity's non-compare half (settings, chat server, benchmark runner, the CLI's resolver, `_review_fn` and `benchmark_run`) can be written in parallel. No pane study runs before the scorer merges: a run recorded before the pre-run mark exists can never gain it (settled by D9: origin unknown), the scorer changes what `decide()` reads, and the compare collisions rebase more easily that way. Scorer order: keys, scorecard, session and pre-run mark, adoption, compare, CLI summary, contracts, docs. Parity order: settings and chat server; benchmark runner; CLI; compare; study fixtures; the ledger schema (one bump or two); the committed ledger; the handoff allowlist. One regeneration of the committed header needs the final column order fixed up front.

**Contradictions.**
- Ledger schema: the scorer bumps `compare.py:75` to 1.1; parity adds run-row fields without the schema, its test (`test_benchmark_compare.py:741`) or the contract's example. Bump twice, or once for both.
- Run column order: parity pins "model view" and "profile" after "explanations"; the scorer's six columns have no position; the header is pinned byte for byte.
- Refusal order: the scorer-version refusal must precede full scorecard validation and parity's model-view cross-check, or a pre-change folder surfaces a validation error instead of "re-run `swreview benchmark score`"; the new aggregate fields have no defaults, so validation (`compare.py:307`) raises first, and the study fixtures (`studies.py:230-253`) must set them.
- Origin on sessions recorded before the scorer: settled by D9 (unknown).
- Tool calls on the pane row: the run's count (`compare.py:293-295`) counts pre-run steps as the model's; the pre-run mark allows a split, and the ledger text must at least say so.
- `bridge`: the pane passes `bridge=bool(bridge)` (`chat/server.py:2132`) and `_review_fn` does not; `_review_fn` passes `bridge=False`, or the parity test normalises defaults.
- The planned contract sentence that the profile digest is "not against the session, which carries none" is false: every standards finding stamps the sha256 (`checks/standards/report.py:258`). It must not be written.
- Compare's baseline branch fires for lever none with no arm (`compare.py:380`), so a plain `benchmark run --payload-slimming` smoke run can never be compared; a model-view condition there keeps it mis-scoped.
- The scorer plan's `test_cli.py:2397-2417` change keys D-1 to a drawing document the fixture package lacks (`tests/support/packages.py:97`).
- Once the scorer lands, rms-part's 18 correct conditions count contradictions as false alarms, so the false-alarm rule decides lever rows more often: correct, and a reason to land it before any study is scored.

**Missing tests.**
- Keys: empty lists (no defects and no conditions, or only conditions) load and score, recall by producer not computable; a target with only document ids, and the reverse; an entry naming parts and documents is credited by a part-only or a drawing-only finding, refused in validation when only one list overlaps, and as a condition contradicted through the document path alone; wildcards (`fastener` against `fastener.*` no collision, nested ones colliding, a wildcard condition against an exact defect, a trailing dot); every id in both committed keys resolves where the package exists (D7).
- Old and foreign folders: a byte-level old scorecard reaches the version refusal, not a validation error; an old checks-first session re-scores as origin unknown (D9); re-scoring writes the new version and compare accepts it; a newer scorer's scorecard is refused (D14.2); an unknown field exits 1 naming the folder (today a bare validation error, `cli.py:530-542`); a run row whose session predates the model view renders "off" or "unknown", deliberately.
- An end-to-end origin test on a real checks-first review (`tests/support/prerun.py` `review`, scripted calls after the pre-run).
- Settled by the spec's Edge Cases and D17: a checked-within-scope and a suspected finding on one defect; off-runs where one credited D-1 and another flagged it.
- Output: the `benchmark score` summary line pinned; the ledger and its schema carry the new run-row keys; the handoff export carries the new provenance fields, never the profile path (D14.9).
- Parity: a no-study smoke run with `--payload-slimming` or `--history-pruning` (refused as a baseline, or a raw row); a bundle-study run with no `--arm`; the provenance digest against the standards findings' sha256 (refused when they differ); a plain `--standards-profile` run without `--pane-defaults` records the digest and does not trip P-3; an on arm with the pane marker and all-off settings (accepted, or refused as mislabelled); `RunSpec.settings()` for a pane baseline, and its "none (pane defaults)" counter cell.

**Plan text written ahead of the owner.** The scorer plan implemented SP-4 B, SP-1 A, SP-9 A, SP-7 A and SP-5's condition ids while quoting other options as "approved", and the parity plan exempted the model view for the bundle study; under 11A these are the adopted defaults.

---

## §5 Spec-review defaults of 2026-09-29

Each default below was taken on 2026-09-29, follows the owner decisions above, and is reversible. Each entry: the default, why, the alternatives considered, the review issues it settles, and what stays open. A table at the end gives the disposition of every review issue.

### D1. The pane study's verdict

**Default.** The pane study does not use the lever adoption rules (`benchmark/adoption.py:252`). Its verdict, *quality loss*, *no 10-point loss* or *inconclusive*, comes from five rules (FR-044, FR-045; §8.1 holds the statistics):
1. **Recall**: paired, on held-out model-produced defects, the package as the unit so the defects of one package run move together.
2. **False alarms**: paired, per package, likewise (narrowed to model origin by 17A, tuned-on hits left out by D15).
3. **Code-produced guard**: worst-case (revised by D17: no control-stability rule).
4. **Cleared guard**: named; deciding only where the simulation allows (revised by D17). Clearing a check on an inferred input is the outcome Principle I names as the worst.
5. **Sensitivity**: rules 1 and 2 again with behavioural stops included (D2).

Any rule's loss is a quality loss, below the floor too; nothing reads first (D17). A zero-width interval is never read as "no difference": inconclusive for recall, no loss for false alarms (RR-A8). The false-alarm rule has no equivalence margin: it can call a loss, and otherwise stays silent. How often the whole verdict calls identical arms a loss is simulated, not asserted per rule (D17). Shown, not deciding: the one list in FR-059 (RR-A24). No token or wall-clock threshold applies: the defaults are already adopted. **Later** (round 1): D15 (now 17A) narrows rule 2; D17 revises rules 3 and 4 and removes the first draft's claim that each paired rule calls identical arms a loss 2.5% of the time.

**Why.** Once defects are stochastic, the lever rules fire on noise. Three off-runs agree on all 34 model defects with probability about 6e-11 (uniform rates) or 1e-7 (U-shaped rates), so CONTROL UNSTABLE would pre-empt every verdict (S3; adoption-rules-vs-stochastic-defects, §2). The false-alarm median rule calls identical arms "keep off" 35 to 46% of the time (S3). A per-defect interval that ignores how defects of one package move together calls identical arms a loss 9 to 11% of the time (S1). Three values separate "a loss was seen", "10 points can be ruled out" and "cannot tell": failing to show a drop is not showing there is none (S2).

**Alternatives considered.** Keep `decide()` and add the paired rule inside it (CL-11's reading of the draft): CONTROL UNSTABLE pre-empts it. CL-11's two-sided 90% interval: 5% one-sided per rule, so two deciding rules could call identical arms a loss up to 10% of the time. C-2: let every failure-mode count decide by its interval: with many rules at 2.5 to 5% each, identical arms fail the 5% bound by a wide margin (S3). A per-defect paired t-interval: 9 to 11% false losses under package correlation (S1). Resampling designs rather than packages (S5): 8 clusters put the critical value at 2.365 rather than 1.96, and (2.365/1.96)^2 = 1.46, so about 46% more data (about 36% with the power term; corrected in round 1, RR-B20). The code guard reading "found on every off-run" (US8 AS2, C-2) against "found on any off-run" (S10): D17 takes every completed off-run, which needs no control-stability rule.

**Review issues**: CL-11, S2, S3, C-2, F9; C-3 (clearances are named and decide only where the simulation allows, D17; the rest are shown); CL-22 (FR-016's scenario names the counts; FR-046's reproducibility needs the recorded seed); F10's call-counter-decides and PP-8 are superseded for the pane study (18A).

**Open.** Moved to §8.1 in round 1: the interval method (resampled or analytic, FR-044); the levels; whether the cleared guard decides; the code guard's rate on identical arms (settled in round 2: under the spec's Terms the on arm's pre-run detects every code-produced defect, D17).

### D2. Failures

**Default.** Two kinds, as User Story 7 lists them.
- **Infrastructure failures** say nothing about the arm: excluded from every score, counted per arm and class, re-run from the start on resume. Each failed attempt is kept as a failure record (class, retry count, the usage it billed), and a re-run whose responding model differs from the run's is refused (S4).
- **Behavioural stops** are the arm's own outcome: counted per arm, never re-run, out of the main figures and in the sensitivity figure (D1 rule 5). Their re-asks, forbidden hits, unanswerable requests and clears up to the stop still count. A wall-clock limit reached while the stream is still active is one (decided in round 1, RR-A13: the arm's own behaviour took the time). **Later** (D16): reaching the answer-turn bound is not a behavioural stop.
- Drops of either kind count against the floor (FR-058; S4, RR-A9); below it they rule out only "no 10-point loss", and any deciding rule's loss, the sensitivity figure's included, still stands (RR3-1): the stops that push a study below the floor are the runs the sensitivity figure keeps.
- The adapters' own SDK retries are turned off, or counted, so the benchmark's retry is the only one and its count is exact (F21).

**Why.** Excluding every failure is survivorship bias (CL-15, C-4, S4). A lever that makes the model loop or burn its step budget would have its worst runs removed, and re-running them until one completes (FR-043 as drafted) removes them again; the loops of R-3 and R-6 are the behaviour under test (no-run-status-in-scorer, §2). Infrastructure failures say nothing about the arm, so they are re-run.

**Signals today.** Gemini: STOP ends a turn; MAX_TOKENS and the two malformed-call reasons truncate it; every other finish reason is an `error` end (`agent/providers/gemini_provider.py:127-145`), and the blocked-turn error is reported and returned, never raised (`:578`), so a session exists. OpenAI: an incomplete response for any reason except the output ceiling raises (`agent/providers/openai_provider.py:638-654`) and today aborts the set (`benchmark/runner.py:236-255`); under D2 the content-filter stop is a behavioural stop, so it must be recorded as one rather than raised through the set (F17, CL-20).

**Alternatives considered.** Exclude every failure (FR-041 as drafted). Count every failure as missed: conflates a rate-limit error with a model miss. Exclude behavioural stops from the headline with no sensitivity figure (S4's first half). Re-run every failure until one completes (FR-043 as drafted). A wall-clock limit with an active stream as an infrastructure failure, re-run.

**Review issues**: S4, C-4, CL-15, CL-20, F17, F21, RR-A13.

**Settled** (round 4, FR-040): any other stop or incomplete reason a provider reports, on either provider, is a behavioural stop (§8.10). **Later** (26A): a review that overflows the model's context window is a behavioural stop too, class `provider_stop` with its reason, never an infrastructure failure.

### D3. Skip

**Default.** An unscripted question gets nothing, exactly like the pane's Skip: it stays open and unresolved, and is listed as unscripted. When every open question of a turn is unscripted, no answer turn follows, and the review ends with those questions open (finalisation reports them unresolved). Scripted answers go as one batch per turn, as the pane sends them (`extractor/SwReview.AddIn/Review/ReviewPage/app.js:2154, 2234, 2289`), through the run's batch answer path (`agent/runner.py:1138`, one resumed turn per batch). A later request that repeats a still-open question, skipped or not yet answered, is a repeat of an open question, beside re-asks, not a re-ask (S6; widened in round 1 from skipped questions to every open one, RR-A16); the product answers it "already asked" (`tools/session.py:116-120`).

**Why.** The pane's Skip sends nothing (`app.js:2234`: "Skipped stays open and unresolved in the backend: nothing is sent for it"), and Send does nothing with zero answers (`app.js:2289`). Delivering the text "skip" would record the request as answered with the answer "skip", which no pane session records; it would feed the re-ask rule an "answered" question the engineer never answered; and it would give the model a turn the pane would not.

**Alternatives considered.** Deliver the literal text "skip" (the spec as drafted). Give unkeyed requests "skip" (C-12): superseded.

**Review issues**: F3, CL-05, S6; C-12 in part.

### D4. Questions

**Default** (made precise in round 1, RR-A13, RR-B9). A question is a request-for-evidence call, counted from the model's calls, including those the product's guard answers without recording (closed by code, already answered, already asked: `tools/session.py:110-126`), which never reach the session's request list. One matcher (C-17) pairs each call with scripted answers, unanswerable-request entries and earlier questions by checklist item and ids, never by wording. The rule starts from the product's own covering rule (`report/session.py:184-198`: equal items, ids a subset), extended so that a request naming an item and no ids repeats an earlier question on that item (R-3). The part-roles question, which code writes, is matched only by its identity. A request naming neither an item nor a resolvable id, and not that question, is unmatched, in its own column, never silently "not a re-ask" (C-12). Re-asks are split into those the engineer would have seen and those the product refused (CL-04); a repeat of a still-open question has its own column (RR-A16). A case file is refused at run start only for two answers on one item whose resolved ids intersect; an item-only request on an item with several answers is unscripted, the ambiguity named (S7; corrected in round 3, RR3-4). §8.3 holds the rule.

**Why.** A request carries a checklist item (`blocks`, optional) and entity ids, but no check family (`report/session.py:88-113`), so an answer keyed to a check family can never match (F5, S7). The product's guard records nothing for a refused request: no id, no event, only the tool call (`specs/013-engineer-first-review/contracts/re-ask-guard.md` section 3). A scorer counting recorded requests would read R-2's and R-3's failures as zero (F4).

**Alternatives considered.** Key answers by check family (the draft of FR-022). Compare question text: fails on rephrasing. Count recorded requests only (F4). Overlap rather than subset for re-asks (S7's proposal): a follow-up that adds a part is still a re-ask, but unrelated questions on a shared plate become re-asks too. Overlap throughout (C-12). One answer per item (RR3-4's other option): no ambiguity at run time, but a case could not script the fasteners question for two plates.

**Review issues**: F4, F5, S7, C-12, CL-04, RR3-4.

### D5. The pane study's scope and the pane baseline

**Default.** The pane study's two arms may differ only in the levers and the model view (and the arm and pane-defaults flag recording them). Every recorded field matches except run identity (repetition, start time, run id, the responding model within documented aliasing) and whether live tools were present (PP-9; RR-A11; run identity named in round 4, since `rep` and `started_at` always differ), and, across the arms only, the arm, the levers, the model view and the pane-defaults flag. So the profile digest, the explanation setting (11A, PP-2), the answer-turn bound (D14.3) and `set_too_small_override` (`benchmark/runner.py:91`) must match. The pane baseline is runs with the pane defaults and no study (`--study none --arm baseline --pane-defaults`, labelled "none (pane defaults)", PP-4): its own row and sign-off key, never merged with the all-off baseline, used to track the shipped configuration over time. A bad profile stops `benchmark run` before any review with one line (PP-5); `review` keeps its current behaviour, recording the refusal and continuing (`agent/runner.py:427-437`, F24), so the preflight lives in `benchmark_run`, not in the shared resolver. The profile error class subclasses `Exception`, not `ValueError` (`checks/standards/profile.py:153`), so the CLI's error handler must be passed it explicitly. **Later** (round 2, RR2-1): the private slice runs as its own study, with its own set and the real profile, as its own row and never in the verdict; a pane-study set naming a private package is refused (FR-056). Every committed case is built and keyed against the fictional example profile (FR-027).

**Why.** The profile changes far more than the levers: without one, part roles are absent and the part-roles question is never asked; it also drives the standards family and drawing conformance (`checks/part_roles.py:659-720`; `agent/runner.py:1610-1612, 1680-1686`; `checks/drawing_context.py:700-712`). Arms that differ in profile would measure the profile (F11). A pane baseline of its own tracks the shipped configuration across commits, independent of any study's repetitions.

**Alternatives considered.** Let the arms differ in all three fields (the spec as drafted). Reuse the pane study's on-arm runs as the pane baseline (CL-07): no separate paid runs, but it ties the baseline to one study. Spell the pane baseline as arm `baseline` of study `pane` (F10): contradicts "a baseline's study is none" (`agent/settings.py:757-761`). The parity plan notes the plain baseline (3 runs), the pane baseline (3) and the bundle study (6) make 12 paid runs; the owner may skip the separate baselines. A set entry naming its own profile, the digest rule applied per package (RR2-1's second option): one study could span both slices, but provenance, compare and the ledger would carry a profile per package, and half of such a study could never be committed.

**Review issues**: CL-06, CL-07, F10, F11, F24; CL-19 (SC-003 also allows the live SOLIDWORKS tools, recorded as absent); RR2-1.

### D6. Reading coverage

**Default** (revised in round 1, RR-B8). An expected-coverage item's reported status is the one the product's own report shows, read with the report's reader (`report/finding_groups.py` `goal_lines`, `:186`); no second reader is written. §8.4 maps the report's goal states to the statuses a key may list. **Later** (round 4, FR-018): the pre-run's other rows stay under no goal, and no committed key may expect a status only they would give (§8.4).

**Why.** The report is what the engineer reads, so grading it grades what R-5's engineer saw. Its reader already files the pre-run's interference and standards rows under their goals (`report/review_words_v1.yaml:134, 171`), and lets an item's own unresolved, skipped or failed row outrank a checked one (`report/finding_groups.py:233-240`), which is what makes R-5's claim visible. The checklist's own reader takes the first bucket in the order checked, skipped, unresolved, and reads coverage only where the entry's check equals the item's id (`agent/checklist.py:40, 68-76`), while checks write under their own ids (`prerun.py:201-218`): an item with a checked and an unresolved row reads "checked", exactly R-5's failure. The session has five buckets, including failed (`report/session.py:283-287`).

**Alternatives considered.** A separate reader (D6 as first taken): every coverage entry whose check is the item's id or starts with the item's check prefix, in the precedence unresolved or failed, then skipped, out of scope, checked, with per-part expectations read from rows scoped to the parts (C-11). It filed the pre-run's `coverage.prerun.*` rows under the close-out item, whose prefix is `coverage.` (RR-B8), and was a second reader. The checklist's `bucket_of` (misses R-5). Findings never count as coverage (F18): a review that closed an item by raising its finding would read "not reported".

**Review issues**: CL-08, C-11, F18, RR-B8.

### D7. Keys and sets

**Default.**
- Key ids are validated against the package only when the package exists. A key whose package is not built (today `rms-part`) loads with every other check, is marked key-only ("ids unchecked", naming the missing package), and is never scored in a run (CL-16, F7).
- Two entries overlap when their checks overlap (equal, or one a `.*` family containing the other, nested wildcards included) and their part sets or document sets intersect (F6), and, where both name references, they share a covered joint, pair, fit or stack (round 3, RR3-6; §8.12). The one test covers defect and condition, defect and defect, and forbidden entry against defect; a forbidden entry may overlap a correct condition (spec US3-1; corrected in round 1, RR-A14).
- Forbidden entries match like FR-001 (exact, or a `.*` family) and FR-003 (parts or documents), and may list several families (US3 AS1's "modelling-practice or hygiene"). Validation refuses a forbidden entry that overlaps a known defect. A finding counts as at most one false alarm, however many forbidden entries and correct conditions it hits (CL-09).
- Also refused at load (C-19): a known defect with no producer (SP-12) or no expected status (13A); a graded item with no held-out or tuned-on tag (15A); an expected-coverage item that is not in the checklist; an unanswerable request whose ids do not resolve, where the package is present. Scripted answers are not in the key: the run checks its case file before any review (FR-022; RR-A22).
- Runs never read keys. Held out is recorded once, on each graded item in its key (15A). The set's package `held_out` flag is derived from the keys and checked against them at scoring and compare, and `benchmark run`'s any-held-out refusal (`cli.py:2226-2245`) reads only that flag; the floor and approved-size refusals are at scoring and compare (revised in round 1, RR-A12, RR-B7; §8.7).
- A set entry may name its own key location, so private keys live beside private packages outside the repository. The scorer reads each key from the path its set names (today one `--answer-keys` directory, `cli.py:2367-2379`; `benchmark/scorecard.py:477-490`). The key-isolation guard, which today recognises only the `benchmarks/answer_keys` segment (`ir/loader.py:23, 46-62`), recognises every key and case-file path a set names (FR-022, FR-030). A set file that names an absolute path is never committed (F13, C-7).
- Scripted answers live in a case file of their own, apart from the key and never in a package folder; only the benchmark's answer driver reads it (C-6; the separate file since round 1, RR-A22).

**Why.** The runner never opens a key (`benchmark/runner.py:3-8`; `benchmark/answer_key.py:3-5`), which keeps keys withheld from the agent (constitution, Development Workflow). A held-out count recorded in the set file would be a second record of a number the keys own; a flag derived from the keys and checked against them is not (§8.7).

**Alternatives considered.** Validate ids always: refuses rms-part. Count held-out defects at run time from a count recorded in the set (CL-10), written by the generator (F12) or by a set-summary command (C-6): a second writer that can drift. One key directory, as today: cannot mix committed and private cases.

**Consequence.** A set too small for a study is refused only at scoring or compare, after the paid runs. The run-time any-held-out refusal still fires early, and the cost gate (14A) sizes the set before any paid study.

**Review issues**: CL-16, F7, F6, CL-09, C-19, F12, F13, C-6, C-7 (in part), CL-10 (superseded where it reads at run time), RR-A12, RR-A14, RR-A22, RR3-6.

### D8. Reachability

**Default** (revised in round 1, RR-A0, RR-A5). Two offline checks per committed case, besides the oracle and null reviews, in a validation over the committed set that is separate from the generator, which imports no check logic (FR-054; D14.1; §8.6):
- **(a) Code only.** With no model, the pre-run's calls (`prerun.py:705`, `planned_calls`) and every other check with no required parameter detect (credit or flag) exactly the case's code-produced defects and none of its model-produced ones, and raise no demonstrated or suspected finding matching neither a known defect nor a forbidden entry; one contradicting a correct condition fails the case (round 4, as §8.6). This verifies each producer tag (16A) and that the fictional package is clean apart from its planted defects (CL-01).
- **(b) Correct reviewer.** A scripted review that uses the real tools the way a correct reviewer would (the fit check with the right references, the drawing finding on the drawing, the fastener joint check on the withheld pair) detects every model-produced defect. This catches a key no real finding can match, which the literal oracle cannot (S8).
- `cover-blind-tap` is regenerated so each defect is reachable (FR-010): D-1 and D-2 expected unresolved; D-2 keyed to its drawing document, since the drawing tool records a value missing from a drawing as unresolved and cannot write demonstrated (`tools/session.py:456-475`; `tools/golden.py:22-39`); D-1 given the hole and fastener rows it lacks (and reachable only once 19A puts references on unresolved results, §8.12), its producer decided by 16A, and its callout seeded as a drill depth with no thread depth, so unresolved is the one correct status and D-2's premise holds (d1-gold-ambiguous, §2). Every item is tuned-on, and it is never in the pane study's set (prompt-encodes-cbt-answer, §2).
- After the pilot, each held-out defect the pilot never detected is listed with the findings raised on its parts or documents under other checks (S8; FR-055), so the owner can tell a hard defect from an unmatchable key.

**Why.** The audit's central finding was a key no reviewer could match (§2). The literal oracle feeds the key's own check ids back and cannot see that. A generated defect found by the model but reported under a sibling check would sit at 0 of R in both arms, add no variance, look hard, and quietly shrink the set below its floor (S8).

**Alternatives considered.** The literal oracle only. Credit evidence requests as flagged (F2), not taken under 13A. Keep cover-blind-tap hand-built and exempt from regeneration (C-18): its D-1 stays unreachable (0 holes, 0 fasteners). Run both checks inside the generator (D8 as first taken): the generator would then import check, tool and agent code, against FR-027 (RR-A0).

**Review issues**: S8, CL-01, CL-02, F2, C-18 (in part), RR-A0, RR-A5.

### D9. Origins

**Default.** Every scored finding has exactly one of seven origins:
1. **pre-run code**: the checks-first calls made before the first turn, marked on the step when it is recorded (SP-2);
2. **code re-run after an answer** (regrade): the restated RMS, hygiene and drawing calls after the part-roles answer (`agent/runner.py:917, 1227-1263`, call ids `restated_*`), marked the same way;
3. **a check the model called**, of one of two kinds (spec Terms; round 3, RR3-3): **code-only** when its tool is one the pre-run calls or a check with no required parameter, whatever arguments the model passed; **configured** otherwise, today the four argument-taking check tools, whose findings cite no step (SP-3; §8.5);
4. **the model**: a finding the model wrote itself (the drawing finding, source model: `findings.py:53, 146`);
5. **carried from an earlier review** (`findings.py:129`);
6. **set-up**: what code writes while preparing every review, whatever the levers, such as provenance discrepancies (`agent/runner.py:401-414`);
7. **unknown**: a session without the session-level record that it carries origin marks, which every new session writes and never omits (revised in round 1, RR-B10; §8.5). No step carries a mark today (`report/session.py:55`). An off-arm session with the record and no pre-run steps is not unknown. Never guessed from event order. A pane study refuses a session without the record, naming it and its re-run (FR-007, US1-12; round 4).

Origin is derived from the finding's source, its carried-over reference, the one new step mark and the tool on the step it cites, and is not stored as a second field (C-17). False alarms are split by origin, and origin 3 by kind.

**Why.** SP-2 left restated calls unmarked because they never happened in an unattended run; scripted answers (9A) make them happen, and without their own value the regrade's findings would read as model-called (F8). "Pre-run" and "set-up" read the same to an engineer unless defined (CL-27). The seventh value keeps Principle I: a missing mark reads unknown, never inferred. Origin 3's two kinds differ in who chose the check's inputs, not in who called it, which is what 17A needs (RR3-3).

**Alternatives considered.** Three buckets (the text first approved); five (SP-1, 11A); six (F8, without unknown as a value). The model's code-only calls folded into pre-run code: one origin for every code-only finding, but FR-059 shows the model's code-only calls beside the pre-run's, which needs them apart.

**Review issues**: F8, CL-27, C-17 (in part), RR3-3. Changes 11A's "five origin buckets" to seven.

### D10. Cost

**Default.** A committed, dated price table per model, covering every model the eval may call, with input, cached input and output prices and the source and date beside each. A cost is derived for each completed package from its recorded usage and the responding model's prices, following each provider's nesting (OpenAI reasoning is part of output; Gemini thoughts and tool-use prompt tokens are separate addends: `agent/providers/__init__.py:149-171`). A model with no price gives cost *unknown*, never 0, and names the model. Cached input is priced separately only where the provider's containment of cached tokens in input tokens is verified: verified for Gemini, unverified for OpenAI until probe L1 (`agent/providers/__init__.py:233-247`); where unverified, input is priced as uncached and the cost says so. Consistent replacements taken (C-13): the scripted provider's runs and the oracle and null reviews are marked synthetic and not priced; a completed package with missing usage, including a session recorded before this feature, reads usage and cost unknown, never a scoring failure, so re-scoring needs only the saved session (spec US1-11). Compare does not refuse runs whose cost is unknown: cost is a side column (corrected in round 1, RR-A15).

**Why.** No price table exists (`benchmark/replay.py` prices tokens only). Principle I: an unknown is shown as unknown. Feature 008 reported cost in tokens because the cached price was unknown (`specs/008-checks-first-review/spec.md:199`).

**Alternatives considered.** Tokens only (feature 008). A cost range with cached input priced at full and at the cached price until L1 (F15). Fail scoring when the responding model has no price, or when usage is missing (CL-25, C-13): superseded by "unknown, never 0".

**Review issues**: CL-25, C-13, F15.

**Open (to resolve in plan.md).** Whether cache-write tokens, recorded but never priced (no-cost-cache-blind-gate, §2), get a price column. Cost is a side column: the pane study has no cost threshold (D1).

### D11. Transcripts

**Default.** Each run records the system prompt and the opening message it sent (every later user message is an answer turn, which the recording already holds), so the report's transcript is complete: the system prompt and opening message as sent, every tool call and its full result, the model's text, and every answer turn. Runs recorded before this show "not recorded".

**Why.** The system prompt is built in memory and never saved (`agent/runner.py:1756-1771`); no event carries it (`agent/providers/__init__.py:101-118`); the opening message carries the pre-run digest (`prerun.py:42-45`). Rebuilding them from code at report time is wrong for a run made at another commit, and the report must build offline from run folders alone.

**Alternatives considered.** Rebuild from commit, package and settings (the audit found the prompt deterministic, trajectories-mostly-saved): needs that commit checked out.

**Review issues**: CL-26, F16. The report reads turns and answer batches with the recording reader's parsing, not its replay preconditions: a run folder holds no `package.json`, so the report finds each package through the saved set, and a repetition that failed before its first round has no usage event (FR-034; round 4; §7, §8.13).

### D12. The profile path in findings

**Default.** Standards findings stamp the profile's digest and file name, not its folder. This is the profile half of FR-051; the saved set is the path half (round 1, RR-B0; §8.8). The committed ledger renders only studies made with the fictional profile over committed cases; private-slice studies, report pages built from them, sets naming a private package, private keys, case files and scripts stay outside the repository (C-7).

**Why.** Today every standards finding stamps `profile:<path> sha256:<hash>` with the full local path (`checks/standards/report.py:92, 258`; `checks/standards/profile.py:626`), and the default studies folder is inside the repository (`benchmark/compare.py:89`). D12 as first taken said this change alone let study folders be committed; the saved set's absolute package paths carry the checkout's location too (RR-B0; §8.8).

**Consequence.** A product change to the standards findings' coverage limits: golden fixtures that pin the text change in the same commit. The provenance-against-findings digest cross-check (§4) still works on the sha256. What counts as a local absolute path in a committable folder is defined in §8.8 (round 3, RR3-5): the builders' fictional roots are not local paths.

**Alternatives considered.** Keep study folders outside the repository (the spec as drafted) and move the default studies folder out (C-7): the committed ledger loses its rows. Scrub folders before commit: a second writer of the scrubbing rule. Run with the profile at a neutral relative path (a parity-plan risk note): fragile.

**Review issues**: C-7 (in part), RR-B0, RR3-5.

### D13. The identifier guard

**Default.** The generator builds only from the fictional builders (the fictional root and vocabulary, `tests/support/mechanical.py:104, 113`) and fails on the fictional-string guards it already has (`fictional_offences`, `:173`), on every machine. The repository's identifier tests (`reviewer/tests/unit/test_tracked_files_carry_no_recorded_number.py` and the fixture fictionality tests) run over the committed output where the owner's lists exist; where they are absent they report skipped, never passed (FR-048, SC-011).

**Why.** The list-based guard is a test over tracked files that skips when the lists are absent, which is CI and every other machine (`test_tracked_files_carry_no_recorded_number.py:72, 176, 196`; the lists' location, `reviewer/tests/support/scramble.py:556-560`). It cannot fail a generation, and on most machines "passes" would mean "was not run" (C-8).

**Alternatives considered.** Generation fails on a denylist hit (the spec as drafted, CL-24's variant with a "skipped" message). The generator runs the denylist scan itself and reports "not run" when the list is absent (C-8).

**Review issues**: CL-24, C-8, F14.

### D14. Other defaults

- **D14.1 Reuse.** The generator builds packages with the builders the golden and fixture generators already use (§7), plus one fictional builder of ingested drawing-sheet dimensions (revised in round 2, RR2-4; §8.14): while native drawing dimensions are unvalidated, the fit, stack and toleranced alignment checks read only ingested sheets, and no existing builder writes one. It adds no other; if it ships under `src/`, the builders move out of `tests/`. It imports no check, tool or agent logic, and a structural test asserts it, which makes "no key entry is derived from check output" testable (C-17, C-18, S12). Allowed: the IR and standards-profile data models and the fictional builders (§8.9). The reachability checks and the golden scorecards run in the separate validation (§8.6; revised in round 1, RR-A0). Alternative (RR2-4's other option): no new builder; fits, stacks and toleranced coaxiality then cannot be model-produced until `DRAWING_BINDING_VALIDATED` is set, and 16A's family weighting goes back to the owner.
- **D14.2 Scorer version.** The version bumps on any change to matching or scoring semantics. The validation writes and checks the golden scorecards: the oracle and the null review of each committed case, and golden probes, one per scored column and per matching rule over committed cases, each scoring exactly its behaviour (FR-049; the generator cannot, §8.6; §8.15). A test fails when a golden changes and the version does not, and when the version changes and no golden does (C-16). Compare refuses any other version, lower or higher, before full validation (§4). **Revised** (round 3, RR3-2): probes added, since the oracle and null alone move no golden on most counting rules, so a correct bump would fail the reverse check (§8.15, which also holds the alternative).
- **D14.3 Answer-turn bound.** 3 answer turns by default, recorded in provenance; compare refuses runs of one study whose bounds differ (CL-23). **Later**: a review that reaches the bound is completed (D16).
- **D14.4 Withdrawn forbidden hits.** A forbidden finding shown and later withdrawn, by an answer or by the review, is counted in its own column; it is not a false alarm (S11).
- **D14.5 Providers.** The first pane study runs on OpenAI, the production default (`agent/settings.py:97-106`). A Gemini study records parallel tool calls and dropped reasoning as inert (spec FR-052): the pane's settings record parallel calls off on Gemini (`agent/settings.py:502-523`), Gemini makes parallel calls regardless (`:506-508`), and dropped reasoning is inert there (`:482-488`); a Gemini study therefore compares four of the six pane settings (F23; citations corrected in round 1).
- **D14.6 Tokenizer.** Offline runs need the o200k vocabulary, fetched once per machine with `swreview tokenizer fetch` (`tokens.py:8-11`). **Revised** (round 1, RR-B16): a missing vocabulary makes token counts unknown, never 0, as the product's contract says (`tools/registry.py:646-657`); nothing refuses for it. A named refusal was the first reading (C-21), now the alternative.
- **D14.7 Representative designs.** **Revised** (round 1, RR-A17): the constitution's acceptance suite (`.specify/memory/constitution.md:213`) is the committed set's at least 8 generated designs plus the real slice (the small and the big assembly): at least 10 designs with known defects and correct designs, keys withheld from the agent, some items held out. The plan's Constitution Check records how representative they are. Alternatives: the first reading, the real slice plus the synthetic big and small assemblies (2 + 2 = 4, below the constitution's 5); count real designs only and amend the workflow line, a MINOR version bump (C-10). **Refined by RF-7 and RF-47** (§9.9, §9.10): the 10 committed designs are the suite; the private slice is 23A's own study, shown and never deciding, so it is not counted in it, and the spec's Assumptions line (`spec.md:428`) now says so (§9.11).
- **D14.8 Definitions** (CL-21, C-14). The spec's Terms hold them; round 1 added *detected*, *missed*, *regression guard*, *completed repetition* and *clean twin*, round 2 *identical arms* and *drop shapes* (RR2-2, RR2-6), and round 3 *code-only call* and *configured check* (RR3-3), the first classed by tool since round 4 (§8.5).
