Three readings sit behind them: on a case with only correct conditions the oracle and the null both read recall not computable (SC-001); the noise floor is simulated on the study's own off-arm rates and co-movement, 2.8 standard errors only approximating it (§6); a judgement family is a check family, not a producer (16A), 16A's four model-produced kinds as the families being the alternative not taken (RR-A3, RR-B15).
- **D14.9 Stale facts** (CL-18, C-20, F22). The branch is cut from `main` at `be01974`, which already holds the concurrent branch that changed the handoff export (its tip `4e6b02c`). The handoff export's provenance summary is a key allowlist (`handoff.py:425`): the new provenance fields (model view, profile digest, pane-defaults flag, live tools present) are added to it, never the profile path.
- **D14.10 Complexity Tracking** (C-22). The plan's Complexity Tracking lists, each with its simpler alternative and why it was rejected: the paired verdict and its simulation (against the worst-case rule); seven origins (against three); scorer versioning (against always re-scoring); the scripted-answer driver (against single-turn cases); the report page (against the ledger); the generator (against hand-built golden packages).

### D15. False alarms that decide (rounds 1 and 3; now 17A)

**Now owner decision 17A** (§1): the deciding false-alarm figure counts only false alarms of model origin: findings the model wrote, and findings of configured checks, the checks with arguments the model set up (D9 origin 3). **Kept here as defaults**: a code-only call's false alarms are code origin whoever made the call, the pre-run's and the model's own calls of a tool the pre-run calls or of a check with no required parameter (spec Terms; RR3-3), which is how the Terms read 17A's "checks the model chose to call". They are shown per arm, the model's code-only calls' beside the pre-run's, with a paired interval named when it excludes zero, and never decide; so are the regrade's, set-up's and carried findings'. Tuned-on items (15A) never decide through the paired rules: a hit on a tuned-on forbidden entry or correct condition is left out of the deciding figure and shown as a regression guard, with its interval, and so is recall on tuned-on model-produced defects. A tuned-on code-produced defect decides only through the code guard (D17), as every code-produced defect does.

**Why.** 17A's (§1). R-8's per-document `hygiene.revision_present` findings come from `check_hygiene`, which the pre-run always runs (`tools/checks_mechanical.py:76`; `prerun.py:740-742`). **Round 3.** D15 as first taken still counted every check the model called as model origin. In the all-off arm the model calls `check_hygiene`, `check_joints` and the RMS tools itself when it follows the checklist; in the on arm the pre-run makes those calls and lever 13 withholds the tools from the model (`agent/settings.py:475, 518`). A held-out forbidden entry hit by argument-free code would then add a deciding false alarm to the off arm only, in proportion to its compliance: the audit's pattern again, biased towards "no loss", and against the identical-arms null, whose model-origin false-alarm rates are equal. 16A already treats whatever an argument-free check finds as code (RR3-3). Tuned-on items are what the prompt was written around; letting them decide would score the tuning.

**Alternatives considered.** 17A's (§1), and: every check the model calls is model origin, and FR-054(a) fails any case in which argument-free code raises a demonstrated or suspected finding on a held-out item, so such false alarms land only on tuned-on items (RR3-3's other option): it constrains every generated case, and on the anchors R-8's hygiene hits would still split by who called the check.

**Review issues**: RR-A2, RR-B6, RR3-3. Revises D1 rule 2; the owner's since 17A.

### D16. The answer-turn bound (rounds 1 and 3)

**Default.** A review that reaches the answer-turn bound is completed: every turn ended normally, so it is graded from its final state and counted as bound-reached, a shown-only column (FR-025, FR-059). It is not a behavioural stop; those are the step and output cut-offs, provider stops on either provider, and the wall-clock limit reached while the stream is still active (D2). A behavioural stop that leaves a defect with no completed repetition in an arm drops it from the main figure and counts against the floor like an infrastructure failure, while the sensitivity figure keeps the stopped runs and can still call a loss (FR-058; round 3, RR3-1).

**Why.** Leaving bound-reached reviews out biases the main figure against the arm that asks more, and the sensitivity figure would count their correctly found defects as missed (RR-B19). A cut-off turn has no graded final state; a bound-reached review does.

**Alternatives considered.** The bound as a behavioural stop (D2 as first taken). Grade it, and also count it as stopped in the sensitivity figure.

**Review issues**: RR-B19, RR-A9, RR-B13, RR3-1. Revises D2 and D14.3.

### D17. Guards, precedence and the 5% bounds (rounds 1 to 3)

**Default.**
- The pane study has no control-stability rule.
- **Code guard**: a code-produced defect, held out or tuned-on, is a loss when every completed off-run detected it and some completed on-run did not. Off-runs that disagree keep the guard from firing on that defect, which is named.
- **Cleared guard**: a defect cleared on some on-run and on no off-run is named; clears before a behavioural stop count. Whether it decides, for items not tuned-on, is set by the simulation, so that SC-005's two bounds hold (§8.1).
- **The 5% bounds**: the whole verdict, every deciding rule together with the sensitivity figure, calls identical arms a loss at most 5% of the time, measured by the simulation (SC-010) and not asserted per rule. Identical arms are as the spec's Terms define them (round 2, RR2-2), their equal false-alarm rates being model-origin ones since round 3 (RR3-3). The second bound (round 2, RR2-7): at a true 10-point drop the verdict reads "no 10-point loss" at most 5% of the time. Both are upper 95% Monte Carlo bounds; if either exceeds 5%, the plan tightens the levels until it does not. The deciding rules and levels are fixed at the cost gate. These two readings of 14A await the owner's confirmation (spec refinements).
- **Precedence**: any deciding rule's loss makes the verdict a quality loss; nothing reads first, the floor included. Drops below the floor rule out only "no 10-point loss", which then reads inconclusive, naming the count (FR-058; round 3, RR3-1; §8.10).

**Why.** Read first, control stability returned inconclusive whenever the off-runs disagreed on a code-produced defect; the off arm has no pre-run, so a real model-judgement loss would often read inconclusive (RR-A1): with 3 independent call events at 95% compliance an unstable control has probability 0.37, 0.60 and 0.79 at 3, 6 and 10 repetitions (RR-B3). The second draft's two precedences went with it. D1's per-rule 2.5% does not hold for a percentile bootstrap at the floor's package count (RR-B4), and the guards and the sensitivity figure are further tests (RR-A7), so only a simulation of the whole verdict bounds 14A's rate. **Round 2.** Under the literal null the code guard alone breaks 5%: one code-produced defect the off arm detects on 95% of runs is called lost with probability 0.95^6 × (1 - 0.95^6) ≈ 0.19 at 6 repetitions, and ten give 0.73, 0.89 and 0.94 at 3, 6 and 10 (RR2-2); but the on arm's pre-run runs every argument-free check on every run, and FR-054(a) proves they detect every code-produced defect. "No 10-point loss", the outcome that keeps the pane defaults, had no bounded error; an undercovering lower tail (RR-B4's 3.8% against 2.5%) would pass unnoticed (RR2-7). **Round 3.** Below the floor, round 2 read inconclusive outright: a lever that stops the on arm on every repetition of a few packages drops their defects below 34 while the sensitivity figure, which keeps those runs, calls a loss, exactly D2's case (RR3-1).

**Alternatives considered.** Control stability read first (D1 as first taken). Below the floor, inconclusive whatever the rules say (FR-058 as round 2 left it). An unstable control gives inconclusive unless a code-produced defect was lost on a stable part of it (RR-B3(b)). The cleared guard on code-produced defects only, model-produced clears as a third paired rule inside the budget (RR-B3(a)). Each paired rule's simulated rate held at or below 2.5% separately (RR-B4). The literal null, with the code guard deciding only where the simulation allows, as the cleared guard does (RR2-2's second option): at the off arm's likely rates the code guard would seldom decide. "No 10-point loss" left unbounded (round 1).

**Review issues**: RR-A1, RR-A7, RR-A10, RR-B3, RR-B4, RR-B5; RR2-2, RR2-5, RR2-7; RR3-1. Revises D1 rules 3 and 4 and the bound.

## §6 Statistics and sizing

### The statistic

For each held-out model-produced defect *i*, let *p_i* be its detection rate in an arm, taken over that arm's completed repetitions (D1, D2). The verdict's statistic is the mean over defects of *d_i* = *p_i*(on) - *p_i*(off). A single number such as pooled recall (matched over matched plus missed, `benchmark/scorecard.py:431-435`) equals this mean only when every defect has the same number of completed repetitions, so the verdict uses the per-defect mean. With R repetitions each rate is a multiple of 1/R, so many *d_i* are exactly zero when the arms agree; a zero-width interval is therefore possible and reads not computable (D1).

### The power arithmetic

A 95% interval with half-width *h* has a standard error of *h*/1.96. At *h* = 10 points the standard error is 5.1 points, and a true 10-point drop sits exactly 1.96 standard errors from zero: on the boundary. A rule that calls a loss when the interval's upper end is below zero (2.5% one-sided, the starting level of §8.1) then detects it about 50% of the time; a 5% one-sided rule about 62%.

Detecting a drop 80% of the time needs the drop to sit at z(0.975) + z(0.80) = 1.96 + 0.84 = 2.80 standard errors. For a 10-point drop the standard error must be at most 10/2.80 = 3.6 points, a 95% half-width of about ±7 points. That is why the first draft's SC-005 (±10) was replaced (14A), and why 2.8 standard errors approximates the noise floor, which is found by simulation (D14.8).

### Where 34 × 3 came from, and when it holds

Decision 10A's figure came from the audit's noise-floor arithmetic for a single arm's recall: near p = 0.5 the 95% half-width of a proportion over n·R defect-trials is about 1.96 × 0.5/√(n·R) ≈ 1/√(n·R), so ±10 points needs n·R ≥ 100, which is 34 defects at 3 repetitions. That was the first draft's SC-005 bar, not SC-010's: the verdict compares two arms, and needs the 2.8-standard-error margin above.

The same 34 follows from a paired t-test at 80% power and two-sided 5% if the per-defect difference has a standard deviation of 0.20: n = ((2.035 + 0.853) × 0.20/0.10)² = 33.4. With identical arms and R repetitions, Var(*d_i*) = 2*p_i*(1 - *p_i*)/R. At R = 3, a standard deviation of 0.20 needs the average *p*(1 - *p*) to be about 0.06. A defect found half the time contributes 0.25; a defect found on every run or on none contributes 0. So 34 × 3 holds only if about three defects in four are found on every run or on none.

Such polarised defects narrow the interval but say almost nothing about a drop. A defect found 0 of 3 times in both arms cannot drop, so the whole 10 points must come from the others; and when a lever turns an always-found defect into a sometimes-found one, its variance jumps from zero, so power under the alternative is lower than the null variance suggests (S1).

### What the review's simulations found

Three reviewers simulated offline; their tests and drop shapes differ, their conclusion does not: at 34 × 3, 80% power needs strongly polarised detection rates.

S1: a paired t-interval, loss when the upper end of the 95% interval lies below zero, the drop proportional to each defect's off-arm rate, 2,500 to 4,000 simulations per cell:

| Off-arm detection rates | 95% half-width at 34 × 3 | Power at 34 × 3 | Defects for 80% at R = 3 | Repetitions for 80% at 34 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Every rate 0.5 | ±14.2 | 29% | about 134 | about 13 |
| Uniform, 0 to 1 | ±11.5 | 36% | about 100 | about 10 |
| U-shaped, Beta(0.5, 0.5) | ±10.0 | 44% | about 78 | about 8 |
| 70% always or never, 30% at 0.5 | ±7.6 | 57% | about 62 | about 6 |

With a 5% one-sided rule the powers are 41, 51, 56 and 69%, and the defects needed at R = 3 are 102, 78, 62 and 46.

CL-13: a paired one-sided test, the drop spread over the defects with room to drop. Power at 34 × 3: 79% with Beta(0.2, 0.2) rates, 71% with Beta(0.3, 0.3), 64% with Beta(0.5, 0.5), 55% uniform, 42% with every rate at 0.5. Uniform rates need about 70 defects at R = 3; 5 repetitions at 34 give 74%. Identical arms were called a loss about 5% of the time in every case.

C-1: one-sided 5%, the on arm 10 points lower on every defect. Power about 40% with every rate at 0.5 and about 53% with uniform rates; 80% needs about 80 (uniform) to 100 (0.5) defects at R = 3, or 6 to 9 repetitions at 34. At 34 × 3 the drop detectable 80% of the time is about 14 to 17 points.

Two effects the simple arithmetic misses (S1):
- **Package co-movement.** With about 4 defects per package run sharing a per-run shock (a logit standard deviation of 1.0), a per-defect interval calls identical arms a loss 9.3% of the time against a nominal 5% (11.3% at 1.5). An interval that resamples whole packages holds 5% in S1's set-up (see the round-1 per-tail check below), but power at 34 × 3 falls to 25%. D1 takes packages as the unit, and the per-package cap (FR-028) limits the co-movement.
- **Drop shape.** The same 10 points concentrated in a fifth of the defects cut power from 36% to 27% (off-arm rates uniform between 0.5 and 1).

The re-review's offline checks (round 1):
- **Per-rule rate.** A percentile bootstrap over whole packages, 34 defects in 17 packages of 2, called identical arms a loss 3.3 to 5.1% of the time per rule, not 2.5% (RR-B4). §8.1 starts from a t interval or a studentised bootstrap and checks only the whole verdict.
- **Cleared guard.** With 10 model-produced defects each cleared on 5% of runs, identical arms are called a loss with probability 0.73, 0.89 and 0.94 at 3, 6 and 10 repetitions (RR-B3); whether the guard decides is set by the simulation (D17).
- **Co-movement.** With a logit standard deviation of 1.0, power at 6 repetitions with every rate at 0.5 was 36% (RR-B5).

### Sizing under 14A

1. **The floor.** FR-028's floor and caps (S5): at 34 defects, at most 6 per design and 2 per package.
2. **The pilot.** Before the paid pane study, a small paid pilot runs the off arm (all levers off) on the committed set. It measures each held-out model-produced defect's detection rate and how the defects of one package run move together (for example the variance of a shared per-run effect, or the correlation of detections within a package run). It also yields time, cost, the off arm's detection of code-produced defects, and behavioural-stop, clearance and false-alarm rates, which the cost gate and D17 need (FR-055).
3. **The simulation's inputs** (SC-010): §8.2 lists them (revised in round 1, RR-B5).
4. **The stopping rule.** Repetitions grow first, then cases, until the recall rule on the main figure detects a true 10-point drop at least 80% of the time under both drop shapes, and SC-005's two 5% bounds hold (D17). Repetitions first because they shrink the within-defect binomial term, p(1 - p)/R, directly and cost only money, while each new case needs a spec, a key and the reachability checks of D8. A per-run package shock averages over repetitions too, and only the drop's spread across defects, small at 10 points, is irreducible, so power keeps rising with R (round 4, RR4-m15): repetitions stop at FR-055's cap of 10 (22A), then cases grow. With every rate at 0.5 and a shock SD of 1.0, 80% needs about 16 repetitions (RR4-2's check; §8.2), so those scenarios grow cases (SC-010). The drop shape can decide the size: at 34 × 3 with uniform rates, S1's proportional drop gave 36% power and CL-13's spread drop 55%, under different tests and levels; the lower of the two governs (spec Terms; RR2-6).
5. **The cost gate.** The owner approves the resulting size and cost before the paid study. If detection rates are spread, the review's figures point to about 6 to 10 repetitions at 34 defects; strong co-movement can take more cases instead.
6. **With the verdict.** The simulation is re-run on the study's own off-arm rates, and its noise floor is printed beside the verdict (D14.8).

### Open

Moved to §8 in round 1 (§8.1, §8.2, §8.13). The two 5% bounds are settled by D17, the repetition cap by FR-055. The sizing targets model-judgement recall only; the false-alarm rule has no power target.

## §8 For the plan

Round 1 of 2026-09-29 moved every mechanism out of the spec, which states what and why, into this section, as the plan's starting design. Each entry names the spec requirement it serves and the outcome that requirement fixes. The plan may change a design here when the outcome still holds, and records why. Points marked **to resolve in plan.md** are open. Rounds 2 to 4 revised it as §5's disposition tables say; round 2 added 8.14, round 3 8.15.

### 8.1 The verdict's statistics (FR-044, FR-045, SC-005, SC-010; D1, D15, D17; 17A)

- **Interval.** Its unit is the package, resampled with a recorded seed or analytic (FR-044). Starting design: a t interval on package-level differences with (packages - 1) degrees of freedom, analytic; alternative, a studentised bootstrap over packages. Neither is taken as calibrated: RR4-m16's offline check put the t interval at about 3.0% per tail on identical arms at 3 repetitions and a shock SD of 1.0, and the level search below absorbs that. Not the plain percentile bootstrap, at 3.3 to 5.1% per tail in the re-review's simulation and about 3.8% analytically (t with 16 degrees of freedom against z, and the square-root-of-(n-1)/n shrinkage; RR-B4, §6). Any resample count and seed are recorded with the verdict, as is the simulation's seed (FR-046). SC-010 runs 39 reference scenarios at 4,000 simulations each, through the size search, with four intervals per simulated study; a studentised bootstrap multiplies that by its inner resamples, one more reason the t interval is the starting design.
- **Levels.** Start each of the four paired tests (recall and false alarms, each on the main and the sensitivity figure) at 2.5% one-sided. "No 10-point loss" reads the lower end of the recall rule's own interval, its other tail at the same level, so both tails tighten together (FR-044; round 2, RR2-7). Simulate the whole verdict, drops and the floor applied as 8.10 orders them, on identical arms, and at a true 10-point drop under each drop shape (8.2). If the upper 95% Monte Carlo bound of either rate (the loss rate on identical arms, or the "no 10-point loss" rate at the drop, the higher of the two shapes) exceeds 5%, tighten the paired rules' levels equally until neither does. Tightening helps both bounds and costs power, which repetitions, then cases, restore (SC-005). At a true drop of exactly 10 points a calibrated lower tail at level α reads "no 10-point loss" at most about α of the time, so the second bound checks the lower tail's calibration under the alternative's own variance, which the drop shape and co-movement widen (§6). No per-rule rate is asserted (SC-010).
- **The cleared guard.** Simulated at the pilot's clearance rates in both arms. Starting design: it decides only when, with it deciding, levels exist under which SC-005's two bounds hold and the recall rule's power at the approved size stays at 80% or more; otherwise it is named only. Not assumed: with 10 model-produced defects each cleared on 5% of runs, identical arms are called a loss with probability 0.73, 0.89 and 0.94 at 3, 6 and 10 repetitions, from (1 - (1 - q)^R)(1 - q)^R per defect (RR-B3). Checks the model sets up pass with checked_within_scope (`checks/fit.py:112`), so model-produced clears are stochastic; code-produced clears come from the pre-run and are close to deterministic. SC-010's clearance scenario is that case (RR2-5). The guard has no level of its own, so tightening the paired levels cannot offset it: the simulator is expected to drop it and print so, and the SC-010 test checks the printed choice and the guard's simulated rate against that closed form. Alternative the plan may put to the owner: the cleared guard on code-produced defects only, with model-produced clears as a third paired rule inside the budget (RR-B3(a)).
- **The code guard.** Under identical arms as the spec's Terms define them (round 2, RR2-2), the on arm detects every code-produced defect on every run: the pre-run runs every argument-free check on every run, and the validation's code-only check (FR-054) proves those checks detect every code-produced defect. The simulation models it so, less what the part-roles regrade withdraws, and the guard then adds nothing to the bounded rate. The literal case, both arms at the off arm's pilot rate p, is printed as a stress case and not bounded: with independent detections, 1 - (1 - p^R(1 - p^R))^n over n such defects, which for SC-010's ten defects at p = 0.95 is 0.73, 0.89 and 0.94 at 3, 6 and 10 repetitions. The SC-010 test checks the printed stress case against that closed form, which exercises the guard offline. **To resolve in plan.md**: if the pilot's stress case is large (the off arm rarely calls argument-free checks), whether to put a narrower guard to the owner.
- **Fixed before the paid study.** Which rules decide, and their levels, are chosen from the simulation before the paid runs, recorded with the owner's approval in the pilot measurement, and printed with the verdict. Choosing them after seeing the study's data would void the 5% bounds.

### 8.2 The simulation's inputs (SC-005, SC-010, FR-055)

Every run of the simulator prints each input with its value or source (RR-B5). Before the pilot the inputs are SC-010's reference values, fixed in the test (round 2, RR2-5); after it, the pilot's measurements:
- the off-arm rate distribution: every rate 0.5; uniform on 0 to 1; or three defects in four always or never found, split evenly, the rest at 0.5 (of 34: 13, 13 and 8; S1's table used 70% and 30%, §6), rates placed in packages at random with the recorded seed. After the pilot, its measured rates fitted with shrinkage (a Beta fit) rather than the raw rates;
- package co-movement: a shared per-run logit shock per package, standard deviation σ of 0 or 1.0; after the pilot, its estimate;
- **marginal rates** (round 4, RR4-2): a stated rate, before or after a drop, is the defect's expected detection over the shock, which is what recall averages. Per defect and arm the simulator solves the logit intercept a with E[logistic(a + σZ)] = p, Z standard normal (a root over a fixed quadrature); rates of 0 and 1 mean never and always, whatever the shock; identical arms share each intercept. At σ = 1.0 a drop from 0.5 to 0.4 conditional on a zero shock is a marginal 8.3 points, and 80% power moves from about 16 repetitions to 21 (RR4-2's check);
- the layout: 17 packages of 2 held-out model-produced defects plus 34 clean twins, from 3 repetitions per arm; repetitions grow first, up to the cap of 10 (FR-055), then cases, added as packages of 2 defects and 2 twins drawn from the scenario. After the pilot, the committed set's own layout (at most 2 held-out model-produced defects per package, FR-028);
- identical arms as the spec's Terms define them (8.1);
- the drop: a true fall D = 10 points in mean detection over the n held-out model-produced defects, as the spec's Terms shape it. **Proportional** (S1's rule): defect i falls by D·p_i/p̄, p̄ the mean rate, so a defect at 0 stays there. **Room** (CL-13's rule), as an algorithm: the total n·D is shared equally among the defects with p_i > 0; any defect whose share exceeds its rate falls to 0 and leaves the pool, and what remains of the total is re-shared equally among the rest, until no share exceeds a rate. Power is the recall rule's on the main figure under the lower of the two (spec Terms; RR2-6); the "no 10-point loss" rate is checked under both, the higher governing (8.1);
- model-origin false alarms per package run: negative binomial with mean m of 0.2, 1 or 3 and size k = 0.5 (variance m + m²/k), its gamma drawn per package run, or Poisson; a behavioural stop keeps a binomial thinning of its run's count at a fraction drawn uniform on 0 to 1, the false alarms "up to the stop" (FR-041). After the pilot, fitted to its model-origin false alarms. No recorded session is used: no committed generated package has yet been run (RR2-5);
- behavioural stops, clearances and code-produced defects: none in the 36 main scenarios (three rate distributions, two co-movements, three false-alarm means, two dispersions). SC-010's three further scenarios each add one of them to the uniform co-moving scenario with false-alarm mean 1 and dispersion 0.5: stops on 5% of runs per arm; 10 held-out defects cleared on 5% of runs; 10 code-produced defects the off arm detects on 95% of runs (8.1). After the pilot, its rates;
- the deciding rules and their levels;
- 4,000 simulations per scenario, and the seed. The results checked against 5% are upper 95% Monte Carlo bounds: at 4,000 simulations an observed rate of about 4.4% or less passes.

Co-movement costs power (RR-B5's check, §6), and the cost gate should see it.

### 8.3 Question matching (FR-019, FR-020, FR-053, FR-060; D4)

Starting design: the product's own covering rule (`report/session.py:184-198`; `specs/013-engineer-first-review/contracts/re-ask-guard.md` section 3), extended only where an anchor needs it. Each column counts every call that matches it, refused calls included.
- **Ids.** A request carries a checklist item (`blocks`, optional) and entity ids. For re-asks, repeats and scripted answers, hole and fastener ids resolve to the component instance that owns them and other ids stay as they are, so two instances of one pin stay two; the product compares ids raw, so this widens it only by reading a hole or fastener as its part. For unanswerable-request entries, which name documents, a request's ids resolve to documents as the product's drawing guard resolves them (`tools/session.py:280-298`: a component names its document, a hole or fastener its component's).
- **Re-ask**: a later request matches an earlier answered question when the items are equal (no item equals no item) and either the request's resolved ids are non-empty and a subset of the question's, or the request names an item and no ids. The second branch is the extension: the product covers an item-only request only when the earlier one had no ids either, but R-3's "now give numeric limits" names the fit item and no ids and must count. A request with no item and no ids never matches. A different item never matches, whatever the ids.
- **Repeat of an open question**: the same test against a question still open, whether unscripted or scripted and not yet answered; the product refuses it as already asked (`tools/session.py:116-120`). A drawing request naming the drawing item and a document with no attached drawing is closed by code and records nothing (`tools/session.py:112-114, 301-340`), so it never becomes an open question: R-2's repeat arises from a request that names no item.
- **The part-roles question** is written by code, not by a request call, with no item and the unclear documents as its ids (`checks/part_roles.py:881-899`). It is recognised as `answered_roles` recognises it: a code-written request whose question, options and ids equal the spec's (`:902-916`); its key (`:232`) sits on the spec only. It never matches by ids, as the product's code questions cover only themselves.
- **Scripted answers**: an answer matches a request when the items are equal and the request's resolved ids are non-empty and a subset of the answer's; a request naming an item and no ids matches that item's one answer, and is unscripted when the item has several; a request with neither matches none. More than one match: unscripted, the ambiguity named (spec Edge Cases). The run refuses a case file holding two answers on one item (or two with no item) whose resolved ids intersect, since a request naming a shared id would match both (US4-8, FR-022). An item-only request is then the only request that can match two answers (round 3, RR3-4).
- **Unanswerable requests**: an entry matches a request whose document-resolved ids intersect the entry's, recorded or closed by code. An entry names a checklist item only when it should match requests on that item alone; R-2's entry names the pin's document and no item, so a request naming the pin, its document or its holes matches.
- **Question-count rules** (FR-060): a kind is the part-roles question, counted from the session's recorded code questions, or a checklist item with optional ids, counted from the model's calls with the re-ask test, refused ones included.
- **Refused calls** are read from the tool calls, since the guard records nothing for them (`tools/session.py:110-126`), and re-asks are split into refused and seen.
- **Spec US3-6 under this design** holds as the spec states it; R-2's second request counts in two columns, a repeat of an open question and, since FR-019 counts every call, a second unanswerable request, and the test asserts both.
- Alternatives: D4's overlaps (S7, C-12), superseded because subset is the product's own rule; counting each call in one column only, which reads R-2's second request once, against FR-019's "every request call".

### 8.4 Reading coverage (FR-018; D6 as revised)

- Read with the report's own reader: `report/finding_groups.py` `goal_lines` (`:186`) and `goal_of` (`:167`), over the goals of `report/review_words_v1.yaml:131-185`. No second mapping table is written.
- The report's state order (`_goal_line`, `report/finding_groups.py:207-247`): a finding in the goal other than a pass gives `issues`; else one of the goal's own item rows that is unresolved, skipped or failed gives `not_reached` with that reason; else no row and no finding gives `not_reached` with `no_check`; else a checked row or a pass gives `checked`; else another row not reached gives `not_reached`; else `not_applicable`.
- The pre-run's rows (FR-018; round 4, RR4-m4): of its `coverage.prerun.<family>` rows (`prerun.py:333, 352, 831-877, 945, 954, 974, 1030`), only interference and standards are goal items (`report/review_words_v1.yaml:134, 171`); no goal prefix starts `coverage.`, so the others, part roles included, are in no goal and appear on no goal line (the "Other checks" group holds findings only, `report/finding_groups.py:337-352`). That stays: no goal reads them, and no committed key may expect a status only they would give. Not taken: adding them to their goals' `items`, a change to the engineer's page with no owner decision. The pre-run writes fit and axial stack skipped on every checks-first run (`prerun.py:943-960`), fastener joint and hole alignment whenever the joint map misses anything (`:306-356`), so under the state order above (`report/finding_groups.py:233-238`) those goals would read not reached after the model checked a fit, or for one unextracted hole, on the on arm only, biasing the pane study. No anchor needs it.
- An expected-coverage item is read through the goal that holds it. Where one goal holds several items (fits and stacks; provenance and hygiene), the expectation reads the goal's state, and the key format says so.
- Report states to key statuses: `checked` reads checked; `issues` reads checked, since the item was examined; `not_reached` with reason `unresolved` or `failed` reads unresolved; with `skipped`, skipped; with `no_check`, not reported, always a mismatch; `not_applicable` reads out of scope. Alternative: keep `issues` as a status of its own that a key may list.
- R-5 offline (§3): the on arm reads not reached either way (the pre-run's skipped row, `prerun.py:993-995`, or finalisation's unresolved one, `agent/runner.py:647-665`); on the off arm a model that marks interference checked reads checked. Its expectation is "unresolved or skipped", its failure the model's own coverage claim (RR-B8).
- Per-part expectations are not taken: the report reads per goal, and a per-part reading would be a second reader. Unread parts show in the gaps and coverage rows the transcript carries. Alternative: per-part expectations read from rows scoped to the parts, in the report's order.
- Alternatives: D6's, a separate reader (RR-B8) and the checklist's `bucket_of` (`agent/checklist.py:68-76`).

### 8.5 Origins (FR-007; D9 as revised)

- A step mark is written on each pre-run and regrade step when it is recorded (SP-2; `prerun.py:1190, 1201`; the restated calls, `agent/runner.py:917, 1227-1263`).
- A session-level record that the session carries origin marks, for example a step-marks version, is written on every new session and never omitted. The codebase omits false or null fields so that older sessions keep their bytes (`report/session.py:70-71, 605-607`), so the record must be a value that is always written, not a flag dropped when false. Unknown is read only from its absence; an off-arm session with the record and no marked steps reads each code finding other than set-up's and carried ones as a check the model called, of the kind below (RR-B10).
- **Code-only or configured** (FR-007, FR-044, FR-059; D9, D15; round 3, RR3-3). An unmarked code finding is code-only when the tool on the step it cites (`InvestigationStep.tool`, `report/session.py:59`) is one `prerun.planned_calls` can name (the three RMS tools, `check_interference_group`, `checks_mechanical.CODE_FIRST_CHECKS`, `check_drawings` and `check_standards`: `prerun.py:144-156, 734-749`; the pre-run's live `bridge_interference` call when SOLIDWORKS is attached, `:159`) or a check tool with no required parameter, whatever arguments the model passed. It is configured otherwise. The four argument-taking check tools cite no step (SP-3), so an unmarked, uncarried code finding citing no step, outside the provenance family that set-up writes, is configured. A test holds every check tool (`tools/registry.py:120-134`, and the two offered on condition) in exactly one of the two sets, so a new check tool must be classed.
- **Refusal** (FR-007, US1-12; round 4): compare refuses a pane study holding a session without the session-level record, naming the session and the command that re-runs it; lever studies and re-scoring read such sessions as unknown.
- The kind is read by tool, not by exact call: `check_rms_part` and `check_rms_equations` take only an optional document that narrows the same rules, and `check_interference_group` a group key the pre-run enumerates (`prerun.py:144-156`), so the model's call writes the finding the pre-run's call would. Alternative: code-only only when name and arguments equal a call the pre-run plans; a model's `check_rms_part` on one document would then decide, the bias D15 removes.

### 8.6 The validation (FR-054, FR-049, SC-006, SC-014; D8 as revised)

- A test over the committed set, apart from the generator. It may import checks, tools, the agent and the scorer; it never writes a key. A case that fails it fails the test, so it cannot be committed (RR-A0, RR-B1).
- (a) Code only: every call of `prerun.planned_calls` (`prerun.py:705`) and every other check with no required parameter, on the generated package, with the fictional example profile every committed case is keyed against (FR-027) and no model. Passes when the code-produced defects are detected (credited or flagged), no model-produced defect is detected, and every demonstrated or suspected finding matches a known defect or a forbidden entry. One that contradicts a correct condition fails the case: code would be calling a keyed-correct condition wrong, so the key or the package is. `cover-blind-tap`'s D-1, expected unresolved, passes when code flags it (RR-A5), which needs 19A's references on the unresolved path (8.12). A hit on a forbidden entry passes: it is a code-origin false alarm whoever calls the check, so it never decides (D15).
- (b) Correct reviewer: a scripted review (`agent/providers/fake.py`) that calls the real tools as a correct reviewer would (the fit check with the right references, the drawing finding on the drawing, the fastener joint check on the withheld pair). Passes when every model-produced defect is detected.
- The validation also writes and checks the golden scorecards, the oracle's, the null review's and the probes' (FR-049; 8.15). The generator cannot: the scorer imports agent code (`benchmark/scorecard.py:50-51`).

### 8.7 Held-out records (FR-031, FR-032; D7 as revised)

- The set's package-level `held_out` flag (`benchmark/sets.py:27, 59`) is derived: true exactly when the package's key holds at least one held-out item. The generator writes it for committed sets; the owner writes it for private sets. `benchmark score` and `compare` check it against the keys and refuse a mismatch, naming the package (RR-A12, RR-B7).
- Held-out recall reads the item tags only; today's filter by the package flag (`benchmark/scorecard.py:431-435`) goes.
- `benchmark run`'s refusal of a study run whose set holds nothing out (`cli.py:2226-2245`) reads the derived flags only; the run never opens a key. The constitution's "some packages are held out" is met through the derived flag.
- `benchmark score` records the held-out count the keys name before any failure; compare's refusal below the floor or below the approved size reads that count and the pilot measurement. Counts after failures are FR-058's (RR-B13).
- Alternative: drop the flag and move the run-time refusal to scoring; it loses the early refusal before paid runs.

### 8.8 Paths in run folders (FR-051, US2-11; D12 extended)

- Today `benchmark run` saves the loaded set into every run folder (`cli.py:2313-2314`), each package path resolved to an absolute path (`benchmark/sets.py:51-58`), and hashes it into the provenance `set_digest` (`cli.py:2327`), which compare checks against the saved copy and requires equal within a study (`benchmark/compare.py:400-415, 500-503`). Loaded in a worktree, the saved path carries the local user name, and runs of one study from two checkouts are refused as different sets (RR-B0). The same check requires one commit across a study's runs, so the pilot workstation and this machine must run one commit.
- Design: the saved set names each package inside the repository by its repository-relative path, absolute only for a private package, whose run folder never enters the repository; `set_digest` is computed over that form, the same from any checkout. D12 is the profile half: standards findings stamp the digest and file name.
- **Fictional absolute paths are expected** (round 3, RR3-5). Every finding's provenance holds `ManifestEntry.vault_path`, the document's path (`findings.py:115`; `ir/models.py:243-245`), and the builders write fictional absolute roots there: `FICTIONAL_ROOT`, `C:\Fictional\` (`tests/support/mechanical.py:104`, written at `:500, 1003`, reused by the drawing and sitting builders); `/Designs/` (`tests/support/features.py:717`; `tests/support/packages.py:54`); the example profile's `vault_root`, `D:/ExampleVault` (`config/standards.example.yaml:11`), which roots the standards builder's documents (`tests/support/standards.py:310-313`) and the profile's bought and library prefixes. So every run over a generated case holds absolute paths, and a test failing on every rooted path would fail every committable folder; the public-repository risk is the checkout's own location, user name included.
- **The test.** Over every file in a committable run or study folder, JSON read as strings so that escaping hides nothing, it fails on: a path under the checkout root, the user profile or the temp folder, all known when the test runs; a saved set naming any package outside the repository, which is a private entry; and any drive-letter, UNC or rooted path outside a named list of fictional roots. The list: the builders' roots above, the example profile's `vault_root`, and the golden fixtures' roots (the fictional profiles' `vault_root`s, `tests/fixtures/standards/profile-a.yaml:10` and `profile-b.yaml:10`, and `/Library/`). It reads `FICTIONAL_ROOT` and each profile's `vault_root` from their sources, so it cannot drift from them.
- Alternatives: the generator writes relative vault paths (RR3-5's other option), which changes the builders' bytes, every golden they feed, and the example profile's prefix resolution against `vault_root`. Withdraw the permission to commit study folders (C-7's option): the committed ledger loses its rows.

### 8.9 The generator's imports (FR-027, SC-006; D14.1 as revised)

- Allowed: the `swreview.ir` models, the standards profile's data model, and the fictional builders under `reviewer/tests/support/`, the ingested-sheet builder (8.14) among them.
- The standards builder imports `StandardsProfile` from `swreview.checks.standards.profile` (`reviewer/tests/support/standards.py:38`), and importing that module runs `swreview/checks/standards/__init__.py`, which imports the standards check modules (`:16`). **Settled** (round 4, SC-006): the structural test checks the generator's direct imports, and those of the builders it imports, against an allowlist naming the profile module; the transitive import of the standards check modules is allowed, since nothing the generator writes calls them. Alternative: move the profile model out of `swreview.checks` and check transitive imports.

### 8.10 Failures and completed repetitions (FR-025, FR-036 to FR-041, FR-058; D2, D16)

- **The verdict's order** (FR-044, FR-058; D17; round 3, RR3-1). (0) Compare has already refused a study whose keys name fewer held-out model-produced defects than the floor, or that falls below the approved size or repetitions (FR-032; 8.7). (1) Each figure's rates are taken over its repetitions: completed only for the main figure, completed and behavioural stops for the sensitivity figure; a defect with none in an arm is dropped from both arms of that figure, and a package with none from both arms of that figure's false-alarm rule, which averages over every package of the set, each counted and named (round 4). (2) Every deciding rule is read on what remains, whatever the count: the paired rules on both figures, the code guard, and the cleared guard where the approved levels make it decide. Any loss gives quality loss. (3) Otherwise, fewer held-out model-produced defects than the floor on either figure gives inconclusive, naming the count. (4) Otherwise "no 10-point loss" needs the recall rule's lower end above -10 points on both figures; anything else is inconclusive, naming why. The simulator follows the same order (8.1).
- Retries (FR-038): the OpenAI client keeps the SDK's default retries (`agent/providers/openai_provider.py:290`), and Gemini gets one attempt (`agent/settings.py:253-263`). Starting design: the SDK's retries off, the benchmark's own retry with backoff and jitter, each attempt counted.
- Provider stops (FR-040; round 4): any stop or incomplete reason User Story 7 does not name, on either provider, is a behavioural stop of class "other", its reason recorded (OpenAI's are read at `agent/providers/openai_provider.py:638-654`, Gemini's at `agent/providers/gemini_provider.py:127-145`).
- **To resolve in plan.md**: what counts as stream activity when the wall clock fires (for example, a streamed event within a set interval).

### 8.11 Clean twins (FR-028)

- Under FR-028's per-package cap a twin keeps at most one other held-out model-produced defect, which counts only in its own case (RR-B18). The plan either marks it in the twin's key as counted elsewhere, or removes it, a stricter twin with no defect at all.

### 8.12 Reference-level matching (FR-003, FR-009; spec Edge Cases, US5-16)

- **Why parts are not enough.** A joint finding's component ids name only parts: the fastener's, the tapped hole's and the clamped components (`tools/checks_fastener.py:304-324`), or the two hole owners with a tolerance's drawing location (`:368-372, 377`); fit and stack findings carry only drawing locations (`tools/checks_fit.py:67, 106-109`); the scorer intersects those sets (`benchmark/scorecard.py:138`). `check_hole_alignment` accepts any two hole ids (`tools/checks_fastener.py:327-378`), so on AF-2's model variant another pair between the same plates writes unresolved and flags the defect (`checks/hole_alignment.py:98-101`), or, with the drawn tolerance, demonstrated or checked_within_scope, crediting or clearing it (`:130`); a degenerate `check_fit(bore, bore)` on R-3's sheet is a suspected transition fit (`checks/fit.py:112`) and credits variant A. The anchor's own failure, checking unrelated hole pairs, scored as detected (RR3-6).
- **The references findings carry** (no new field; on every path once 19A lands, below):
  - fastener joint, `fastener.bottoming`, `.engagement`, `.thread_match` and `.head_clearance`: `fastener_id` and `hole_id` in `calculation.inputs` (`checks/fastener.py:267-268, 809-810`, merged at `:311`), from `check_fastener_joint` and the joint map alike, at `be01974` only on results that keep a calculation (below). `fastener.head_fit` names the counterbore or countersink instance only (`checks/tool_access.py:433`), and `fastener.unsupported` the fastener only (`checks/fastener.py:700`), so their entries name that hole or that fastener;
  - hole alignment, `hole.coaxiality`: `hole_a` and `hole_b` in `calculation.inputs`, and both ids in `inputs` (`checks/hole_alignment.py:82-83, 108, 140`); the joint map's `hole.nominal_alignment` and `hole.position_stack`: the joint's hole instances in `inputs` (`checks/joint_alignment.py:399, 643`);
  - fit, `fit.size_only`: the bore and shaft `Dimension`s in `inputs` (`checks/fit.py:121`, and `:170` when unresolved), each with its `source`;
  - axial stack, `stack.worst_case`: each contributor's and the target's `Dimension` in `inputs` (`checks/stack.py:86`).
- **19A: references on every path** (FR-061; round 4, RR4-1). In scope: the four fastener rules, `hole.coaxiality`, `hole.nominal_alignment`, `hole.position_stack`; `fastener.head_fit` and `fastener.unsupported` name their one reference on every path today (`checks/tool_access.py:409-420`, `checks/fastener.py:689-700`). `unresolved()` writes no calculation (`checks/result.py:114`), and the fastener rules pass it only `stack.sources`, quantities with no ids: bottoming `checks/fastener.py:362, 371`, thread match `:521, 535`, head clearance `:609`; engagement's unresolved result keeps a calculation only when protrusion and thread depth are known (`:450-480`). So an unresolved fastener finding names no joint, and US1-17's four-screw case, like `cover-blind-tap`'s D-1, is unreachable. Starting design: those paths lead `inputs` with the fastener and hole ids (`[fastener.id, hole.id, *stack.sources]`), for `check_fastener_joint` and the joint map's placed screws alike (`_placed_stack`, `:803`). Alignment: `check_hole_alignment`'s unresolved result already names both holes (`checks/hole_alignment.py:98-118`), and the joint map's `_no_fastener` and missing-tolerance results every instance of the joint (`checks/joint_alignment.py:236, 520`); `_unknown_role` names only the instances of unknown role (`:222`, called at `:291, 508`) and is widened to every instance. Consequences: the tool results the model reads grow by those ids, moving token measurements slightly; golden fixtures pinning these results, and the replay fixtures (regenerated from the recordings on the machine that holds them, feature 008 SC-001), regenerate in the same change (008's decision 3A); unit tests assert the ids on each unresolved path, and a golden probe scores an unresolved joint finding (8.15). The findings' text is unchanged.
- **Rule.** An entry of those checks names, for each joint, pair, fit or stack it covers, its references: the fastener and hole ids, both hole ids, or each dimension's document, sheet and annotation, the triple `resolve_dimension` matches on (`tools/refs.py:73-75`). Holes are named in the form the entry's check writes: package hole ids for the tools and the fastener checks, hole instances (`<hole id>#<n>`, `checks/joints.py:649`) for the joint map's alignment checks. A finding matches when its parts or documents overlap the entry (FR-003) and its `inputs`, `calculation.inputs` or `drawing_locations` name every reference of one covered item. A call on another pair, another fit on the same sheet, or the bore against itself names a different set, so it neither credits nor clears the planted entry (US5-16).
- **Folding.** The joint map folds joints with one result into one finding whose `inputs` name the first joint only; the others appear only in its text (`checks/joints.py:1185-1226`). An entry covering a folded defect names every joint it covers, so the finding matches through whichever comes first.
- **Validation.** Two reference-keyed entries of overlapping checks overlap only when they share a covered joint, pair, fit or stack, since one finding names one item's references (D7). A forbidden entry stays on parts or documents, so it still overlaps a reference-keyed defect on its parts. Key validation resolves every hole, fastener and dimension reference against the package (FR-009's id resolution), and the generator writes them from the case spec.
- **What the seeds gain** (§3): R-3 may keep the anchor's two pins; AF-1's other screws may join the same parts; AF-2's candidate pairs may sit between the same plates; US5-16's case is generable. Round 2's generator constraint is dropped.
- **To resolve in plan.md**: a `.*` family entry that spans reference-keyed and other checks (`fastener.*` also covers `fastener.identity`, which judges a fastener document and names components only, `tools/checks_mechanical.py:284-294`).
- Alternative (RR3-6's other option): keep part-level matching and widen FR-028 to refuse any other hole on either part of a planted alignment defect, any other tapped hole on a planted fastener defect's part, and any other length dimension on a planted fit's sheet. It holds, but bars from the planted parts and sheets every sibling the anchors had.

### 8.13 Other points moved from the spec or the re-review

Each is the plan's because it is mechanism, not outcome.
- R-3's floating-fastener pass (§3 R-3). A forbidden entry grades only demonstrated or suspected findings, so the pass is not graded as keys stand. Keying the joint's press fit as a known defect fails the validation: the joint check can only pass it, so code never detects it and no correct reviewer can (FR-054). The options are a forbidden entry that may also name checked-within-scope findings (a key-format extension), or leaving the pass ungraded and visible in the transcript. It is tuned-on, so neither decides the verdict (D15, D17).
- R-8: whether the product emits one "profile likely wrong" line for hygiene decides whether the key expects it (§3 R-8); making it emit one is outside this feature.
- The pilot's size; how its estimation noise enters the simulation; whether pilot runs count as off-arm repetitions (a mild optional-stopping effect). The repetition cap is FR-055's 10 (§6).
- The report's reader (FR-034, D11; round 4, RR4-m19). `read_recording` refuses a folder with no usage event (`benchmark/recording.py:163-167`) or no `package.json` (`:168-172`), which `benchmark run` never writes (only the replay copies one, `benchmark/replay.py:323-325`). Starting design: the report shares the recording reader's event and turn parsing (`_EventReader`, `:175-178, 222`) without those two refusals, finds each package through the run folder's saved set (8.8), and shows a repetition with no usage as failed (FR-035). Alternative: a tolerant mode of `read_recording`.
- FR-009's refusal of an expected-coverage item that is not in the checklist has no acceptance scenario of its own; the plan's key-validation tests cover it (RR-A27).
- `specs/001-agentic-design-review/contracts/cli.md:37`, updated with PP-1, and the cross-check's contradictions and missing tests (§4).

### 8.14 Ingested drawing sheets and the example profile (FR-027, FR-054, 16A; D14.1 as revised)

- **Why a builder.** `check_fit`, `check_axial_stack` and a `check_hole_alignment` with a tolerance read their dimensions only through `resolve_dimension`, which matches ingested `package.drawings` sheets and refuses a native drawing dimension while `DRAWING_BINDING_VALIDATED` is false (`tools/refs.py:94-97`; `drawings/binding.py:59`). No existing builder writes an ingested sheet (§7: model dimensions, native drawing records, hand-written fit and stack goldens), so without one 16A's first model-produced kind has no generable seed (RR2-4).
- **Design.** One fictional builder writing a drawing `Document`, its `pdf` `ManifestEntry` and a `DrawingSheet` of `Dimension` records (document, sheet and annotation; nominal; tolerance; text as read), in the shape `reviewer/tests/unit/test_tools_checks_fit.py:112-156` builds inline. It lives with the other fictional builders (8.9). `cover-blind-tap`, a PDF-ingest package whose drawings are ingested sheets, is regenerated with it (FR-010).
- **What a model-produced seed leaves out.** Model dimensions are the joint map's tolerance source 3 (`checks/tolerances.py:768-839`), which the argument-free `check_joints` asks (`tools/checks_mechanical.py:312`). With any source held anywhere in the package (`holds_any_source`, `checks/tolerances.py:1007-1030`: a toleranced model dimension, a model position tolerance, an ISO 286 Hole Wizard class, or a validated drawing binding) the joint map runs `hole.position_stack` on every joint (`checks/joint_alignment.py:698-712`), and on a joint whose pin and hole sizes resolve it writes suspected or demonstrated for an oversize pin (`:578-610`): a code-produced defect of another check. A model-produced fit, stack or alignment seed carries no such source for its joint's parts; FR-054(a) verifies.
- **Standards drawing rules** read ingested sheets (`checks/standards/drawing.py:375`), so the builder's sheets satisfy the example profile, or their findings are keyed; FR-054(a) fails a case that does neither.
- **The example profile.** Every committed case is built and keyed against `config/standards.example.yaml` (FR-027). The existing builders and fixtures write profile A's names (`tests/support/mechanical.py:108-111`; `tests/support/sitting.py:8-9, 188-198`), so the generator passes the example profile's: the data card (`config/standards.example.yaml:41-49`), the part-number pattern (`:51-55`), the revision property (`:57-60`), the hygiene properties (`:97-101`) and the part-role signals (`:125-180`). A case built in profile A's names would raise a revision finding on every document, which FR-054(a) fails. The fictional-string guard's vocabulary is extended with those names, as the drawing fixtures extend it (`tests/support/drawings.py:96`).
- Alternative: D14.1's (RR2-4's other option).

### 8.15 Golden probes and the version rule (FR-008, FR-049, SC-013; D14.2; round 3)

- **Why probes.** The oracle scores zero on every failure-mode column its key grades (SC-001; made exact by RF-58) and the null review records nothing, so neither moves when a counting rule changes, nor when a matching rule the oracle never exercises does: the `.*` boundary, reference matching (8.12), or id-to-instance resolution (8.3). Under a two-way version rule such a change could not bump the version without failing the tests, so FR-008 would be skipped in practice (RR3-2).
- **What a probe is.** A synthetic review of one committed case, built as data by the validation and fed to the scorer, as the oracle and null reviews are: the session's findings, coverage, requests, answer batches and steps (refused request calls included, which the scorer reads from the calls, 8.3). A probe never runs a tool or check, so a tool change moves no golden and only a scoring change does, which is what the version tracks. Each probe starts from the case's null or oracle review, adds or changes one behaviour, and names the columns that behaviour moves. Its test asserts its scorecard differs from the base's in those columns only: exactly its behaviour. Some behaviours move two columns by definition, a forbidden hit also being a false alarm (FR-017) and a repeat of an open request also an unanswerable request (8.3).
- **Coverage.** At least one probe per scorecard column (the detection outcomes of User Story 1's table; findings and false alarms by each origin and each kind of origin 3; contradictions, confirmations, forbidden and withdrawn forbidden hits, coverage mismatches, unresolved coverage, unanswerable requests, re-asks seen and refused, repeats of open questions, unmatched requests, question-count misses, unscripted questions, bound-reached) and per matching rule (exact check; `.*` family at depth and its boundary; a sibling rule on the same part; part, drawing-location and provenance-document overlap; each reference kind of 8.12 on resolved and unresolved results, and a folded joint-map finding; hole and fastener ids to instances, and ids to documents for unanswerable entries; the item-only re-ask; the part-roles question by identity). A structural test fails when a field of the scorecard model, or a rule in the list of matching rules kept beside the probes, has no probe, so a new column or rule arrives with one. Each probe names the case and key entry it needs, and fails, naming both, when a case change removes the entry.
- **The version rule.** A committed record holds one row per scorer version with a digest over every golden scorecard, the oracles', the nulls' and the probes'. The test fails when the goldens' digest differs from the current version's row (a golden change without a version change) and when two rows share a digest (a version change with no golden change). Rows are appended, never edited. **To resolve in plan.md**: whether a test also compares the record with the one at the merge base with `main`, skipped where git history is absent as the identifier tests are (D13), to catch an edited row. (Settled by SR-19, then dropped by RF-69.)
- Alternative (RR3-2's other option): the oracle and null goldens only, and only "a golden change needs a version change"; cheaper, but a rule change they cannot see goes unversioned.

---

## §9 Plan decisions

Taken 2026-09-29 while planning, by five area designs (scoring and the key; the run and provenance; cases and the generator; questions and scripted answers; the verdict, simulator, pilot and report) and their integration. Each entry: what it resolves, the decision, why, and the alternatives. Every one is a reversible plan-level default under the owner decisions; the questions that change scope, cost or what the study measures are §9.7's, all answered or kept at their defaults on 2026-09-29 but question 8, on whose recommended option the plan proceeded until 30A chose it on 2026-09-30. The review fixes of §9.9 to §9.11 refine earlier entries, each of which ends with a pointer to the fix. Where the integration overruled an area, the area's entry says "superseded" and names the integration decision, and the losing contract was edited to match. Line numbers are at `be01974`.

### 9.1 Integration decisions

- **INT-1** (resolves: `benchmark-run.md` 12 against `report.md` 3, FR-057, D11). Decision: `prompts.json` is written by the benchmark attempt only, before the first turn and when the body ends, as `{prompts_version, system, user_messages[]}`, every `role: user` message of `ReviewRun.messages` in order; the report reads it. Why: `ReviewRun.messages` holds every user message whole (`agent/runner.py:1399-1402, 1444-1447`: the history is never pruned in place); writing from `_ask` on every review would change the pane's run folders and add a `coverage.prompts` row to every product review for a record only the eval reads. Alternatives: the `_ask` writer (RP-3, superseded); a new event type (changes the chat-events schema and streams a 30 KB prompt to the pane).
- **INT-2** (resolves: `scoring.md` 7 against `questions.md` 6). Decision: one reasons map, `not_assessed`, over every `null` column, the question columns included. Why: one reader, one shape; a second map would be a second writer of "why is this column null". Alternatives: two maps (`questions_not_assessed` beside `not_assessed`).
- **INT-3** (resolves: `cases.md` 5, CS-12, against `answer-key.md` 2.2 and PROV-10; US2-11, FR-031). Decision: every digest of a text input is `sha256_of` over raw bytes; `answer_key.current_stamp()` names the prompt and checklist once; the checkouts agree through `.gitattributes` `eol=lf` on the digested inputs; `test_line_endings.py` fails on a working copy that still holds a carriage return; the two pane studies refuse, before any review, a digested repository input holding one. Why: a key's stamp and a run's `checklist_digest` of one file must be one number, and the profile's digest is the product's raw-byte identity that findings stamp; this checkout reads `i/lf w/crlf` for the three files (`git ls-files --eol`), so without the guard a stale working copy would record digests another machine's runs do not share and compare would refuse the study only after it was paid for. Alternatives: CS-12's CRLF-as-LF digest (superseded: two numbers for one file on Windows); normalising every digest (breaks equality with the product's profile identity). Refined by RF-48 (§9.10): `sha256_of` lives in `benchmark/digest.py`; and by RF-64 (§9.11): 25A adds the tool descriptions to the stamp.
- **INT-4** (resolves: VD-12 against CS-4; SC-010, FR-055). Decision: the simulator's reference scenarios keep SC-010's layout and growth unit (17 packages of 2 defects, 34 twins, growing by 2 defects and 2 twins), as the spec fixes them; after the pilot the layout and its growth unit are the committed set's own, read from the saved set's `twin_of` (2 defects and 1 twin under twin rule B). Why: the reference scenarios calibrate the simulator against the spec's stated case; the approved size must be the set that will run. Alternatives: rule A's unit everywhere (over-sizes a rule-B set); rewriting SC-010 (a spec change for no gain).
- **INT-5** (resolves: D10's open column, RUN-13's `cache_write` against RP-5; FR-042). Decision: three prices per model, as FR-042 fixes; cache-write tokens are shown as a count and never priced, and the cost's basis names a non-zero count. Why: FR-042 names three prices; a fourth column is configurability nothing asks for; the pane study has no cost threshold (D1). Alternatives: RUN-13's optional `cache_write` price (superseded).
- **INT-6** (resolves: GN-5 against QA-1's module layout). Decision: `CaseFile` and `ScriptedAnswer` live in a data-only `benchmark/case_file.py`; `benchmark/answers.py` holds `load_case_file`, the driver and the answer log. Why: the generator writes case files through the model and may not import the driver, which imports the agent's runner. Alternatives: the models in `answers.py` (the generator could not write through them); plain dicts (a second format definition). Applied to the key as well by RF-1 (§9.9).
- **INT-7** (resolves: `benchmark-run.md` 5.1's seam against `scripted-answers.md` 3). Decision: `drive_review` gains a required `stop_requested` callable in the robustness slice, checked at each answer point; a set stop ends the loop with `ended: "stopped"`. Why: a review whose turns call no tool would otherwise take answer turns after its wall clock; landing it with the stop it serves keeps slice 3 free of an unused parameter. Alternatives: an optional parameter from slice 3 (a default no one sets for three slices).
- **INT-8** (resolves: `cases.md` 6.3's `mir-sitting` A-2 against QA's R-2 risk). Decision: `mir-sitting`'s case file scripts only the part-roles answer; no answer to the drawing candidate question. Why: that question is asked only when the host opens closed drawings (`checks/drawing_context.py:490-503`), and a benchmark attempt has no bridge, so its read mode is `none` (`tools/drawings.py:79-90`); the entry could never be delivered. R-2 is graded on what the model records unprompted. Alternatives: keep the entry (an unused entry on every run).
- **INT-9** (resolves: the run area's risk "compare loads a session per scorecard package", and the verdict's and report's run-status reads). Decision: the scorer copies each package's outcome into the scorecard through `package_outcome` and `attempt_facts`: `PackageScore.run_status` and `cost`; `Scorecard.unscored_packages` for infrastructure failures, which get no `PackageScore`; `Scorecard.failed_attempts`; `Aggregate.cost` and `failed_attempts_cost`; `per_package` may be empty and `Aggregate.packages` may be 0. `run_status` is in the golden view; `cost` is not. Why: compare loads one session per `PackageScore` (`benchmark/compare.py:310-316`), so a failed package must not have one; the verdict, pilot and report then read one file, as the scorecard already copies `session.usage`. Alternatives: every reader calls `package_outcome` itself (three readers of the run-status layer); failed packages as `PackageScore`s with null outcomes (breaks "the defect columns are always assessed"). Refined by RF-16 and RF-17 (§9.9): `served_models`, and each failed attempt's `folder`.
- **INT-10** (resolves: §4 cross-check "ledger schema", "run column order", "tool calls on the pane row", "old-scorer numbers not annotated"; SR-20, PROV-11, VD-16). Decision: one bump, `LEDGER_SCHEMA = "1.1"`, in the parity slice, with the final `RUN_COLUMNS` and `LEVER_COLUMNS` order fixed in `provenance.md` 5 and the committed header regenerated once; the 1.1 schema record names `pane_studies`, omitted when empty and filled from the verdict slice; no run-status column (its source arrives later and would regenerate the header twice); the tool-call count is not split by the step mark, and the ledger's notes say it counts pre-run and regrade steps; the hand-written "Measurements outside the ledger" gains one sentence that its quality figures were scored under scoring rules version 1. Why: `ledger.json` is written and never read back by code (`compare.py:796`), so a key filled later moves no reader, and the committed document is the markdown, whose pane section renders only when present. Alternatives: two or three bumps; a status column added in slice 6 (a second regeneration); splitting tool calls (changes the lever counters' meaning).
- **INT-11** (resolves: the slice order against the version rule; SC-013, D14.2). Decision: the scorer slice writes `SCORING_RULES_VERSION = 2`; the key-extensions slice, which adds counting columns, writes 3; the validation slice creates the goldens and the record with its first row at 3; the robustness slice's `run_status` moves every golden and appends the next row. Until the record exists a slice that changes scoring bumps the constant, pinned by a test of its value. Why: the goldens need the committed cases, which arrive in slice 4, but a scorecard scored at an earlier slice must still meet the re-score line, never a silent difference. Alternatives: one version for the whole feature (scorecards of one version would differ between slices); goldens over test fixtures from slice 1 (a second case set to keep).
- **INT-12** (resolves: SR-19's digest; SC-013 on Windows). Decision: `goldens_sha256` reads each golden as git stores it, through `tests/support/mechanical.as_committed` (CRLF as LF). Why: `data_regression` writes in binary with LF (`pytest_regressions/data_regression.py:79`) while this checkout hands `*.yml` back CRLF (`git ls-files --eol` reads `w/crlf` for `test_golden/joint-ok.yml`), so a raw digest would differ between a fresh regeneration and a checkout on one machine. Alternatives: `eol=lf` on the golden folder alone (a regeneration on Windows still writes before git normalises; the reader must not depend on the working copy).
- **INT-13** (resolves: VD-14's "only private packages" reading against PROV-05's study names). Decision: a pane study's slice is its study name, `pane_defaults` committed and `pane_defaults_private` private; a `pane_defaults` study whose saved sets name a private package is refused at the verdict only (raw rows kept), the run-time refusal being the first reader. Why: provenance already keys the private slice as its own study; inferring the slice from the sets is a second rule that could disagree. Alternatives: slice from the saved sets (VD-14 as drafted); a run-level refusal (loses the raw rows of paid runs).
- **INT-14** (resolves: where CS-16's existence checks and QA's case-file checks run, against RUN's "provenance before any review"). Decision: `benchmark/runner.preflight_set(set, profile, checklist)` runs the set's refusals, the existence checks (keys stat-ed, never opened) and the case files' refusals, and `run_benchmark` requires its result; the command line's order is: usage errors, a non-empty `--out`, the profile, the provider, `preflight_set`, the committable guard, the pane-study file rules, P-3, then provenance and the saved set, then the first review. Why: one function gives a library caller the same refusals and lets the command line write the record between them and the first review. Alternatives: checks inside `run_benchmark` (the record could not precede the first review without a callback); checks in the command line only (a library caller skips them).
- **INT-15** (resolves: the scorer slice needs format-2 committed keys before the generator exists). Decision: in the scorer slice `cover-blind-tap.json` is rewritten by hand in format 2 with D-2 on the housing drawing document `DRW-2001` (expected unresolved, model, tuned-on) and C-1 with its dimension references where they resolve; D-1 is left out until the generator slice regenerates the case, since the package has no hole or fastener row (0 and 0 at `be01974`) for its references to resolve against (`answer-key.md` row 13). Why: the committed-keys test must load every key from the first slice; D-1 is unreachable today anyway (research §2). Alternatives: move the generator earlier (against 12A); mark the key key-only (refused while its package exists). Refined by RF-12 (§9.9).
- **INT-16** (resolves: the verdict area's risk "keys could change between runs of a study"). Decision: the scorecard records `key_sha256` per package; compare refuses a study whose runs name two digests for one package's key, naming it. Why: an arm scored against an edited key measures the edit; private keys are not pinned by the commit. Alternatives: a provenance field (runs never read keys); no check.
- **INT-17** (resolves: §4 cross-check "shared files"). Decision: one owner per file per slice, as the plan's "Lanes and file ownership" table assigns `cli.py`, `compare.py`, `scorecard.py`, `benchmark/runner.py`, `sets.py`, `answers.py`, `agent/runner.py`, `chat/server.py`, `handoff.py`, `report_page.py`, `tests/support/studies.py`, `test_benchmark_compare.py`, `docs/llm-efficiency-options.md`, `ab-harness.md`, the goldens, the replay fixtures and `benchmarks/**`. Why: the collisions the cross-check listed become sequential edits by one lane each. Alternatives: parallel lanes rebasing over each other in `compare.py`.
- **INT-18** (resolves: FR-047's tokenizer clause and D14.6, which no area's tests named). Decision: `benchmark-run.md` 15 adds an offline run, score and report with the vocabulary unavailable: every stored `result_tokens` is `null` (`tools/registry.py:646-657`), never 0, and nothing is refused. Why: the product already leaves the count unknown; the eval must not refuse or read 0. Alternatives: none needed.

### 9.2 Scoring and the answer key

- **SR-1** (FR-001, SP-10, US1-1, US1-2). Decision: a finding's check matches an entry only when equal to its exact check or starting with a family id and a dot, at any depth, never `x`, never `xs.y`; `classify_match`, `check_prefix` and `MatchRule` (`benchmark/scorecard.py:57, 122-140`) go and the loop written twice (`:267-271, 303-307`) becomes one pass in `benchmark/matching.py`. Why: the last-dot prefix rule is the audit's sibling credit; exact-or-family is 4A's rule and SP-10's default. Alternatives: keep the prefix rule; a family of exactly one more segment.
- **SR-2** (FR-003, SP-4). Decision: targets overlap when component ids intersect, or when documents intersect, a finding's documents being its drawing locations' and, only when it names no component and no location, its provenance documents. Why: SP-4 B, the one reading that reaches document-scoped findings without letting a component finding's part document match document entries (`findings.py:273-290`). Alternatives: drawing locations only; always all provenance documents.
- **SR-3** (FR-003, FR-061, §8.12's rule, SC-002, US5-16). Decision: references are read from `inputs` only, by kind (joint and hole pair: plain id strings; recess and joint instances: `#` instance strings; fit and stack: dimension triples), and a finding matches when that set equals one covered item's; roles are not compared. Why: after SR-5 every reference-keyed result carries its references in `inputs` on every path, so one field suffices; equality stops a stack with an extra contributor, or two partial-joint entries, letting one finding match two entries; a swapped call's verdict is what the report tells the engineer. Alternatives: §8.12's three fields with subset semantics (over-match); ordered roles.
- **SR-5** (FR-061, 19A). Decision: `_Stack` gains `refs = [fastener.id, hole.id]` (`checks/fastener.py:267-268, 809-810`); every result of the four fastener rules writes `inputs = [*stack.refs, *stack.sources]` at all thirteen sites, resolved paths included; `_unsupported` takes the hole; `joint_alignment._unknown_role` names every instance of the joint; four check goldens and the replay fixtures regenerate in the same commit. Why: one field for the scorer and the exact joint on every result for the engineer (Principle IV), at two short ids per result. Alternatives: unresolved paths only (two readers); 19A's rejected options. Refined by RF-2 (§9.9): five check goldens and one new one; the replay fixtures stay byte-identical under 19A.
- **SR-6** (FR-002, 13A, SP-6, SP-8). Decision: per defect, any demonstrated or suspected match credits (a status mismatch when expected unresolved); else an unresolved match flags (expected unresolved) or is flagged below expected; else missed only if nothing matched; lists `matched_defect_ids` (kept, now "detected"), `flagged_…`, `status_mismatch_…`, `flagged_below_expected_…`, `cleared_…`, `missed_…`, with invariants asserted. Why: order-independent; keeping `matched_defect_ids` as detected leaves `adoption.py:210, 220, 236` unchanged. Alternatives: renamed lists; SP-8 B.
- **SR-7** (FR-005, SP-7, US1-9). Decision: cleared is independent of detection; a cleared-only defect is neither detected nor missed. Why: the Terms and the Edge Case "cleared and credited: both recorded". Alternatives: ignore clears; cleared as missed.
- **SR-8** (FR-004, SP-5). Decision: `contradicted_condition_ids` and `confirmed_condition_ids`, `null` with no condition; an unresolved match counts in neither; the excuse at `scorecard.py:274-276` goes. Why: the overlap refusal guarantees a contradicting finding credits no defect. Alternatives: indexes or counts.
- **SR-9** (FR-017, D14.4, CL-09, US3-1, US3-2). Decision: a false alarm is a demonstrated or suspected final-state finding crediting no defect, at most one per finding; forbidden hits are false alarms matching a forbidden entry; withdrawn forbidden hits are read from `events.jsonl` (a `finding.withdrawn` id absent from the final state, its last `finding` body demonstrated or suspected and matching a forbidden entry); an unreadable stream makes that column `null` with its reason. Why: the session holds no withdrawn finding (`tools/context.py:316-335`). Alternatives: record withdrawn findings in the session; a missing stream as zero.
- **SR-10** (D15, 15A). Decision: `tuned_on_false_alarm_finding_ids`: false alarms hitting any tuned-on forbidden entry or condition; a mixed hit is tuned-on. Why: letting a behaviour the prompt was written around decide would score the tuning. Alternatives: held-out wins; refuse mixed-tag overlaps.
- **SR-11** (FR-018, D6, §8.4). Decision: expected coverage is read with `goal_of` and `goal_lines`: `issues` and `checked` read checked; `not_reached` with unresolved or failed reads unresolved, with skipped skipped, with no check not reported (always a mismatch); `not_applicable` out of scope; one expectation per goal; a key expecting a non-checked status on a goal holding a known defect's check is refused; an item no goal reads is refused. Why: no second reader; the refusal keeps the oracle satisfiable. Alternatives: `issues` as a listable status; per-part expectations; `bucket_of`. Refined by RF-23 (§9.9): read through `goal_readings`.
- **SR-12** (FR-007, D9, SP-1, SP-3, 17A, 20A). Decision: eight values (`prerun`, `regrade`, `code_only_call`, `configured_check`, `model`, `carried`, `setup`, `unknown`) derived, first rule winning; literal `CODE_ONLY_TOOLS` and `CONFIGURED_CHECK_TOOLS` held true by a structural test; `MODEL_ORIGINS = {model, configured_check}`; `finding_origins` stored per package. Why: D9's seven with 20A's by-tool split; literals are readable; origin is never a second stored field. Alternatives: three or five buckets; code-only only for exact pre-run calls. Refined by RF-6 (§9.9): `CODE_ONLY_TOOLS` is built from the pre-run's constants; by RF-38 (§9.10): the signature rule names the two interference tools as exceptions; and by RF-68 (§9.11): `CONFIGURED_CHECK_TOOLS` is dropped.
- **SR-13** (SP-2, FR-007, US1-13). Decision: `InvestigationStep.origin_mark` set by `recorded_call(mark=...)` after the call; `_prerun_call` passes `prerun`, `_restate` `regrade`. Why: SP-2 A; restated calls now happen in benchmark runs. Alternatives: a flag through five layers; a session-level id list.
- **SR-14** (FR-007, US1-12). Decision: `ReviewSession.origin_marks = 1`, written by `start_review` on every new session; without it every code finding but a carried one reads unknown; the pane-study refusal is compare's. Why: a value always written, never a false flag the serializer drops (`report/session.py:73-75`). Alternatives: written by `new_session`; setup and configured readable without the record. Refined by RF-27 (§9.10): its commit regenerates the replay fixtures, after 19A's.
- **SR-15** (FR-006, FR-031, FR-032, §8.7, US5-12). Decision: `graded_items` copied per package; recall over held-out items read from tags (the package-flag filter at `scorecard.py:431-435` goes); `recall_by_producer`; `held_out_model_defects` counted from every package's key before failures; the set flag must equal the key's derivation; a key-only key in a scored set is refused. Why: compare reads no key. Alternatives: compare re-reads keys; the package-flag recall. Refined by RF-39 (§9.10): compare checks the flag against `graded_items`; by RF-62 (§9.11): a scored package missing from the saved set is refused; and by RF-67 (§9.11): the two derivations are `AnswerKey` methods.
- **SR-16** (FR-021, US3-8). Decision: columns tied to a key list are `null` when the key has none, rendered "not assessed"; one `not_assessed` map (name settled by INT-2). Why: Principle I. Alternatives: zeros; two maps.
