- **SR-17** (SP-14). Decision: id lists only, counts derived; every new field required. Why: one writer per number; old scorecards are refused by version before validation. Alternatives: list-plus-count; defaults.
- **SR-18** (FR-008, SP-11, D14.2, §4 refusal order, US1-11). Decision: `SCORING_RULES_VERSION` written as `scoring_rules_version` (absent reads 1); `read_scorecard` checks it before validation, refuses lower (naming the re-score command) and higher; compare's `_load_run` calls it first. Why: without the pre-validation read, an old folder surfaces a validation error. Alternatives: document re-scoring; refuse lower only.
- **SR-19** (FR-049, SC-013, D14.2, §8.15's open point). Decision: golden view `PackageScore` less `GOLDEN_EXCLUDED`; goldens by `data_regression`; probes as data with `MATCHING_RULES`; the record `scoring_rules_versions.json` fails on drift without bump, a bump without drift, non-consecutive versions, and a record at `HEAD` or the merge base with `main` that is not a prefix (skipped without history). The digest reads files as git stores them (INT-12). Why: the prefix check catches an edited row. Alternatives: oracle and null only, one-way rule; no merge-base check. Refined by RF-21 (§9.9): `goldens_unchanged`; by RF-43 (§9.10): `GOLDEN_IDENTITY`; and by RF-69 (§9.11): the `HEAD` and merge-base prefix check is dropped.
- **SR-20** (SP-13, §4 schema and column order). Decision: the scorer supplies fields, the compare contract the columns, one bump; the order is INT-10's. Why: two bumps collide. Alternatives: two bumps.
- **SR-21** (§8.13 R-3's pass). Decision: forbidden entries grade demonstrated and suspected findings only; R-3's floating-fastener pass stays ungraded and visible. Why: a forbidden hit is a false alarm (FR-017); the item is tuned-on and code-produced. Alternatives: a forbidden entry that may name passes.
- **SR-22** (§3 R-8's "to resolve", §8.13). Decision: R-8's key grades its per-document `hygiene.revision_present` forbidden hits only; no "profile likely wrong" line is expected. Why: hygiene writes one result per document and no zero-match line (`checks/hygiene.py:384-387`; the only one is part roles', `checks/part_roles.py:194`); making it is outside this feature. Alternatives: add the line to hygiene.
- **SR-23** (§4 `test_cli.py:2397-2417`). Decision: the CLI test's key is rewritten in format 2 with its drawing defect keyed to `doc:1`, tagged `held_out` with `held_out_against` equal to `current_stamp()`, which `write_key` computes, since the fixture's set marks `cover` held out (`tests/unit/test_cli.py:2373-2393`) and `score` refuses a flag its key does not back. Why: the fixture tests wiring; the support package has no drawing document (`tests/support/packages.py:97`). Alternatives: add a drawing document to `build_package`.
- **SR-24** (SC-001, SC-002, FR-049). Decision: the oracle is one `build_finding` per known defect at its expected status (the first covered item's references in `inputs`), no finding for conditions or forbidden entries, one coverage row per expectation, `origin_marks` set; the null is an empty session with `origin_marks`. Why: the product's own constructor gives real provenance; SC-002 is asserted by matching each oracle finding against every defect. Alternatives: a literal key echo. Refined by RF-15 (a key-only key's stand-in package) and RF-58 (§9.11: both written as package folders the scorer reads as it reads a run).
- **SR-25** (FR-007, `scoring.md` 5.3 rule 4). Decision: a finding citing a step its session does not hold reads `unknown` in a session carrying the origin-marks record too, and compare refuses the verdict of a pane study holding such a false alarm (`verdict.md` 7, refusal 3), as it refuses a session without the record. Why: FR-007 says only a session without the record reads unknown; a cited step missing from a session that has the record means the folder was altered or written by something other than the review, and any guessed origin would move the deciding figure without anyone seeing it (Principle I). Alternatives: guess from the nearest step's tool; read it as a configured check; name it on the verdict and decide anyway (a silent hole in the deciding figure). The spec's FR-007 now says the same (§9.11).
- **AK-1** (FR-009, D7). Decision: `key_format: 2`; format 1 refused naming the contract; the pydantic model is the only definition. Why: one clear refusal; two definitions drift. Alternatives: infer the format; a JSON schema file. Refined by RF-1 (§9.9): the model lives in the data-only `benchmark/answer_key_model.py`.
- **AK-2** (FR-009, SP-5). Decision: ids carry their list's prefix (`D-`, `C-`, `FB-`, `CV-`, `U-`, `Q-`), unique across the key. Why: a scorecard reader sees which list an id came from; `FB-` because findings are `F-`. Alternatives: free ids.
- **AK-3** (FR-009, FR-010, D7, US1-16). Decision: an explicit `key_only: true` marker. Why: a mistyped package path is never read as an unbuilt package. Alternatives: key-only from the package's absence.
- **AK-4** (§8.12's "to resolve": a family spanning reference-keyed checks). Decision: a known defect or condition whose family covers a reference-keyed check (`fastener.*`, `hole.*`, `fit.*`, `stack.*`) is refused; forbidden entries may use them; `cover-blind-tap`'s D-1 is exact `fastener.bottoming`. Why: a pass on one rule would clear a defect of another, the sibling credit 19A removed. Alternatives: family entries matching non-reference checks by parts.
- **AK-5** (FR-009 for hole instances). Decision: an instance resolves by its hole id and a positive `#n`; whether the joint map forms it is FR-054 (a)'s. Why: instances come from face geometry (`checks/joints.py:619-660`). Alternatives: run the joint map at key load.
- **AK-6** (FR-009's typo guard). Decision: every exact id and family id must fall under a report goal (`goal_of` not `None`). Why: a mistyped check grades nothing and passes every test. Alternatives: none; a committed-keys-only test.
- **AK-7** (FR-010, US5-4). Decision: `rms-part` rewritten by hand, key-only, producer code, statuses from each rule's registered severity (a test pins them), conditions `C-1` to `C-18`, all tuned-on; `cover-blind-tap` regenerated by the generator (its interim is INT-15). Why: no digests on a key no run reads; statuses cannot drift from the registry. Alternatives: tag `rms-part` held out.
- **AK-8** (FR-031, 15A). Decision: `held_out_against {prompt, checklist}` required iff held out (25A adds `tools`, RF-64); a stale digest is not a load refusal. Why: 15A removes status in the citing commit, not on drift. Alternatives: refuse stale digests.
- **AK-9** (FR-019, FR-060, FR-053). Decision: the question sections' fields and refusals are `questions.md` 5's, run at key load through `benchmark/questions.py`. Why: one definition. Alternatives: define them twice.

### 9.3 The run and provenance

- **RUN-01** (PP-1, FR-015, US2-1, US2-7). Decision: `_review_settings` returns `ResolvedReview(efficiency, model_view, explain_findings)`; a typed flag wins, else `PaneDefaults.explain_findings` (new, `True`), else off; tri-state on `review` and `benchmark run`; the chat server reads the default; 008's and 001's `cli.md` updated. Why: one resolver, one meaning of `--pane-defaults`. Alternatives: PP-1 B. Refined by RF-19 (§9.9).
- **RUN-02** (PP-2, US2-4). Decision: both pane studies require a typed explanation setting on every arm (exit 2). Why: held equal and stated (11A). Alternatives: PP-2 B, C.
- **RUN-03** (6A, FR-011). Decision: `benchmark run` gains `--pane-defaults`, the model-view switches, `--standards-profile`, tri-state explanations and `--package-time-limit`, resolved by `_review_settings` only; retry policy, activity window and grace are recorded constants. Why: no second resolver; no unasked configurability. Alternatives: a benchmark-only resolver; `--lever` approximations. Refined by RF-19 (§9.9).
- **RUN-04** (PP-5, FR-050, US2-2). Decision: the profile is loaded before the set and any review; its errors passed to `_errors_as_exit_1` by name (`ProfileError` subclasses `Exception`, `checks/standards/profile.py:153`); `profile_digest` and `profile_is_example` recorded; the file re-hashed before each attempt. Why: a study grading no standards wastes paid runs; a mid-run edit splits a run. Alternatives: PP-5 B; preflight in the shared resolver.
- **RUN-05** (PP-6). Decision: `--pane-defaults`, `--payload-slimming` and `--history-pruning` declare a study for P-3; `--standards-profile` alone does not. Alternatives: PP-6 B.
- **RUN-06** (FR-037, US7-4, §8.10's "to resolve": stream activity). Decision: each attempt on a worker; at W (`--package-time-limit`, default 60 min) active means any sink event within A = 600 s or a tool call in progress; a stop raised at the next tool boundary through the pane's `StoppableTools`, moved to `agent/runner.py` with `ReviewRun.end_stopped()`; a worker not back within G = 900 s is abandoned, never promoted. Why: a limit independent of the stream; one stop mechanism; A is the OpenAI library's read timeout, G one response at the output ceiling. Alternatives: a subprocess per attempt; SDK timeouts only; raw provider stream events. Refined by RF-3, RF-18 and RF-22 (§9.9), RF-50 (§9.10) and RF-71 (§9.11: no client close).
- **RUN-07** (FR-038, US7-1). Decision: request-level retries inside the adapters, only before the request emitted any event; `agent/providers/retry.py` `RetryPolicy` with full-jitter backoff raised to `Retry-After`; `None` keeps each library's behaviour byte for byte; `BENCHMARK_RETRY = (5, 2 s, 60 s)`; counted on the stream (`retries` on usage and error bodies, only under a policy). Why: rate limits handled without discarding paid rounds; exact counts; no duplicated text; the pane untouched. Alternatives: a package-level re-run; counting library retries through HTTP hooks. Refined by RF-20 (§9.9): the loop's placement, `use_retry`, and the stop before each retry; RF-35 (§9.10) weighs library retries counted through hooks, and RF-52 scopes "one error per failed request".
- **RUN-08** (FR-039, US7-3). Decision: `served_model` on usage bodies; `benchmark/served.py`'s aliasing table (dated OpenAI snapshots, numbered Gemini versions) shared with pricing; outside the aliases the attempt ends at the next tool boundary as `wrong_model`; unreported fails nothing. Why: fail at round one, not after a review; unknown is not a mismatch. Alternatives: check at the end; exact equality; unreported as failure. Refined by RF-32 (§9.10): written on every stream, the pane's included.
- **RUN-09** (FR-040, D2, US7-2). Decision: an optional `stop_reason` on error bodies for provider-reported stops (Gemini finish reasons outside `FINISH_REASONS`; OpenAI incomplete other than `max_output_tokens`; OpenAI `context_length_exceeded`; Gemini's input-token-limit 400); one behavioural class `provider_stop` with the reason; a `ProviderError` base; adapters raise or return as today. Why: one typed signal on either provider; refines §8.10's "other" to one class with its reason. Alternatives: parse messages; make OpenAI return; a class per reason. The overflow rows are owner decision 26A. Refined by RF-32 and RF-52 (§9.10) and RF-72, RF-76 and RF-77 (§9.11: the base's shape; OpenAI's policy codes; the inferred Gemini overflow).
- **RUN-10** (FR-036, FR-041, D2, D16). Decision: infrastructure `provider_error`, `wrong_model`, `served_model_changed`, `wall_clock_silent`, `abandoned`, `internal_error`, `interrupted`; behavioural `max_steps`, `truncated`, `provider_stop`, `wall_clock`; completed; the first infrastructure condition wins, then the first behavioural stop. Why: every failure named; a harness bug or an interrupt is re-runnable. Alternatives: `internal_error` behavioural; no `interrupted`.
- **RUN-11** (FR-043, US7-5, rerun-appends-events). Decision: attempts in `<run>/attempts/<package>/<n>/`; a kept attempt recorded in `run-status.json` first, then renamed to `<run>/<package>/`; `attempt_facts()` and `package_outcome()` the only readers; `swreview benchmark resume` re-runs only infrastructure failures from the start, with its refusals. Why: nothing appended to a completed session; failed attempts kept with their billed usage. Alternatives: a pointer; per-package status files; a lock file. Refined by RF-22 (§9.9), and by RF-29, RF-41, RF-44 and RF-51 (§9.10).
- **RUN-12** (D11, FR-057, US6-2, US6-3). Decision: `prompts.json` written by the attempt body with the system prompt and every user message, before the first turn and at the end (confirmed by INT-1). Why: an answer turn's message is composed by code, so the recording does not hold it byte for byte. Alternatives: a new event; writing in `start_review`; rebuilding at report time.
- **RUN-13** (D10, FR-042, US7-6, SC-008). Decision: `benchmark/prices.yaml` (strict, per model input, cached input and output with source and date, optional tiers); `price_rounds()` per round from usage events; OpenAI cached input priced apart only when `CACHED_SHARE_PUBLISHABLE`; Gemini's addends with the zero-omission rule; model by the served id's alias; scripted runs synthetic; unknown names what is missing; package cost the kept attempt, run spend every attempt. Its optional `cache_write` price is superseded by INT-5. Why: Principle I; tiers need per-round input. Alternatives: tokens only; a cost range; totals. Refined by RF-8 (§9.9): no tiers; by RF-45 (§9.10): `Scorecard.price_table`; and by RF-79 (§9.11): a pane study refuses a model the table cannot price.
- **RUN-14** (rerun-appends-events, FR-043). Decision: `benchmark run` refuses an `--out` holding anything, pointing at resume. Alternatives: refuse only a folder with provenance.
- **RUN-15** (FR-012, SC-003, §4 "bridge"). Decision: the parity test's `start_review` arguments differ only in named transport fields; the benchmark passes `bridge=False`, recorded as `live_tools`. Why: a new argument breaks the test until classified. Alternatives: normalise defaults in the test. Refined in §9.10's corrections: `package_dir` and `out_dir` are transport.
- **RUN-16** (FR-036, provider-error noise). Decision: one adapter built and the effort mapped before any review; a missing key or unoffered effort refused once; provenance and the saved set written before any review. Alternatives: let each package fail. Refined by RF-60 (§9.11): that adapter is the first attempt's.
- **PROV-01** (FR-013, US2-3, US2-9, D14.3). Decision: `RunProvenance` gains `provenance_schema` (2), `pane_defaults`, `model_view`, `profile_digest`, `profile_is_example`, `live_tools`, `inert_settings`, `max_answer_turns`, `prompt_digest`, `tree_dirty`, the three clock values, `retry_policy`; never a path; absent fields read not recorded. Why: inputs recorded once before any review; `profile_is_example` recorded because the example may change. Alternatives: recompute the example digest at compare time.
- **PROV-02** (US2-6, D5, PP-9, §4 "other levers unchecked"). Decision: must-match is one loop over `RunProvenance.model_fields` less named exemptions, a structural test partitioning every field. Why: a new field must match until exempted by name. Alternatives: extend the six-field tuple (`compare.py:500-502`). Refined by RF-10 (§9.9): `arm` is exempt across every study's arms.
- **PROV-03** (PP-3, US2-4). Decision: compare checks the on arm by the typed marker and refuses one whose settings equal the off arm's; the off arm against constants; the session cross-check extended to the model view; no re-derivation. Alternatives: PP-3 B.
- **PROV-04** (PP-4, D5, US2-5, §4 "baseline branch fires with no arm"). Decision: the pane baseline is `--study none --arm baseline --pane-defaults`, labelled `none (pane defaults)`, its own row and sign-off; the plain baseline refuses model-view switches; compare's baseline branch applies to `arm == baseline` only. Alternatives: PP-4 B, C.
- **PROV-05** (FR-056, 23A, US5-15, FR-027, D5). Decision: two studies, `pane_defaults` (committed set, the example profile, no private package) and `pane_defaults_private` (the real profile, private packages, `--out` outside the repository); both need a typed arm, a known commit and a clean tree. Alternatives: one study keyed by set name; dirty trees allowed.
- **PROV-06** (§4 "false sentence"; digest against findings). Decision: compare cross-checks every standards stamp's sha256 against `profile_digest` through one parser `profile_sha256()` that reads old and new stamps. Why: every standards finding stamps the digest (`checks/standards/report.py:258`). Alternatives: no cross-check. Refined by RF-5 (§9.9): the parser is `stamped_profile_digest`.
- **PROV-07** (D12, FR-051). Decision: `PROFILE_LIMIT` (`checks/standards/report.py:92`) stamps `profile:{file name} sha256:{digest}`; `ProfileIdentity.path` unchanged; standards goldens and replay fixtures regenerate. Alternatives: keep study folders out; scrub before commit. Refined by RF-28 (§9.10): the add-in's pane fixture regenerates in the same commit.
- **PROV-08** (FR-051, US2-11, RR-B0, §8.8 path half). Decision: the saved set names in-checkout paths relative to the running checkout root with `/`, absolute otherwise; LF bytes; `set_digest` over those bytes; `load_saved_set(path, repo_root)`. Why: today every path is absolute (`sets.py:51-58`) and `write_text` writes CRLF on Windows (`cli.py:2314`). Alternatives: relative to the set file's grandparent; relative vault paths. Refined by RF-4 (§9.9): one `load_set(path, *, root)` core, `load_saved_set` a thin caller; and by RF-54 (§9.10): `file_form`.
- **PROV-09** (FR-051, US2-10, §8.8). Decision: committable is inside the checkout and not ignored; `benchmark run` refuses a committable `--out` over private packages or another profile; compare refuses the same when writing somewhere committable; run folders render repository-relative. Alternatives: withdraw permission to commit study folders.
- **PROV-10** (US2-11, D5 digests). Decision: `.gitattributes` `eol=lf` for the example profile, the checklist, the prompts and `benchmarks/**`; digests over raw bytes; a test checks the attribute and the bytes (strengthened by INT-3). Alternatives: normalise inside digests.
- **PROV-11** (PP-7, §4 schema and order). Decision: `RunRow` gains `model_view`, `profile_digest`, `live_tools`, `pane_defaults`; three columns after "explanations"; one bump to 1.1 (the full order is INT-10's). Alternatives: PP-7 B; two bumps.
- **PROV-12** (FR-052, D14.5, US2-8). Decision: `INERT_SETTINGS` beside `pane_efficiency` (Gemini: parallel calls, dropped reasoning), recorded per run; `compare.settings_differed(off, on)`. Alternatives: infer from the provider at verdict time.
- **PROV-13** (D14.9, US2-9). Decision: the handoff allowlist gains every new provenance field; `_session_summary` gains `model_view`; `OPTIONAL_ARTIFACTS` gains `run-status.json`, `prompts.json`, `answer-log.json`. Alternatives: export without an allowlist. Refined by RF-42 (§9.10): the allowlist per slice, and the export's folder.
- **PROV-14** (FR-051's test, §8.8, RR3-5). Decision: a scanner in `tests/support/local_paths.py` flags the checkout, home and temp paths, saved sets with absolute entries and rooted paths outside the named fictional roots (read from their sources), over committable files and an offline run in a temporary checkout. Alternatives: fail on every rooted path. Refined by RF-6 (§9.9): drive-letter and UNC paths through `scramble.WINDOWS_ANCHOR`; RF-41 (§9.10) adds the writer's half; RF-78 (§9.11) commits the study and pilot event streams, which the scanner then reads, packed since 28A and scanned decompressed (RF-84, §9.12).

### 9.4 Cases and the generator

- **CS-1** (FR-027, D14.1). Decision: case specs are Python frozen dataclasses under `reviewer/tests/benchmark_cases/`, one module per design, ids written as place names resolved at render. Why: every existing generator is Python over the builders; no id typed twice. Alternatives: YAML specs plus an interpreter; a generator under `src/`.
- **CS-2** (FR-022, FR-030, SC-006). Decision: generated `benchmarks/packages/<case>/`, `answer_keys/`, `case_files/`, `sets/{committed,pilot}.json`; hand-kept `README.md`, `rms-part.json`, `native/rms-part/RECIPE.md`; the generator never deletes and fails on stale files. Alternatives: case files beside keys; hand-kept set files.
- **CS-3** (FR-010, §2 isolation residual). Decision: `cover-blind-tap`'s ingest inputs move byte for byte to `reviewer/tests/fixtures/ingest/cover-blind-tap/`, their tests and script re-pointed; `notes.md` dropped. Why: parser inputs are not a generated case; `notes.md` states the answer in a folder the reviewer loads. Alternatives: keep them beside the package.
- **CS-4** (§8.11, FR-028). Decision: twin rule B, one twin per core case with every seed clean, each removal a correct condition (18 twins). Why: FR-028's "kept defects count only in their own case" holds with nothing to mark; 18 fewer packages per arm and repetition. Alternatives: rule A. Owner decision 24A.
- **CS-5** (FR-028, SC-004, 10A, 14A). Decision: nine core designs × two cases × two held-out model-produced defects = 36 (fastener 12, fit and stack 12, drawing 12); 15 demonstrated, 13 suspected, 8 unresolved; largest design 4 of 36. Why: the floor with a margin of 2 and every cap; interference and part role are code-decided. Alternatives: ten designs; exactly 34.
- **CS-6** (§3 seeds, FR-029). Decision: 11 anchor and analyst-found packages (`mir-sitting`, `mir-sitting-positive`, `kalo-fit-1`, `-2`, `-clean`, `kalo-contact`, `-positive`, `kalo-lightweight`, `kalo-empty`, `-positive`, `tessa-groups`); AF-1's and AF-2's model forms are `tessa-1` and `tessa-2`. Alternatives: one package per anchor.
- **CS-7** (15A tags). Decision: every R item tuned-on; every AF item held out, code forms included; every `cover-blind-tap` item tuned-on. Alternatives: AF code forms tuned-on. Refined by RF-80 (§9.11): enforced per item by the spec checks.
- **CS-8** (US5-16, drawing entries). Decision: a drawing-family defect's drawing document carries no other seed and no condition; the generator refuses a spec that breaks it. Why: `drawing.manufacturing_inputs` entries have no references. Alternatives: reference-level drawing entries. Refined by RF-12 (§9.9): a pilot-only, all-tuned-on spec is exempt.
- **CS-9** (FR-022, 9A, D3). Decision: only anchors carry case files; no follow-up messages. Alternatives: scripted core answers; a follow-up entry kind. Put to the owner as question 8, answered B (30A, §9.13); RF-14 (§9.9) states the rule.
- **CS-10** (§3 R-2, "Gaps and candidates"). Decision: in `mir-sitting` the assembly and plate get distinct stems. Why: a shared stem raises `hygiene.duplicate_part_number`, a finding no engineer judged. Alternatives: a shared stem keyed as forbidden or defect.
- **CS-11** (FR-054 (a) on R-5). Decision: `kalo-lightweight` keys `hygiene.component_not_resolved` on the pin document as a code-produced, tuned-on defect. Why: it is true of the package (`checks/hygiene.py:259-301`). Alternatives: a forbidden entry.
- **CS-12** (FR-031, 15A). Superseded by INT-3: the stamp is `sha256_of` of raw bytes through `answer_key.current_stamp()`, the checkouts made to agree by `eol=lf`. Kept from CS-12: every committed held-out seed uses `stamps.CURRENT`, and a stamp test fails on a stale committed item, listing items and the new digests; private keys are listed by the summary.
- **CS-13** (FR-030, FR-032, D7, §8.7). Decision: `BenchmarkPackageRef` gains optional `key`, `case_file`, `design`, `twin_of`, `represents`; committed files carry `key`, `held_out`, `design`; `held_out` derived; `case_file_sha256` computed at load, only in the saved set; old sets fall back to `--answer-keys`. Alternatives: keys only through a directory; metadata in keys. Refined by RF-4 (§9.9): keyless and keyed sets.
- **CS-14** (FR-030, §8.8 set half). Decision: relative paths resolve only for set files under `benchmarks/sets/`; an entry outside the root is private; `saved_form()` writes in-root paths repository-relative. Alternatives: root from the code location; relative private sets. Superseded in its root by RF-4 (§9.9): the running checkout's root decides.
- **CS-15** (FR-030, US4-9, US5-11, D7). Decision: withheld paths are every named key and case file and their folders plus the fixed segments; a package at or under one, or a key or case file under a package, is refused. Why: both directions. Alternatives: the segment guard alone.
- **CS-16** (US5-3). Decision: before any review every `package.json` exists (all missing named) and every named key exists (stat only); placed in `preflight_set` by INT-14. Alternatives: fail at the first missing package; check keys only at score.
- **CS-17** (§8.6, FR-054, US5-8, US5-9, US5-16). Decision: leg (a) under pane defaults also fails a status mismatch on a code defect; leg (b) under off and pane also fails model-origin false alarms, contradictions, mismatches, flagged-below-expected, clears and unconfirmed twin conditions; decoys fail on any credit, flag or clear; meta-tests prove each leg fails. Why: FR-054's two checks as worded pass keys that punish a correct review. Alternatives: FR-054 as worded. Refined by RF-38 (§9.10): leg (a)'s call set.
- **CS-18** (FR-033, US5-2). Decision: `swreview benchmark set-summary <set>` prints the per-family summary, floor, largest share and stale items; exit 1 on a flag/key mismatch; below the floor printed, not refused. Alternatives: the generator prints it; fold into `score`.
- **CS-19** (FR-010, US5-4, D8, d1-gold-ambiguous). Decision: `cover-blind-tap` stays PDF-ingest-shaped with hole and fastener rows and ingested sheets; D-1 model-produced (withheld `joint_placement`), expected unresolved over four joints; D-2 on the housing drawing; a drill-depth-only callout; `pilot.json` only. Alternatives: a native-shaped package with placed screws. Refined by RF-12 (§9.9): C-1 names both drawing documents.
- **GN-1** (D14.1, SC-006). Decision: the generator is `tests.benchmark_cases`; `render_all()` returns path to bytes; `main()` checks everything in memory before writing. Alternatives: under `src/`.
- **GN-2** (16A joints; `answer-key.md` 2.5 row 13). Decision: `PackageBuilder.screw(fastener_row=False)`; the generator passes true, writing an `fst:NNNN` Toolbox row. Why: `check_fastener_joint` and `list_fasteners` resolve only package rows (`tools/context.py:192, 430`; `tools/query.py:596`); the default keeps fixtures byte-identical. Alternatives: rows only on model-variant screws.
- **GN-3** (FR-027, D14.1). Decision: designs compose `PackageBuilder`, `part_trees` (extracted from `rms_package`, `features.py:636-656`), `model_copy` overlays, `DrawingBuilder` candidates and `SheetBuilder` sheets; `card` moves to `tests/support/standards.py`. Why: extract on the second use; `standards_fixtures` imports the standards run (`golden_standards.py:39`). Alternatives: base on `rms_package`; duplicate `card`.
- **GN-4** (SC-006). Decision: `package_id = uuid5(CASE_NAMESPACE, case_id)`, `created_at = mechanical.CREATED_AT`, IR pinned 1.6.0, JSON UTF-8 LF indent 2; `as_committed` moves to `tests/support/mechanical.py`. Alternatives: follow `SCHEMA_VERSION`. Refined by RF-55 (§9.10): both of its copies.
- **GN-5** (§8.9, SC-006). Decision: direct imports allowlisted: `swreview.ir`, `ir.models`, `checks.standards.profile`, `benchmark.answer_key`, `benchmark.case_file`, `benchmark.sets`, `benchmark.families`; any `tests.checks` import refused; the case-file model in `benchmark/case_file.py` (INT-6). Alternatives: transitive checking; keys as plain dicts. Refined by RF-1 (§9.9): the key's format is allowlisted as the data-only `answer_key_model`, and the four format modules are checked transitively.
- **GN-6** (FR-048, D13, SC-011). Decision: `CASE_VOCABULARY` = `DRAWING_VOCABULARY` plus the example profile's tokens plus short noun, callout and case-id lists; names held to it on every machine; sentences checked only against the owner's denylist, skipped where absent. Alternatives: hold prose to the vocabulary; a denylist scan in the generator.
- **GN-7** (§8.14, FR-027). Decision: `SheetBuilder` in `tests/support/drawings.py` shares one drawing-document writer with `DrawingBuilder`; mm only; `dim-<page>-<n>`; unreadable sheets write `no_text` and their gap; misuse refused. Alternatives: a separate module; methods on `DrawingBuilder`.
- **GN-8** (FR-028 twins, D8(b), US5-16). Decision: twins derived by `twin(spec)`; correct calls and decoys only in the specs. Alternatives: hand-written twins; a committed correct-reviewer script.
- **GN-9** (§8.12's open point). Decision: reference-keyed defects and conditions use exact checks; families only on forbidden entries (with AK-4). Alternatives: `fastener.*` for D-1.

### 9.5 Questions and scripted answers

- **QA-1** (FR-023, 9A). Decision: `benchmark run` drives each package through `start_review`, `ReviewRun.start` and one `answer_evidence_batch` per answer point, the method the pane's batch route calls (`chat/server.py:1384-1399`); `_review_fn` switches from `run_review` to the driver; every package goes through it. Alternatives: drive the chat server over HTTP; answer turns in `run_review`; the replay's `_drive`.
- **QA-2** (D3, FR-024, US4-2 to US4-4). Decision: skip is nothing sent; a point with no delivery ends the review; deliveries go as one batch per point in session order. Why: the page sends nothing for a skipped question (`app.js:2233-2242`). Alternatives: send "skip"; one request per turn. Refined by RF-13 (§9.9): an entry is delivered again whenever a question asked again matches it.
- **QA-3** (D14.3, D16, FR-025, FR-013, US4-5). Decision: `--max-answer-turns N` (default 3; 0 is the opening turn only); `max_answer_turns` in provenance, absent reading not recorded; bound-reached when a delivery is ready at the point after the N-th answer turn. Alternatives: any open request at the bound; the opening turn counted; absent as 3.
- **QA-4** (D2, US7-7, FR-036). Decision: the driver answers only after a turn ended `end` (read by a sink listener); otherwise `turn_did_not_end`, the log written in a `finally`. Alternatives: answer after a cut-short turn.
- **QA-5** (US4-8). Decision: a part-roles entry is refused when `roles_question(classify_parts(...))` is `None`. Why: the product's classifier cannot disagree with the review. Alternatives: refuse when `profile.part_roles is None`; no check. The spec's US4-8 now reads the same (§9.11).
- **QA-6** (FR-022, FR-030, US4-9, D7). Decision: committed case files at `benchmarks/case_files/<id>.json`, named by the entry's `case_file`; the guard's segments gain `benchmarks/case_files`; only `load_case_file` reads one. Alternatives: beside keys; lookup by convention.
- **QA-7** (US4-6, FR-023, D5). Decision: `case_file_sha256` reaches the saved set and so `set_digest`, which must match within a study. Alternatives: a provenance digest with its own rule.
- **QA-8** (FR-023 parity). Decision: the answer is sent verbatim, trimmed; outside a closed option set it is still sent and marked `outside_options`. Alternatives: treat as unscripted; pick a matching option.
- **QA-9** (FR-024 to FR-026, D3, §8.3 timing). Decision: `answer-log.json` with points (`after_turn`, `steps_before`, sent, unscripted, held), `ended`, `last_turn_end`, `unused_entries`; ids only; the scorer reads it and never the case file; `ended` gains `stopped` (INT-7). Alternatives: timing from `events.jsonl`; no log.
- **QA-10** (FR-019, FR-020, D4). Decision: a request call is a `request_evidence` step read with its stored full result; `open` recorded, `already_answered` and `already_asked` refused with the cited id, `closed_by_code` refused; error answers are not questions; a missing or foreign stored result makes the call columns not assessed. Alternatives: pair by arguments; parse `result_summary`; bracket events; count errors. Refined by RF-30 (§9.10): the one `read_stored_result`.
- **QA-11** (§8.3 ids, refined). Decision: holes and fasteners resolve to their instance, a document to every instance of it (itself when none); unanswerable entries use the product's document rule, `documents_named` made public. Why: the model names a part by document or instance interchangeably. Alternatives: §8.3 as written; everything to documents.
- **QA-12** (§8.3; 013 re-ask guard). Decision: only model-written questions are earlier questions. Why: product parity; the part-roles question would otherwise make re-asks of unrelated questions. Alternatives: include code questions.
- **QA-13** (FR-053, D4, §8.3). Decision: one relation `asks_within` serves re-asks, repeats, scripted answers and count rules; the product's covering rule widened only by id resolution and the item-only branch; a re-ask outranks a repeat. Alternatives: a rule per use; overlap. Refined by RF-6 (§9.9): the product's predicate is extracted as `report.session.covers`, which `asks_within` wraps.
- **QA-14** (§8.3 the part-roles question). Decision: `is_part_roles_question`: source code, the product's question text and options, no blocks; entity ids not compared. Why: the scorer has only the profile's digest. Alternatives: a question key on `EvidenceRequest`; rebuild the spec at scoring.
- **QA-15** (FR-060, US3-6). Decision: count rules of kind `part_roles` or item with optional ids; `min` 0 or 1, optional `max`; refused calls counted. Alternatives: recorded requests only; any `min`.
- **QA-16** (FR-019, US3-5, US3-6). Decision: an unanswerable entry names documents and an optional item; every matching call counts whatever its outcome. Alternatives: each request once.
- **QA-17** (FR-021, US3-8). Decision: call columns need every call readable; re-asks and repeats also an answer log when requests were answered; unscripted and bound-reached need a log; the log is checked against the session. Alternatives: missing data as zero.
- **QA-18** (Key Entities, SP-14, FR-059). Decision: `re_asks`, `open_repeats`, `unanswerable_requests`, `unmatched_requests`, `question_counts`, `unscripted_questions`, `bound_reached` on `PackageScore`, lists only; re-asks rendered seen and refused; none decides. Alternatives: separate seen and refused lists.
- **QA-19** (FR-009, SC-001). Decision: key-load refusals for overlapping unanswerable entries, overlapping count rules, and a `min` 1 rule whose oracle question would match an unanswerable entry. Alternatives: count once per call with a hidden precedence.
- **QA-20** (FR-022 against §3 R-2's follow-up). Decision: case files script answers only; the driver never calls `continue_session`. Alternatives: one scripted follow-up. Owner question 4, default kept (§9.7).
- **QA-21** (SC-001, FR-049, §8.15). Decision: the oracle holds one question per `min` 1 rule and no answer log; the validation writes probes for every question column and matching rule. Alternatives: oracle and null only.

### 9.6 Verdict, simulator, pilot and report

- **VD-1** (§8.1 interval, FR-044, RR4-m16). Decision: one analytic package-level t interval, `stats.package_interval`, ratio-linearised so recall stays a per-defect mean after drops; not computable when K < 2 or SE = 0; two-sided 95% for every shown interval. Why: no resample seed, one evaluation per simulated study; an offline check put it at 2.3 to 2.6% per tail; the level search absorbs the rest. Alternatives: a studentised bootstrap; the percentile bootstrap.
- **VD-2** (§8.1 levels, SC-005). Decision: one one-sided `alpha` for the four paired tests from the ladder 0.025, 0.02, 0.015, 0.01, 0.005, 0.0025, the loosest holding both bounds; fixed in the approval. Alternatives: separate levels; a continuous root-find.
- **VD-3** (§8.2 Monte Carlo bound). Decision: the one-sided 95% Wilson upper bound; 176 of 4,000 pass, 180 fail. Alternatives: Clopper-Pearson.
- **VD-4** (§8.2 marginal rates, RR4-2). Decision: intercepts solved by a 40-node Gauss-Hermite rule on a table of 3,999 rates per SD, read by interpolation. Alternatives: solve per defect.
- **VD-5** (DRY between compare and the simulator). Decision: `verdict.judge(StudyStats, rules)` is the one rule order; compare and the simulator both call it; test V-25 holds them equal. Alternatives: a vectorised copy; compare's code run 4,000 times.
- **VD-6** (§8.1 cleared guard, D17). Decision: search the size with the guard named only, then test it deciding at that size; it decides only if some `alpha` holds both bounds and 80% power. Alternatives: RR-B3(a) (owner question 7, default kept, §9.7).
- **VD-7** (§8.1's "to resolve": a narrower code guard). Decision: the stress case is printed in the cost-gate record; no narrower guard is designed now. Why: whether it is large is known only after the pilot. Alternatives: design one now.
- **VD-8** (§8.13 estimation noise). Decision: rates by a Beta fit by moments with binomial noise removed; co-movement by a profile likelihood over SD 0 to 2.5, sizing at the largest SD within the 80% limit; false alarms negative binomial by moments or Poisson. Alternatives: plug-in estimates; a parametric bootstrap; maximum-likelihood beta-binomial. Refined by RF-9 (§9.9): defects with fewer than two counted runs are left out of `V`, named.
- **VD-9** (§8.13 pilot size). Decision: 3 repetitions of the off arm over the committed set; `benchmark pilot` refuses fewer than 2. Alternatives: 2; 5. Owner decision 27A.
- **VD-10** (§8.13 pilot runs as repetitions). Decision: pilot runs never enter the study; compare shows them as raw rows marked `pilot`. Why: data that chose the size would otherwise be judged. Alternatives: reuse them, withdrawn by RF-57 (§9.11) as incompatible with the approval rule. Owner question 6, default kept (§9.7). Refined by RF-11 (§9.9), which RF-36 (§9.10) supersedes: the pilot's folders live under `benchmarks/pilots/`, so no pilot run meets a study's checks.
- **VD-11** (FR-055, FR-032). Decision: `pilot-measurement.json` with identity, runs, measured, sizing, a digest and a hand-set approval; compare refuses a digest mismatch, an unapproved record, a late approval, or a study below the approved size. Alternatives: a provenance field naming the approval. Refined by RF-37 and RF-40 (§9.10), and RF-65 and RF-66 (§9.11).
- **VD-12** (FR-055, 22A, SC-005). Decision: repetitions grow from 3 to the cap, then cases one growth unit at a time to a ceiling of 200 defects; never below 34 × 3; every size printed. The growth unit is INT-4's. Alternatives: bisection over cases.
- **VD-13** (noise floor, D14.8). Decision: simulated on the study's own layout and repetitions, shrunk rates, plug-in SD, the approved `alpha` and seed; the smallest drop on a 0.5-point grid with 80% power. Alternatives: 2.8 × SE. Refined by RF-53 (§9.10): simulated once and stored beside the study.
- **VD-14** (23A, FR-056 compare half). Decision: the private slice's study renders as its own row with every interval and no verdict; its slice is read from its study name (INT-13). Alternatives: slice from the sets (superseded). Refined by RF-24 (§9.9).
- **VD-15** (FR-032, US5-5, FR-007). Decision: verdict refusals in order (arm missing; a private package in `pane_defaults`; no origin-marks record; below 34; no or two pilot records; digest; unapproved; late; below size); each refuses the verdict only, raw rows written, exit 1. Alternatives: abort the ledger. Refined by SR-25 and RF-24 (§9.9).
- **VD-16** (§4 "one bump or two"). Decision: one bump for the feature; the pane section rendered only when present (with INT-10).
- **VD-17** (FR-046, US8-8). Decision: intervals analytic; the noise floor's draws from numpy PCG64 with the approval's seed; the numpy version printed. Alternatives: a hand-rolled PRNG. Refined by RF-53 (§9.10): compare draws nothing.
- **VD-18** (SC-010 test placement). Decision: the default run holds the mechanics and three full scenarios; the 39-scenario sweep is opt-in (`-m simulation`), run before any approval. Alternatives: the sweep in the default run.
- **VD-19** (dependencies). Decision: no scipy; `t_quantile` by bisection through the regularised incomplete beta, pinned against six published values; quadrature from numpy. Alternatives: add scipy. Refined by RF-34 (§9.10): the closed-form series for integer degrees of freedom.
- **RP-1** (FR-034, SC-009). Decision: `benchmark/report_page.py` writes static HTML, no script, no external resource; no Node, no JSONL renderer. Why: the scorecard stays the only writer of every number; `file://` pages cannot fetch; the pilot workstation cannot serve loopback. Alternatives: JSONL plus a JS renderer; Markdown.
- **RP-2** (§8.13 the report's reader). Decision: transcripts walk `events.jsonl` in order; only the line reader is shared (refactored into a generator). Why: `_EventReader` drops calls outside a round (`recording.py:267-274`), losing the regrade's calls. Alternatives: share `_EventReader` extended. Refined by RF-29 (§9.10): the shared reader is the product's `replay_events`.
- **RP-3** (FR-057, D11). Superseded by INT-1: the record is written by the benchmark attempt, not by `_ask` on every review, and no `coverage.prompts` row exists.
- **RP-4** (FR-034). Decision: packages found through the saved set, relative entries against this checkout, absolute as written; the package supplies only a component legend; a missing package is a note, never a refusal. Alternatives: rewrite ids to names; refuse.
- **RP-5** (D10 cache-write). Decision: cache-write tokens counted, never priced (confirmed by INT-5); totals sum-or-none; failed attempts' billed cost shown apart. Alternatives: a fourth price column.
- **RP-6** (SC-009). Decision: full results embedded as escaped text, stale ones named; a fixed perf fixture of 800 transcripts under 60 s; linking results is the owner's fallback. Alternatives: link results; incremental builds. Refined by RF-46 (§9.10): the budget test builds 940 transcripts.
- **RP-7** (FR-051, determinism). Decision: `--out` required; an empty folder written; a previous report recognised by a meta tag and replaced; anything else refused; relative links; no timestamp. Alternatives: always clear; stamp the build time.

### 9.7 Owner questions

Each changes scope, cost or what the study measures. On 2026-09-29 the owner answered questions 1, 2, 3 and 5 (24A to 27A, §1) and kept the recommended default of questions 4, 6 and 7; on 2026-09-30 the owner answered question 8 with its option B (30A, §9.13). Answered and kept questions gate nothing.

| # | Question | Options | Recommended, and why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Twins (§8.11, CS-4): one fully clean twin per core case, or one twin per removed family? | A: rule B, one twin with both seeds clean, each removal a condition (47 packages; 94 × R paid reviews per study). B: rule A, one twin per removed family keeping the other defect, marked "counted elsewhere" (65 packages; 130 × R; a new key marker) | **A (rule B)**: about 38% fewer paid reviews; twins feed only the false-alarm rule, never recall power; no key-format change; research §8.11 names the stricter twin as a valid resolution | answered: A (24A) |
| 2 | Held-out stamp scope (15A): should `held_out_against` also digest the tool descriptions the model reads? | A: also a digest of every registered tool's description, forcing the same cite-or-restamp decision on a docstring change. B: the prompt and checklist files only, as 15A says | **A**: the tool docstrings state review policy (research §2, prompt-encodes-cbt-answer), so tuning one on a held-out defect would leave it held out under B; such changes are rare and already pinned (`test_tool_payload`). It amends 15A | answered: A (25A), applied in slice 1, which defines `Stamp` (RF-64) |
| 3 | A context overflow (OpenAI `context_length_exceeded`, Gemini's input-token-limit 400): behavioural or infrastructure? | A: behavioural `provider_stop` with its reason, as D2 treats step and output limits. B: infrastructure, excluded and re-run. C: infrastructure first, behavioural if the resumed attempt overflows again | **A**: two pane defaults shrink the context, so the off arm is likelier to overflow; excluding its overflowing runs would remove the off arm's worst runs, the survivorship bias D2 exists to prevent | answered: A (26A) |
| 4 | Should case files also script R-2's follow-up message ("what should the drawing improve?")? | A: no follow-ups; R-2 graded on what the model records unprompted. B: one scripted follow-up per case through the pane's message path, within the bound | **A**, the spec as written (FR-022): R-2's prose is not graded (8A) and it is tuned-on; B adds a driver path, a case-file field and paid turns for a regression guard | default A kept |
| 5 | The paid pilot's size (FR-055)? | A: 3 repetitions of the off arm over the committed set. B: 2 (co-movement barely estimable; sizing leans on its conservative upper value). C: 5 (about 1.7 × the pilot's cost, tighter estimates) | **A**: the minimum that shows co-movement; at least 102 defect-trials; VD-8's conservative SD covers the rest | answered: A (27A) |
| 6 | Do the pilot's off-arm runs also count as the study's first off-arm repetitions? | A: no; they render as raw rows marked `off (pilot)`, in no count | **A**: keeps both 5% bounds honest, and is the only reading the approval rule allows: counting them was withdrawn (RF-57), since the pilot's folders are committed with the approval before any study run (quickstart P3), so its runs carry an earlier `commit` (and another `set_digest` when growth units are added) and a `started_at` before `approved_at`, which the must-match loop and `verdict.md` 7's refusal 8 refuse | default A kept |
| 7 | How do cleared defects enter the verdict (D17)? | A: the default: the simulator decides at the cost gate whether the cleared guard decides, and prints which (expected "named only"). B: RR-B3(a): the guard on code-produced defects only, model-produced clears a third paired rule inside the 5% budget | **A**: keeps Principle I's worst outcome visible on every verdict without spending power; B needs more repetitions or cases for the same 80% | default A kept |
| 8 | Should held-out cases take an answer turn, so the verdict can see the pane default that acts only between turns (lever 14, dropped reasoning, `agent/settings.py:482-488`; RF-14)? Under CS-9 every held-out defect sits in a core case with no case file, so each held-out review is one turn, and on OpenAI the verdict cannot see one of the six settings the arms differ in; 9A chose scripted answers because one turn leaves dropped reasoning unobservable | A: every core case and its twin (36 packages) scripts one answer to its likeliest question (the fit or the fastener item), worded so it states nothing about a planted defect: at most one answer turn per package run, and only when the model asks it; up to 36 × 2 × R = 72 × R more paid turns per study (216 at R = 3) beside the 94 × R reviews, each turn re-sending the review's history, and 36 answers for the case author to write and the validation to run. B: CS-9 as is; the verdict names dropped reasoning as a setting it does not test ("acts only between turns; no held-out case takes an answer turn: the anchors show it"), and the anchors show it with paired intervals (FR-059). C: a follow-up entry kind, one scripted follow-up message per core case: a driver path, a case-file field and paid turns, as question 4's B | **B**: a held-out review that takes no answer turn is inert to lever 14 in the pane as well, so the verdict is not wrong about the reviews it measures, and B states the gap on every verdict; A's answers are the case author's words beside the planted defects, a risk to the held-out figure the study exists to protect, at up to 72 × R paid turns | answered: B (30A) |

### 9.9 Review fixes

Taken on 2026-09-29 after a review of the plan and its contracts. Each entry: what it resolves, the decision, why, and the alternatives; the entry it refines ends with a pointer here, and every contract it touches was edited to match. They are reversible plan-level defaults under the owner decisions, except RF-14, which 30A made the rule (§9.13). Line numbers are at `be01974`.

- **RF-1** (GN-5 against INT-6; FR-027, SC-006). Decision: the key's format moves to a data-only `benchmark/answer_key_model.py`: the entry models, `Stamp`, the check-pattern grammar, the table of reference-keyed checks and their kinds, and the validators that need nothing but the model (`answer-key.md` 4, rows 1, 3, 4, 5, 7 and row 6's grammar). `benchmark/answer_key.py` keeps `load_answer_key`, `current_stamp()` and the rows that need other modules: 2 (the file stem, the set entry), 6's typo guard (`goal_of`), 8 (`benchmark/matching.py`), 9 and 10 (the checklist, `goal_of`), 11 (`benchmark/questions.py`, built on `tools/context.context_for`, whose module imports `agent.checklist`, `agent.providers` and `agent.settings`, `tools/context.py:34-36`, and on `tools/session.documents_named`), 12, 13 (`tools/refs.resolve_dimension`) and 14. The generator's allowlist names `answer_key_model` in place of `answer_key`. `SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE`, defined only in `agent/runner.py:143` (a module importing `tools.session`, `:141`), moves beside `CHECKLIST_FILE` in `agent/checklist.py` (`:20`), and `agent/runner.py` re-exports it, so `current_stamp()` names both files without importing the runner. `test_benchmark_generator_imports.py` also imports each data-only format module (the key's format, `case_file`, `sets`, `families`) alone in a fresh interpreter and fails when `sys.modules` then holds any `swreview.tools`, `swreview.agent` or `swreview.checks` module. Why: the loader imports the matcher, the question reader and tool logic, so allowlisting it loaded them through the key, the reason INT-6 split `case_file.py` out; `answer_key.py` imports only `findings.ReviewModel` today (`:15`), so the direct-import test alone would never notice. Imported alone at `be01974`, `benchmark.sets` loads 8 `swreview` modules and none under `tools`, `agent` or `checks`. Alternatives: allow the loader, checking direct imports only (FR-027 broken transitively); keys as plain dicts (a second format definition). RF-48 (§9.10) adds `benchmark.digest` to the modules imported alone.
- **RF-2** (SR-5 against Principle III; RK-1; the plan's freeze). Decision: 19A's commit regenerates five check goldens, `joint-bottoming`, `joint-ok`, `joint-unsupported`, `thread-mismatch` and `tool-envelope` (`joint-unsupported.yml:3-7` pins `fastener.unsupported` with `inputs: [fst:1]`, which `_unsupported` changes, `checks/fastener.py:700`), and adds one, `joint-unknown-role`, the joint map's alignment checks on a joint with an instance of unknown role: no check golden pins a joint-map alignment result today, and `_unknown_role` (`checks/joint_alignment.py:222`, called at `:291, 508`) changes. The replay fixtures are regenerated in that commit only to assert they stay byte-identical: their recordings call no fastener rule and no joint map (their fastener and alignment tools are `list_fasteners` and `check_hole_alignment`), so 19A moves none of their bytes; 013's freeze applies to D12 alone, whose old stamp (`profile:..\config\standards.example.yaml`) they carry. Why: Principle III asks a changed check for a golden; a freeze with nothing to freeze serializes work for nothing. Alternatives: a Complexity Tracking exception for the unknown-role path; keep the freeze for 19A. Refined by RF-27 and RF-28 (§9.10).
- **RF-3** (RUN-06's "one stop mechanism"; `benchmark/replay.py:253-259, 452-455`). Decision: in the robustness slice the replay's `_RecordingTools.call` raises the shared `TurnStopped` in place of `_Stop`, and `_drive` closes a stopped turn with `run.end_stopped()`. Why: the replay held a third copy that already differs from the pane's (it skips `cancel_presentation`, `chat/server.py:2253-2257`). Alternatives: leave the copy (three stop close-outs, two of them different). Refined by RF-33 (§9.10): no replay fixture ends a turn stopped, so the call is unconditional.
- **RF-4** (CS-14 against PROV-08; FR-030, FR-051, US2-11). Decision: one root, the running checkout's `REPO_ROOT`, moved from `benchmark/runner.py:52` to `benchmark/sets.py` (the runner imports it back; `benchmark/runner.py:40` already imports `sets`), and a keyword parameter of one core, `load_set(path, *, root=REPO_ROOT)`; `load_saved_set(path, *, root=REPO_ROOT)` is a thin caller of that core that also accepts relative entries outside `benchmarks/sets/` and each entry's `case_file_sha256`. The root decides resolution, "private", `saved_form()` and the saved copy's reading. A relative path resolves only in a set file inside `<root>/benchmarks/sets/`; one in another checkout's or worktree's `benchmarks/sets/` is refused naming both roots. A set in which no entry names `key` is **keyless**, a set written before this feature: its keys come from `--answer-keys` and `cases.md` 4.3 row 6 does not apply; in a **keyed** set every entry names its key. `benchmark score`'s `--answer-keys` becomes optional in the generator slice, required only for a keyless set, and the re-score line reads "Re-score it: swreview benchmark score {run dir} (add --answer-keys <dir> when its set names no keys)". Why: two roots gave one set two saved forms, so two `set_digest`s, whenever a worktree ran a set file of another checkout, and classified one entry two ways; every existing test set names absolute temporary packages and no key (`tests/unit/test_cli.py:2373-2393`, `test_benchmark_runner.py:23-34`) and would have been refused as private without a key; a static re-score line needs no second file read on a refusal path. Alternatives: the set file's grandparent (CS-14 as drafted); resolve another checkout's set against its own grandparent (two roots again); rewrite every existing fixture with keys; a re-score line that reads the saved set to decide. Refined by RF-61 (§9.11): `run_benchmark` forwards a `root` in the parity slice.
- **RF-5** (PROV-06; `checks/tolerances.py:83, 313-317`). Decision: the stamp parser is `stamped_profile_digest(limits) -> str | None`, returning the full digest; the 12-character display is one helper, `display_digest(sha256) -> str` in `checks/standards/profile.py` beside `ProfileIdentity`, which `tolerances.profile_sha256`, the ledger's profile column and the resume and exit messages call. Why: `tolerances.profile_sha256(profile)` is public already and returns 12 characters; a second public `profile_sha256` returning 64 would make a wrong import refuse every study, and the display rule was restated in four places. Alternatives: keep the name and rely on the argument type.
- **RF-6** (SR-12, QA-13, PROV-14; Principle V). Decision: `CODE_ONLY_TOOLS = frozenset({*prerun.prerun_tools(), prerun.LIVE_INTERFERENCE_TOOL, drawings.DRAWINGS_TOOL, standards.registry.CHECK_TOOL})`, from the constants that already name them (`prerun.py:144-159, 209-213`; `tools/checks_mechanical.py:76`; `tools/drawings.py:67`; `checks/standards/registry.py:98`); the product's covering predicate, inline in `covering_requests` (`report/session.py:203-209`), is extracted as `report.session.covers(blocks, ids, earlier_blocks, earlier_ids)`, which `covering_requests` calls and `benchmark/questions.asks_within` wraps with id resolution and the item-only branch; `tests/support/scramble.py`'s `_WINDOWS_ANCHOR` (`:299`) becomes public `WINDOWS_ANCHOR`, which `tests/support/local_paths.py` reuses for drive-letter and UNC paths. Why: each rule was written twice, and a pre-run change needed two edits; with the extraction the agreement table QA-13's test kept between the two predicates has nothing left to check. Alternatives: literal sets held by structural tests (SR-12 as drafted); an agreement table (QA-13 as drafted). Refined by RF-38 (§9.10).
- **RF-7** (the plan's acceptance-suite row; the constitution's "5 to 10 representative designs"; spec Assumptions). Decision: the committed set holds 10 designs, the nine core designs of `cases.md` 6.2 and `mir` (`kalo` and `tessa` also hold anchors), within 5 to 10; the private slice's two real designs are 23A's own study, shown and never deciding; no Complexity Tracking row. Representativeness is recorded in the Constitution Check with its known gap: every committed design is a small assembly of two to five parts, while the pilot's real designs are large assemblies, where payload slimming and history pruning act most; the private slice covers that gap and is shown only. Why: the drafted count above 10 counted designs that never decide. Alternatives: justify 12 designs in Complexity Tracking (as drafted). Refined by RF-47 (§9.10): the spec's Assumptions line is cited as superseded.
- **RF-8** (RUN-13's tiers; FR-042, INT-5). Decision: the price table holds three prices per model and no `tiers`: no default model (`agent/settings.py:103-107`) is shown to need them, and a model whose public price is tiered gets no entry, so its cost reads unknown naming it, until a commit adds tiers with their test. Pricing stays per round for what needs it: the served model, aliased per round (`benchmark-run.md` 7); Gemini's zero-omission rule, read per round; and failed attempts priced from their own events. Why: tiers were prices FR-042 does not ask for, the configurability INT-5 refused for a fourth price. Alternatives: keep optional tiers.
- **RF-9** (VD-8; Principle V's edge cases, Principle III's invalid inputs). Decision: the pilot's Beta fit leaves out of `V` every held-out defect with fewer than two counted runs, naming and counting them in `measured`; one with none is dropped as `verdict.md` 3 drops it; `mu` reads every defect with at least one. `t_quantile`, `package_interval` and `mc_upper_bound` refuse invalid input with a `ValueError` naming it: degrees of freedom below 1, `alpha` outside (0, 0.5], `n` of 0, hits outside 0 to `n`. Why: `r_i(1 − r_i)/(n_i − 1)` divides by zero at one counted run and `r_i` is undefined at none, both reachable at 3 repetitions after infrastructure failures or behavioural stops. Alternatives: refuse the pilot (one failed run would void a paid pilot).
- **RF-10** (PROV-02; US2-6). Decision: `arm` is exempt across the arms of every study, beside `rep` and `started_at`; `efficiency`, `model_view` and `pane_defaults` stay exempt across a pane study's arms only, and the studied lever's flag within a lever study. Why: a lever study's off and on runs share one study key (`benchmark/compare.py:475-480`), so the loop would have refused every lever study on `arm`; US2-6 exempts "across arms, the arm" for any study. Alternatives: none.
- **RF-11** (VD-10; `benchmark/compare.py:483-534`). Decision: the runs a measurement lists as its pilot runs, matched through `--pilot` or `recorded_pilots()` on `started_at` and commit, are separated from their study before `_check_one_study` and `_check_repetitions`, and render as raw rows whose arm cell reads `off (pilot)`, within `provenance.md` 5's fixed columns. Why: pilot runs are the pane study's off arm at repetitions 1 to 3 under `benchmarks/studies/`, so `recorded_runs()` finds them; left in, the run-level checks abort the whole ledger on a repetition collision or an earlier commit. Alternatives: separate them before verdict refusal 1 only (as drafted: too late); a pilot study name (a new study key and must-match rule). Superseded by RF-36 (§9.10): the pilot's folders live under `benchmarks/pilots/`, which `recorded_runs()` never scans, so nothing is separated.
- **RF-12** (CS-8 against CS-19 and INT-15; `generator.md` 10 row 5). Decision: row 5 does not apply to a spec whose `sets` is `("pilot",)` alone and whose every item is tuned-on, which is `cover-blind-tap`: its housing sheet carries both D-2's drill-only callout and C-1's bore, as the package always has (`DRW-2001` `Sheet1` `dim-1-2` is the bore, the shaft `DRW-2002` `Sheet1` `dim-1-1`). C-1 names both drawing documents. Why: the rule keeps a drawing finding written about a fit or a stack from crediting a held-out drawing defect keyed by document; `cover-blind-tap` holds nothing out and is never in the pane study's set, and moving its bore would stop it being the package it regenerates. Alternatives: narrow row 5 to drawing-family entries (a model's drawing finding about a fit on the same sheet would then credit a held-out drawing defect of a core case); move the bore to an assembly sheet.
- **RF-13** (FR-022 and US4's Independent Test against QA-2). Decision: an entry is delivered at every answer point where it is the one entry matching an open request; nothing limits an entry to one delivery. A question asked again after its answer is answered again, as the pane's engineer would answer it, and the bound (`scripted-answers.md` 4) is what ends a model that keeps asking. `test_scripted_answers_flow.py` scripts R-3's refused follow-up (naming the pin's hole alone, answered `already_answered`), so it records one answer turn and one refused re-ask, the Independent Test's shape; re-delivery is pinned in `test_benchmark_answers.py`. Why: the bound test already relied on re-delivery, while the flow test as drafted expected one answer turn from an item-only follow-up that re-delivery answers again, which the scripted provider would refuse. Alternatives: "an entry answers each question lineage once" (a new rule and a lineage to define); a seen follow-up in the flow test with two answer turns.
- **RF-14** (CS-9 against 9A; lever 14). Decision: owner question 8 (§9.7). Until it was answered the plan proceeded on its option B: CS-9 stands, and the pane block names every setting the arms differed in that acts only between turns (`BETWEEN_TURN_SETTINGS = ("drop_prior_reasoning",)` in `agent/settings.py`, beside `INERT_SETTINGS`) as untested by the verdict whenever no saved-set entry holding a held-out item names a case file. Why: lever 14 acts only at a turn boundary (`agent/settings.py:482-488`) and every held-out defect is in a core case with no case file, so the verdict cannot see it; a verdict that says so is not over-claimed. Alternatives: question 8's A and C. Owner decision 30A (2026-09-30) answers question 8 with B, so this is the plan's rule, no longer an interim (§9.13).
- **RF-15** (SR-24 against SC-001's "every committed key with a known defect" and US1's Independent Test). Decision: the oracle builder (`oracle_review(key, package)` in `reviewer/tests/support/scoring_probes.py`) takes the key's package or, for a key-only key, a stand-in built from the key, `stand_in_package(key)`: `tests/support/packages.build_package` with one component instance per component id the key names and one document with its manifest entry per document id, so `build_finding` attaches provenance; nothing else about it is read, and the ids stay unchecked, as the key's are (US1-16). Oracle and null reviews are scored through the scorer's per-package function (`_score_package`, `benchmark/scorecard.py:363`, which `score_run` calls), since `score_run` refuses a key-only key. `test_committed_answer_keys.py` asserts SC-001 and SC-002 on both committed keys. Why: no test asserted SC-001 on `rms-part`, and the slice-1 gate could not be built for it. Alternatives: the owner amends SC-001's wording. Refined by RF-58 (§9.11).
- **RF-16** (INT-9 against `provenance.md` 2's served-model check; Principle III). Decision: `PackageScore.served_models`, the distinct served ids of the kept attempt's rounds in first-seen order (empty when no round reported one), is copied by the scorer from `attempt_facts` beside `run_status` and `cost` and is in `GOLDEN_EXCLUDED`; compare's served-model re-check reads it, so compare still reads only the scorecard (`benchmark-run.md` 10). Tests are named for the behaviours the contracts stated without one: the profile re-hashed before each attempt, a reader meeting an incomplete promotion, `unattended_runtime_minutes` from the kept attempt's span, compare's served-model re-check, and a pilot measurement matching no compared study. Why: every behaviour needs its failing test (Principle III), and the re-check as drafted had compare read attempt events. Alternatives: amend `benchmark-run.md` 10 to let compare call `attempt_facts`.
- **RF-17** (INT-9 against `report.md` 5 item 5). Decision: `Scorecard.failed_attempts` and `unscored_packages` rows carry the run-relative `folder` of their attempt (the last attempt's for an unscored package), copied through `attempt_facts` and `package_outcome`; the report opens `<run>/<folder>/events.jsonl` from it. Why: readers never read `run-status.json`, so the report had no path to a failed attempt's log. Alternatives: the report guesses `attempts/<package>/<n>/`.
- **RF-18** (RUN-06 against lever 7; `chat/server.py:445-466`, `agent/providers/__init__.py:539-567`). Decision: `StoppableTools` gains `tools_withdrawn()`, returning `providers.tools_withdrawn(self.tools)`, so a wrapped `CoverageStopTools` still asks for the withdrawal; `reopen` needs nothing, since the run keeps its own reference to the inner set (`agent/runner.py:1057`). Why: both adapters decide lever 7 through `isinstance(tools, WithdrawableTools)` (`openai_provider.py:444`, `gemini_provider.py:439`), which a wrapper without the method fails, so wrapping every attempt would silently turn lever 7 off in every `--lever coverage_stop` study, as the pane's wrapper already does. Alternatives: wrap the inner dispatch rather than the set (a second wrapper shape). Its test is RF-63's (§9.11).
- **RF-19** (RUN-01, RUN-03 against `benchmark run`'s refusals; `cli.py:2289-2295`). Decision: `_review_settings` gains keyword parameters `explain_findings: bool | None = None`, `study: str = NO_STUDY`, `arm: str | None = None` and `allow_workstation_levers: bool = True`; it passes the last three to `_efficiency`, and `check_study_arm` gains the typed markers the pane-study rules read (`pane_defaults`, the levers typed, whether a model-view switch was typed, whether an explanation setting was typed). `benchmark run` calls it with `allow_workstation_levers=False` and its study and arm; `review` and the replay keep today's call (`tests/unit/test_cli_benchmark_replay.py:171-200` unchanged). Why: `benchmark run` resolves through `_efficiency(..., allow_workstation_levers=False)` with its study and arm today, and routing it through `_review_settings` as drafted would have dropped the workstation-lever refusal (`tests/unit/test_efficiency_settings.py:556` expects "workstation harness", exit 2) and the study checks. Alternatives: a benchmark-only resolver (RUN-03's rejected second resolver).
- **RF-20** (RUN-07's placement and seam; FR-038). Decision: (a) the retry loop sits inside each adapter around the raw request and the iteration of its stream, and retries only while no `text.delta` has been emitted; `_failed`, the no-final-response check and `_mapped` (`openai_provider.py:573-579, 597-598, 708-740`) and Gemini's `_report` and `_emit` (`gemini_provider.py:564-565, 574-591`) build their error without emitting it, and the one `error` event, carrying `retries` under a policy, is emitted after the final failure; Gemini's transient transport errors, which it does not map today, are the SDK's `httpx.TimeoutException`, `httpx.NetworkError` and `httpx.RemoteProtocolError`, retryable under a policy only. (b) A policy reaches an adapter through an optional `use_retry(policy, *, stop_requested)`, an extension like `use_model_view` and `use_prompt_cache` (`agent/providers/__init__.py:501, 518`), which the attempt body calls on the adapter it builds, which turns the library's own retries off (`with_options(max_retries=0)` on OpenAI) and which `for_presentation` copies; `provider_factory`'s signature is unchanged. (c) The loop asks `stop_requested()` before each retry and raises the failure as it is once the stop is set. Why: an `error` emitted inside the stream before a retry would stay on the stream of a request that later succeeded; the test fakes patch `provider_factory(settings, efficiency=None)` (`tests/unit/test_cli.py:170-185`); a stop-deaf loop would buy up to five backed-off, paid re-requests after the wall clock. Alternatives: a loop around `_stream` (retries after an emitted error); `provider_factory(..., retry=)` (every fake's signature moves). RF-35 and RF-52 (§9.10) refine its reasoning and its one-error rule.
- **RF-21** (SR-19's version rule against fields outside the golden view). Decision: a version-record row may declare `"goldens_unchanged": true` with its reason, for a bump that adds or changes only fields no golden holds (`GOLDEN_EXCLUDED`, or `Scorecard`-level fields); "two rows share a digest" skips such a row, and a row declaring it whose digest differs from the previous row's fails. Why: a new required field outside the view must bump the version yet moves no golden, which the rule as written refused. Alternatives: a probe per excluded field (cost cannot enter the view without a price-table edit moving goldens).
- **RF-22** (RUN-06, RUN-11 on Windows). Decision: the driver waits for its worker in slices of about 1 s, so Ctrl-C is seen while an attempt runs (on Windows a long `Future.result(timeout)` is not reliably interrupted, bpo-29971); a promotion rename that fails leaves the recorded-kept attempt under `attempts/`, prints one line and goes on, and the run exits 1 naming `swreview benchmark resume`, which completes it. Why: a file another process holds, or an interrupt nobody sees, must not lose a paid attempt or hang the run to W. Alternatives: retry the rename (holds the run on another process's lock). Refined by RF-50 (§9.10): a daemon thread, not an executor.
- **RF-23** (SR-11 against `GoalLine.reason`; `report/finding_groups.py:144-151, 226`). Decision: the state decision of `_goal_line` is extracted as `finding_groups.goal_readings(session, words) -> list[GoalReading]`, each `(goal, state, reason, row)` with `reason` the `GoalReason` key; `goal_lines` builds its lines from it, so the pane's bytes do not move, and the scorer reads the key from it. Why: `GoalLine.reason` holds the words file's display text ("evidence missing", `report/review_words_v1.yaml:115-120`), not the key `scoring.md` 4 maps; matching display text ties scoring to wording, and re-deriving the key would be a second reader. Alternatives: a `GoalLine` field excluded from serialization (a hidden field on a display model); inverting the words map.
- **RF-24** (VD-14, VD-15 for the private study). Decision: `verdict.md` 7's refusals 1 (an arm missing) and 3 (no origin-marks record, `not_recorded`, or an unknown-origin false alarm) apply to `pane_defaults_private`; 2 and 4 to 9 do not. Why: the private row's intervals need both arms and known origins as much as the verdict does. Alternatives: refuse nothing on the private row (a one-arm row shows nothing).
- **RF-25** (the quickstart's offline run against `cli.py:238-246`). Decision: the quickstart's offline run over the committed set and `test_an_offline_run_over_a_committed_style_package_writes_no_local_path` pass `--max-answer-turns 0`: the one-turn `FAKE_REVIEW_SCRIPT` cannot play `mir-sitting`'s scripted part-roles answer (the scripted provider refuses a turn beyond its script, `agent/providers/fake.py:372-382`), which would end that package `internal_error` and the run with exit 1; at 0 the delivery is held and the package ends `bound_reached`, completed. Why: a smoke run needs no product change. Alternatives: a fake script closing one turn per answer turn (a change to `cli.py`'s script, with its own test).
- **RF-26** (test placement; `reviewer/tests/conftest.py:72-82`). Decision: every new test under `reviewer/tests/integration/` that needs no native package is not marked `integration`, as `tests/integration/test_coverage_stop.py` documents; the case-file isolation pins live in `tests/unit/test_answer_key_isolation.py` only, since the integration file of that name is module-marked `integration`. Why: the marker is skipped wherever the native package is absent, which is this development machine, so a marked pin would never run here. Alternatives: move the files under `unit/`. RF-49 (§9.10) deletes the integration file's key assertions.

### 9.10 Review fixes, second round

Taken on 2026-09-29 after a second review of the plan and its contracts, with every code claim read at `be01974`. Each entry: what it resolves, the decision, why, and the alternatives; the entry it refines ends with a pointer here, and every plan file it touches was edited to match. All are reversible plan-level defaults under the owner decisions; none changes scope, cost or what the study measures, so none is an owner question. Line numbers are at `be01974`.

- **RF-27** (SR-14 against RF-2; the plan's slice 1). Decision: slice 1 lands 19A and origin marks as two commits, in that order. First 19A, with its five regenerated check goldens, the new `joint-unknown-role`, and the replay fixtures regenerated and asserted byte-identical (RF-2). Then origin marks (`InvestigationStep.origin_mark`, `ReviewSession.origin_marks`, `recorded_call(mark=)`, feature 001's session schema amended), which regenerates the replay fixtures on the development machine; its gate is that the only difference under `reviewer/tests/fixtures/replay/` is one `"origin_marks": 1` in each of the three `session.json` files, every `events.jsonl` and `package.json` byte-identical, and `test_pane_fixture.py` passing unchanged (the pane fixture is `review_snapshot`, whose top-level keys, `run_id` to `labels`, hold no session-level field). No freeze: nothing the model reads moves. The `session.started` body names no session field, and no step is marked, since the three recordings run no pre-run (`prerun_checks` is false in all three sessions) and answer nothing (no `evidence.answered` event in any). Why: `start_review` writes the record beside `session.efficiency` (`agent/runner.py:1620`), and the fixtures' `session.json` is written by that call (`tests/fixtures/replay/generate_fixtures.py:162` lists it; `tests/support/replay.py:109-153` `record_scripted_review` calls `benchmark/replay.play_review`, which calls `start_review` at `benchmark/replay.py:337`), so with origin marks first 19A's byte-identical gate would fail; feature 008's 3A asks the change that moves the fixtures to regenerate them. Alternatives: origin marks first, RF-2's assertion ignoring the two fields (a gate that skips what it should pin); one commit for both (a freeze-free change mixed with a fixture-moving one).
- **RF-28** (PROV-07; D12's pinned text). Decision: D12's one commit also regenerates the add-in's pane fixture, `extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/Fixtures/review-big-assembly.json`, with `uv run python tests/fixtures/pane/generate_pane_fixture.py --write` after the replay fixtures; the slice-2 gate includes `test_pane_fixture.py`, and the add-in's WebView2 acceptance tests that load the file re-run on a Windows machine (the development machine is one). No C# changes: only the JSON fixture moves. D12 regenerates the replay fixtures after 19A's byte-identical check (RF-2) and origin marks' regeneration (RF-27), both in slice 1. Why: the file carries the old stamp five times (`profile:..\\config\\standards.example.yaml sha256:a4e6…`), and `test_pane_fixture.py:49` requires it to equal a fresh generation from `tests/fixtures/replay/big-assembly/` (`generate_pane_fixture.py:43-47`), so the slice-2 gate could not pass without it. Alternatives: none; a hand edit is what the fixture's own test refuses.
- **RF-29** (RP-2, SR-9, RUN-11; the plan's "one line reader"). Decision: the one reader of `events.jsonl` is the product's `replay_events` (`chat/sessions.py:106-129`, which the pane's reconnect, `chat/server.py:1345`, and remodel, `chat/remodel.py:678`, already use), moved beside `EVENTS_FILE_NAME` in `agent/events.py` (`:51`) and re-exported from `chat/sessions`, gaining one optional keyword, `on_unreadable: Callable[[int], None] | None`, called with the 1-based number of the line where it stops (a line that is not JSON or not an `AgentEvent`); without it nothing changes for its callers. It moves in the key-extensions slice (lane S3), before the first new reader. Its callers: the scorer, in one pass per package, reading withdrawn findings (`scoring.md` 3.3) and time to first finding, whose inline loop in `seconds_to_first_finding` (`benchmark/scorecard.py:311-353`) goes, an unreadable line making the withdrawn column `null` with its reason and leaving time to first finding what it is today (the first computed finding before the stop, else `None`); the report (slice 5), which shows everything before the stop and names the line; `recording.read_recording` (slice 5), whose `_events` (`benchmark/recording.py:194-212`) goes, raising `RecordingRefused` on the reported line; and `attempt_facts` (slice 6), which replays an attempt's events into `UsageLedger` (`agent/events.py:158-163`), as `generate_pane_fixture.py:75-77` already does, reads each round through a new public `UsageLedger.rounds()` for `price_rounds`, and keeps each `usage` body's `served_model` and `retries` beside the round of the same index in the same pass. A spy test pins that each of the four reads through it. `read_recording` then refuses a line that is JSON but not an `AgentEvent`, where `_events` took any object with a body; every event is written through `AgentEvent` (`EventSink.emit`), and a test reads each committed replay fixture's events through the reader with `on_unreadable` failing. Why: the product already had a typed, tolerant reader; the drafted refactor of `recording._events` would have been a third reader beside it and `seconds_to_first_finding`'s loop, and slices 3 and 6 named none, so each would have written its own. Alternatives: `recording._events` as the shared generator (RP-2 as drafted); a reader per use. Refined by RF-83 (§9.12): the reader also reads a packed stream, and `event_stream` beside it finds a run's stream in either form.
- **RF-30** (QA-10, RP-6; `benchmark/replay.py:1012-1036`, `tools/registry.py:391-432`). Decision: one reader of a stored tool result, beside its one writer: `tools/registry.read_stored_result(run_dir, session_id, step, *, tool=None, arguments=None) -> StoredResult | Unstored`. `StoredResult(payload, status, text)` carries the file's raw text; `Unstored(reason)` names `missing`, `unreadable` (not JSON, not an object, or a payload that is not an object) (refined: research 9.14, implementation follow-ups), `other_session`, or `other_call` (another step, or, when given, another tool or other arguments). The replay's `_stored_result` passes the recorded call's tool and arguments and treats any `Unstored` as today's `None` (the call stays estimated); `benchmark/questions.py` passes the step's tool and arguments, and any `Unstored` makes the call columns not assessed, naming the step and the reason; the report passes the session id and step, embeds `text`, and renders `missing` as "full result not stored" and `other_session` as "a stale result from another session in this folder". Lands in the key-extensions slice (lane S3), with a test that the replay's classification is unchanged. Why: the validity rule (session, step, tool, arguments, a payload) was about to be restated by the questions reader and the report beside the replay's copy. Alternatives: a reader per use (three copies); a boolean reader (the report could not tell missing from stale).
- **RF-31** (`benchmark-run.md` 4's "every record is written atomically"; Principle V). Decision: one helper, `write_atomic(path: Path, data: bytes) -> None` in a new `swreview/atomic.py`: a temporary file in the same folder, `os.replace`, the temporary removed in a `finally`, bytes so `\n` stays `\n`; its own test, `tests/unit/test_atomic.py`.
