Every record this feature writes calls it: `run-provenance.json` and the saved set (slice 2, which creates it), `answer-log.json` (slice 3), `prompts.json` (slice 5), `run-status.json` (slice 6), `pilot-measurement.json` and `noise-floor.json` (slice 8). The product's three copies (`report/attention_record.py:126-140`, `tokens.py:221-226`, `ir/loader.py:179-192`) are named as candidates to migrate and are not touched (surgical changes). Why: the rule is already written three times; the eval would have added more copies. Alternatives: migrate the three now (product writers this feature does not need to touch). RF-74 (§9.11) gives the three copies their Complexity Tracking row. *Amended 2026-10-06 by owner decision 34A (RF-88):* `write_atomic` is the only atomic writer; the three copies move onto it in Phase 2, lane P, each with a failing-first test (tasks.md T010, T011); at `be01974` their blocks are `report/attention_record.py:126-145` and `ir/loader.py:177-195`, whole, and `tokens.py:221-226`.
- **RF-32** (RUN-08, RUN-09 against the plan's "the new event fields written only by benchmark attempts"). Decision: `served_model` on a `usage` body whenever the provider reports one, and `stop_reason` on a provider-reported stop, are written on every stream, the pane's and `review`'s included: they are evidence (Principle I), and feature 002's `chat-events.schema.json` is amended for them. Only `retries` (written under a retry policy) and `prompts.json` are benchmark-only. `test_chat_server.py`'s `test_the_pane_stream_carries_the_served_model` pins that a stub response's model reaches the pane's `usage` event. The replay fixtures do not move: the scripted provider reports a served model only when a turn scripts one, and no replay turn does. Why: the contracts already wrote both fields on every stream (`benchmark-run.md` 7 and 8, and the provider tests' "on every usage body"), while the plan said the pane's bytes would not move; gating the fields would add a switch nobody needs and keep the answering model out of the pane's own records. Alternatives: gate both behind the policy.
- **RF-33** (RF-3's open branch). Decision: the replay's `_drive` closes a stopped turn with `run.end_stopped()` unconditionally; the branch that kept the replay's own close-out is removed. `test_benchmark_and_pane_attempts_wrap_tools_in_the_one_stoppable_tools` pins the stopped close-out, `cancel_presentation` included. Why: no replay fixture ends a turn stopped: the three `events.jsonl` files hold only `turn.ended` with `"reason":"end"` (2, 2 and 4), so `end_stopped` cannot move their bytes. Alternatives: keep the conditional (a third close-out left available for a case that cannot occur).
- **RF-34** (VD-19). Decision: the Student t distribution function is computed for integer degrees of freedom, which the verdict always has (`K − 1`), by its closed-form finite series (Abramowitz and Stegun 26.7.3 for odd ν, 26.7.4 for even ν, with θ = arctan(t/√ν)), and `t_quantile` bisects on it to 1e-10; no continued fraction and no incomplete beta. V-1's six pinned values and V-26's refusals stand; an offline check of the series reproduces all six to six decimals. Why: the incomplete beta by continued fraction is a hand-written special function beyond the simplest design, and the series is exact for the only degrees of freedom the verdict meets. Alternatives: the incomplete-beta route (VD-19 as drafted, which would need a Complexity Tracking row); scipy (a new dependency).
- **RF-35** (RUN-07, RF-20; the plan's Complexity Tracking row on retries). Decision unchanged: request-level retries inside the adapters. The row now weighs the alternative FR-038 itself allows, keeping each library's jittered retries (the OpenAI client's; google-genai 2.23's `HttpRetryOptions(attempts, initial_delay, max_delay, jitter)`) and counting them per request through each client's HTTP hook, and rejects it for three reasons: library retries cannot consult the attempt's `stop_requested()` after the wall clock (RF-20 (c)); they do not retry OpenAI's `response.failed` or a stream that ended with no response, which reach the adapter as events inside an HTTP exchange that succeeded; and a count made in a transport hook cannot be tied to the round's `usage` body (`retries` on the round that finally succeeded), which "counted exactly" reads. Why: the constitution asks for the simpler alternative and why it lost; the row had rejected a package re-run instead. Alternatives: adopt library retries with hooks (the three gaps above).
- **RF-36** (VD-10 and RF-11; `benchmark/compare.py:89, 769-779`). Decision: the paid pilot's run folders and its `pilot-measurement.json` are written under `benchmarks/pilots/<study folder>/`, which `recorded_runs()` never scans (it walks `STUDIES_DIR`, `benchmarks/studies/`, for every `scorecard.json`); `recorded_pilots()` scans `benchmarks/pilots/`. Pilot runs never enter a study, so RF-11's separation before `_check_one_study` and `_check_repetitions` is dropped, with test C-11's collision case; the pilot's raw rows (question 6's kept default) are rendered from the measurement's `pilot_runs` list, each folder named relative to the measurement's, the arm cell `off (pilot)`, in no count. A pilot folder passed to compare among a study's runs meets the existing repetition and must-match refusals, as any stray run does. The committable-folder scanner reaches `benchmarks/pilots/` as it reaches `benchmarks/studies/`. Supersedes RF-11. Why: the separation step existed only because the pilot's folders sat where `recorded_runs()` finds a study's. Alternatives: RF-11 (match on `started_at` and commit, then separate).
- **RF-37** (the delivery order's slices 7 and 8 against `verdict.md` 7's refusals 5 to 9). Decision: the verdict slice (lane V7) lands the `PilotMeasurement` model in `benchmark/pilot.py` (identity, `pilot_runs`, `measured`, `sizing`, `sizing_digest` and its computation, `approval`), compare's `--pilot`, `recorded_pilots()` and the pilot raw rows (RF-36), and compare's reading of the noise-floor record (RF-53), each tested against hand-written files, so V-22 and C-1 to C-17 close in slice 7. The pilot slice (lane V8) keeps `benchmark pilot`'s estimators, the sizing search, `benchmark noise-floor` and the paid pilot. Why: refusals 5 to 9, V-22's approval line and C-11, C-14 and C-15 need the measurement's model and compare's reading of it, which the drafted order put in slice 8, so slice 7's gate could not close before slice 8's code existed. Alternatives: keep the split and move those tests into slice 8's gate (the verdict slice would close without its refusals).
- **RF-38** (SR-12 and RF-6 against the spec's Terms; CS-17's leg (a)). Decision: test S-5.4 holds that every `CODE_ONLY_TOOLS` tool is in `prerun.prerun_tools()`, is `LIVE_INTERFERENCE_TOOL`, `DRAWINGS_TOOL` or `CHECK_TOOL`, or has no required parameter, and that every `CONFIGURED_CHECK_TOOLS` tool has a required parameter and is not a pre-run tool (RF-68 drops that set: every check tool outside `CODE_ONLY_TOOLS`); `check_interference_group(group_key)` (`tools/checks_interference.py:69`) and `bridge_interference(component_ids, configuration, settings)` (`tools/bridge.py:397-399`) are pinned as the named exceptions, pre-run tools that take arguments (`prerun.py:156, 159`). Leg (a)'s scripted turn calls every offered check with no required parameter that `prerun.planned_calls` did not plan: the interference groups are the pre-run's own per-group calls (`prerun.py:737-739`), and `bridge_interference` is not offered offline (`live_tools` false). Why: the drafted pin required every code-only tool to take no required parameter, which the two interference tools contradict, so it could never pass; the spec's Terms define a code-only call as "any tool the pre-run calls, or a check with no required parameter". Alternatives: drop the signature half (a check given a required parameter would stay code-only unseen).
- **RF-39** (FR-032's compare half, US5-12; `cases.md` 4.5). Decision: compare derives each scored package's flag as "some `graded_items` entry of its `PackageScore` has tag `held_out`" and compares it with the saved set's `held_out`; a mismatch is a run-level refusal naming the run and the package (`provenance.md` 2). An unscored package's flag was checked by `score` against its key (`scoring.md` 6). Pinned by `test_compare_refuses_a_set_flag_that_disagrees_with_the_scored_items` in `test_benchmark_compare.py`, lane P2. Why: `cases.md` 4.5 said score and compare both check the flag, but only score's check was designed, and compare reads no key; the scorecard's copied items are what compare has. Alternatives: compare re-reads keys (it reads none, by rule). Refined by RF-62 (§9.11): a scored package the saved set does not hold is refused.
- **RF-40** (VD-11, VD-12 against the quickstart's P2 and P3). Decision: the owner approves the printed size, cases to add included; the added growth units are then written as case specs, generated, validated and committed in the same commit as the pilot's folders and the approved measurement, with no second paid pilot. `sizing` records `pilot_packages` (the package ids of the paid pilot runs' saved sets, the committed set at the pilot's commit, sorted; never `benchmarks/sets/pilot.json`'s, which holds only `cover-blind-tap`, never in `committed.json`, so refusal 9 would refuse every pane study after its paid runs) and `growth_units` (0 when none). `verdict.md` 7's refusal 9 also refuses a study whose saved sets do not hold every `pilot_packages` id plus at least `growth_units` core cases beyond them (a package outside `pilot_packages` whose `graded_items` hold a held-out item). `benchmark pilot` refuses an existing `--out` ("one study has one measurement: delete the unapproved file to size again"), so a second cap replaces the file only by the owner's hand and one study never has two. Why: the sizing already searched over the grown layout, so re-measuring over the same folders prints the same cases to add, and a re-pilot is a paid cost nothing stated; `--cap` writing a second file would trigger refusal 5 on the committed document. Alternatives: a paid re-pilot on the grown set; `--cap` rewriting an existing file in place (an approved file could be overwritten). Refined by RF-66 (§9.11): growth units are counted by held-out known defects.
- **RF-41** (FR-051's writer half; RF-22's Windows file locks). Decision: `benchmark/scrub.py` `without_local_paths(text, *, run_dir, root=REPO_ROOT) -> str` replaces the run folder, the checkout root, the temporary folder and the home folder, longest first, case-insensitively, in their forward-slash, backslash and doubled-backslash spellings (an `OSError`'s text quotes its path through `repr`), with `<run>`, `<repo>`, `<tmp>` and `<home>`. The driver passes each escaped exception's message through it before `run-status.json` records it, and the attempt body composes it after the run's key redactor, so every error body the attempt's review writes into its `events.jsonl` (`emit_error`, `agent/events.py:195-216`) is scrubbed too. The committable-folder test exercises it. Pinned by `test_an_internal_error_naming_a_local_path_records_no_local_path`. Why: the contracts had only a detector, so a `PermissionError` naming an attempt folder would fail slice 9's gate on the paid folders with nothing but hand edits to fix them. Alternatives: the scanner alone; dropping `message` (loses the failure's words). Narrowed by RF-73 (§9.11).
- **RF-42** (PROV-13; `handoff.py:61-76, 128-155, 425-433`). Decision: the allowlist grows with the slices that add the fields: parity its slice-2 fields, scripted answers `max_answer_turns` (lane A3), robustness `package_time_limit_s`, `stream_activity_window_s`, `stop_grace_s` and `retry_policy` (lane B6); `test_the_handoff_allowlist_names_every_provenance_field` holds the allowlist equal to `RunProvenance.model_fields`, no recorded value a path. A benchmark export takes `<run>/<package>` as its input tree, as today, and, when the parent holds `run-provenance.json` beside `benchmark-set.json` (a benchmark run's root), also reads those names only from it: `run-provenance.json` from the parity slice, `run-status.json` from the robustness slice; `prompts.json` and `answer-log.json` join `OPTIONAL_ARTIFACTS` in the robustness slice. The export tests run on a folder `benchmark run` wrote with the scripted provider. Why: `export_handoff` reads one folder (`handoff.py:128-155`), while the provenance and status sit at the run root, which holds no session, so no real folder held what the tests expected together; and nothing held the allowlist to the record, so five fields could silently never reach the bundle. Alternatives: export the run root (no session, no events); every package of the run (the export becomes a run archive).
- **RF-43** (SR-19's structural test against `PackageScore`, `benchmark/scorecard.py:143-208`). Decision: `GOLDEN_IDENTITY = ("package_id", "held_out", "graded_items")` names the view's fields no behaviour can move: in the golden view, exempt from "every field has a probe", and pinned by `test_golden_identity_never_moves` (every probe's golden equals its base's in them). The six derived fields are declared by the probes that move them: `valid_findings` by the credited probe, `missed_known_defects` by the missed probe, `false_alarms` by the plain false-alarm probe, `unresolved_count` by the flagged probe, `carried_findings` by the `carried` origin probe, and `coverage_bucket_mix` by each coverage-mismatch probe and the unresolved-coverage split probe. Why: as worded, the structural test failed on three identity fields. Alternatives: drop the identity fields from the view (a golden would no longer say which package and items it grades).
- **RF-44** (the review function's contract; `benchmark/runner.py:201-251`, `cli.py:2192-2223`). Decision: `run_benchmark` passes the review function one new keyword, `attempt: AttemptRequest`, a frozen dataclass in `benchmark/runner.py`, beside today's `provider`, `model`, `effort`, `efficiency` and `explain_findings` (still forwarded only when true, `:250`). Its fields land with their slices: `model_view` and `standards_profile` (parity: `_review_fn` passes both to the review, so SC-003's parity test compares like with like), `case_file` and `max_answer_turns` (scripted answers), `stop` (robustness: the attempt's stop event); `run_benchmark` gains the matching keyword parameters. The three injected fakes that accept exactly today's four keywords (`tests/unit/test_answer_key_isolation.py:118-127`, `tests/unit/test_efficiency_settings.py:365-374`, `tests/integration/test_answer_key_isolation.py:56-64`) gain `attempt` in the parity slice; `tests/unit/test_answer_key_isolation.py:109-110`'s `pkg-1`, a folder with no `package.json`, gains one in the generator slice, whose existence check would refuse it. Why: the attempt body needs five inputs the call does not carry, and no contract said how they reach it. Alternatives: each new keyword forwarded only when it differs from its default (the stop and the answer-turn bound always differ in a benchmark run, and a keyword set that varies with the values is implicit); every input in the one request, today's keywords included (moves every existing fake and caller for no gain).
- **RF-45** (`report.md` 4's head against `scoring.md` 7). Decision: `Scorecard.price_table`, written with the robustness slice's cost fields: `null` when no round was priced, else `{"prices_version", "models": [{"provider", "model", "input": {"source", "date"}, "cached_input": {...}, "output": {...}}]}` for each table entry a round of the run was priced by, copied from `benchmark/prices.yaml`. Scorecard-level, so outside the golden view (RF-21). Why: the report's head prints the price table's source and date "as the scorecards record them", and no field carried them. Alternatives: drop the line (the page would not say whose prices it shows).
- **RF-46** (RP-6, SC-009; RK-11). Decision: R-18 builds the committed set's worst case, 47 cases at 10 repetitions in both arms, 940 transcripts, under the same 60 s; a report over the pilot's folders is its own, smaller build. Why: SC-009's 800 is below the size the plan runs, and RK-11 relies on R-18 to catch a breach before the pilot. Alternatives: keep 800 and record that the linking fallback applies above it.
- **RF-47** (RF-7 against the spec's Assumptions, `spec.md:428`). Decision: the Constitution Check cites the spec's acceptance-suite line (D14.7: the committed designs plus the private slice, at least 10) as superseded by RF-7's reading, a refinement of the default D14.7: the 10 committed designs are the suite, and the private slice is shown and never decides. The spec is the owner's document and is not edited by the plan. Why: the plan and the spec disagreed on what the constitution's acceptance suite is. Alternatives: amend the spec line (the owner's, when the spec is next revised). Later (§9.11): the line was amended to RF-7's reading.
- **RF-48** (INT-3, RF-1; `benchmark/runner.py:39-40, 100-102`). Decision: `sha256_of` moves to a new dependency-free `benchmark/digest.py` (`hashlib` and `pathlib` only) in the scorer slice, and `benchmark/runner.py` re-exports it, so `benchmark/compare.py:70` and the command line keep working; `answer_key.current_stamp()`, `sets` (`case_file_sha256`, `saved_form()`), provenance, and the resume and pane-study checks import it from there. `test_benchmark_generator_imports.py` imports `benchmark.digest` alone too. Why: `runner` imports `sets` (`:40`) and `agent.settings` (`:39`), so `sets` importing `runner.sha256_of` would be a circular import and would load `swreview.agent` into `sets`, failing RF-1's transitive test; hashing inline in `sets` would be a second digest function; and `current_stamp()` importing the runner would load the agent and, from slice 3, the answer driver. Alternatives: `sha256_of` in `sets.py` (the key loader would import the set module for a hash).
- **RF-49** (the scorer slice's reach into existing tests; SR-23). Decision: lane S1 also rewrites `tests/unit/test_scorecard_usage.py` (its `write_package`, `:121-199`, writes format-1 keys with `pkg-a-D-1` ids and set entries at `Path("unused")` with no package on disk and `held_out=True` over untagged keys, so its ten `score_run` calls, `:215-491`, would each be refused) and `tests/unit/test_carry_over_guards.py:759-803` (a format-1 key `KD-1` over a `tmp_path` holding no `package.json`, `held_out=True`): format-2 keys with `D-<n>` ids and tags, a support package saved at the entry's path (`tests/support/packages.build_package` and `ir/loader.save_package`), and `held_out=False` over tuned-on items. Keys in tests are written through one new helper, `tests/support/answer_keys.py` `write_key`, which builds through `AnswerKey` and which `test_scorecard.py`, those two files and `test_cli.py`'s `answer_keys` fixture share. `tests/integration/test_answer_key_isolation.py`'s key assertions (`:40-47`, its one `load_answer_key` call, `:41`) are deleted in the scorer slice, since the unmarked `test_committed_answer_keys.py` asserts the committed keys on every machine; its isolation checks stay, and its fake takes RF-44's keyword. Why: under the loader's rows 1 and 3 and `answer-key.md` 6 every one of those calls is refused, and the integration file, skipped wherever the native package is absent (RF-26), would go red only on the machine that has it. Alternatives: rewrite the integration assertions for the interim key and again for the regenerated one (a second copy of the committed-keys test).
- **RF-50** (RUN-06, RF-22; `chat/server.py:2176-2184`). Decision: the attempt body runs on a `threading.Thread(daemon=True)` that hands its result or exception back through a `queue.Queue`, which the driver polls in slices of about 1 s; not a `ThreadPoolExecutor`. Pinned by `test_a_hung_attempt_is_abandoned_and_the_process_still_exits`, which runs a hung scripted turn in a subprocess with a short W and G and asserts the process ends within G plus a margin. Why: executor workers are not daemon threads and `concurrent.futures` joins them at interpreter exit, so an abandoned worker blocked in a read (Gemini's client sets no timeout of its own) would hang the process at exit, defeating the abandoned class and RK-8's mitigation. Alternatives: an executor with `shutdown(wait=False)` (the exit-time join still waits).
- **RF-51** (FR-043; `provenance.md` 3.2 against `benchmark-run.md` 11). Decision: resuming a `pane_defaults` or `pane_defaults_private` run applies 3.2's run-time rules again before any review: a clean tracked tree and no carriage return in a digested input (the commit is already re-checked); a failure is refused naming the rule. Why: a pane study resumed at its commit with uncommitted edits would run paid attempts on code the record calls clean, and compare could not see it. Alternatives: record each resumed attempt's own tree state (a field compare would then have to check).
- **RF-52** (RUN-09, RF-20 against `agent/events.py:219-235`). Decision: "exactly one `error` per failed request" is the adapter's emission through its `on_event`; when the failure escapes `provider.run`, `ReviewRun._run_turn`'s `emit_turn_failed` adds its generic body after it (`error_class`, `message`, `retryable: true`, no `stop_reason`, no `retries`), as today. Classification and `attempt_facts` read `stop_reason` and `retries` from the bodies that carry them, the adapter's, and never count `error` events. Why: the stream holds two `error` events per raised failure today and will keep doing so. Alternatives: suppress the runner's generic event after a `ProviderError` (a change to the pane's stream that no reader needs).
- **RF-53** (VD-13, VD-17, FR-046; `tests/unit/test_benchmark_compare.py:994-1005`). Decision: the noise floor is simulated once, by `swreview benchmark noise-floor <run dirs...> --pilot <file> --out <file>` after the study's last repetition is scored, and stored in `noise-floor.json` beside the study's folders: the study key, `runs_digest` (sha256 over the sorted pairs of each study run's relative folder and its `scorecard.json` sha256), the seed, the simulations, the numpy version, the grid and the result (points, "above 50 points" or "not attainable"). Compare reads it (`--noise-floor`, or `recorded_noise_floors()` under `benchmarks/studies/`) and simulates nothing; a missing record renders "not computed" and one whose `runs_digest` differs "stale", both shown and neither a refusal, since the noise floor never decides. Written through `write_atomic`, committed with the study's folders. Why: compare re-simulating on every render draws from numpy, which `reviewer/uv.lock` resolves to 2.4.6 on Python 3.11 and 2.5.3 on 3.12 and later (`:776, 858`), and whose distribution streams are not promised stable across releases, so the committed ledger's byte-identity test could render different bytes on two supported Pythons; that default-suite test would also run 4,000-simulation searches over up to 100 grid points. Alternatives: pin one numpy across the Python markers (holds every 3.12+ environment back, and the default suite still simulates); compute it at the pilot (the Terms' noise floor is the study's own). Refined by RF-56 (§9.11): `runs_digest` reads each scorecard's canonical JSON.
- **RF-54** (CS-13, PROV-08 against the generator's sets; `benchmark/sets.py:26`). Decision: `BenchmarkSet.file_form(*, root) -> bytes` beside `saved_form(*, root) -> bytes`, both through one entry serializer: in-root paths root-relative with `/`, others absolute; UTF-8 JSON, indent 2, LF, a final newline; `file_form` omits `case_file_sha256` and `saved_form` adds it. The generator writes `committed.json` and `pilot.json` through `file_form`. Why: `BenchmarkPackageRef.path` is a `Path`, serialized with the platform's separators, and `saved_form()` adds a field a set file must never hold, so the generator had no serializer for the committed form and would have written a second one or other bytes on Windows (SC-006). Alternatives: the generator's own JSON writer (a second writer of the set format).
- **RF-55** (GN-4, INT-12). Decision: `as_committed`, written twice today (`tests/unit/test_support_mechanical.py:266`, `tests/unit/test_support_drawings.py:253`), moves once to `tests/support/mechanical.py` beside `load_generator` (`:1034`); both test modules, the case generator's regeneration test and the golden digest import it. Why: moving one copy would leave a third reader beside a survivor. Alternatives: none.

Corrections with no decision of their own, made in the same round:
- `reviewer/tests/support/scoring_probes.py` has its row in the plan's lanes table (S1 the oracle, the null and the stand-in, RF-15; S3 the coverage and question halves; G4 the probes and `MATCHING_RULES`; B6 the behavioural-stop probe); the parity rows of `cli.py` and `benchmark/runner.py` name the forwarding of the model view and the profile (RF-44).
- `benchmark-run.md` 3 row 1's message says only "choose a new folder" in the parity slice; the robustness slice adds the resume pointer with its own test.
- RUN-15's parity table classifies `package_dir` and `out_dir` as transport: the pane reviews in place (`chat/server.py:2121-2123`), a benchmark attempt reads the package folder and writes `attempts/<package>/<n>/`, which behave the same offline (no bridge, no live-row write-back).
- `verdict.md` 8's example reads 47 retained packages and 46 degrees of freedom; `questions.md` 5's examples carry their tag.
- S-10 also asserts that every `half-sd1.0-*` scenario's size search reaches 10 repetitions and then adds at least one growth unit (SC-010).

### 9.11 Review fixes, third round, and owner decisions 24A to 27A

Taken on 2026-09-29 after a third review of the plan and its contracts, with every code claim read at `be01974`, and after the owner answered questions 1, 2, 3 and 5 of §9.7 (24A to 27A) and kept the defaults of 4, 6 and 7. Each entry: what it resolves, the decision, why, and the alternatives; the entry it refines ends with a pointer here, and every plan file it touches was edited to match. All are reversible plan-level defaults under the owner decisions, except RF-64, which applies 25A. Line numbers are at `be01974`.

- **RF-56** (RF-53's `runs_digest`; FR-046; `cli.py:2382`). Decision: `benchmark/digest.py` gains `canonical_sha256(value) -> str`, the sha256 of `json.dumps(value, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False)` as UTF-8, beside `sha256_of` (the module then imports `json` too, still the standard library only), landing with the module in slice 1. `runs_digest` is `canonical_sha256` of the sorted pairs of each study run's folder (relative to the record, `/`) and the `canonical_sha256` of its parsed `scorecard.json`; `sizing_digest` (RF-65) and 25A's tool digest (RF-64) use the same helper. P-8 adds: one scorecard written with CRLF and with LF, or with its keys reordered, gives one `runs_digest`. Why: `benchmark score` writes `scorecard.json` with `write_text` (`cli.py:2382`), so the Windows working copy that wrote it holds CRLF while every checkout of the committed file holds LF (`benchmarks/** eol=lf`); a raw digest would render the noise floor "stale" on every checkout but the writer's and break the committed ledger's byte-identity test (`test_benchmark_compare.py:994-1005`) once slice 9 commits the study. Alternatives: route every scorecard writer through `write_atomic` as LF bytes (the writer's copy and a checkout would agree, but every writer, test fixtures included, would have to, and one missed writer breaks it silently); normalise line endings inside the digest (a second rule beside the canonical form `sizing_digest` already used).
- **RF-57** (§9.7 question 6's option B; `verdict.md` 7 refusal 8; `provenance.md` 4; quickstart P3). Decision: option B, counting the pilot's off-arm runs as the study's first repetitions, is withdrawn, not merely declined, and the plan keeps no trace of it: no gate waits on question 6, and the contracts name the pilot's raw rows as the kept default's. Why: P3 commits the pilot's folders and the approved measurement before any study run, so the pilot's runs carry an earlier `commit` than the study's (and another `set_digest` whenever growth units are added) and a `started_at` before `approved_at`; the must-match loop and refusal 8 would refuse the study. Alternatives: keep B with the three exemptions it would need (the pilot at the study's commit, no growth units, no refusal 8), each against RF-36, RF-40 or the approval rule.
- **RF-58** (SC-001; `scoring.md` 9.2, `questions.md` 1, 6 and 8). Decision: `oracle_review(key, package, folder)` and the null review write each review into a package folder of the run's shape: `session.json`; `events.jsonl` holding the one `session.started` event, through the product's `EventSink` (`agent/events.py:76-119`); and the oracle's request calls made through the real `request_evidence` tool with `tool_results_dir` set, so `SessionSink.record` writes each step's stored result (`tools/registry.py:362-432`). The scorer's per-package function reads the folder as `score_run` reads a run's package. SC-001 is asserted column by column: zero on every column the key grades, `null` with its reason exactly where the key has no entry of the list a column reads, and on `unscripted_questions` and `bound_reached` always (no oracle has an answer log, and no key grades them); `not_assessed` names no other column. Lanes: S1 writes the session and the stream; S3 the request calls. Why: built as sessions only, the oracle read `null` (withdrawn hits, for a missing stream) or not assessed (the question columns, for unstored results) on columns the key does grade, and the drafted "zero, or not assessed" still passed, so SC-001 went untested there. Alternatives: pass the stream, answer-log and stored-result readings into `_score_package` as explicit inputs (a second way into the scorer beside the folder every run takes).
- **RF-59** (the delivery order's gates for slices 1, 2, 3 and 5). Decision: each gate names only tests whose code has landed. `test_scorecard.py` 9's absent-session half is test 16's (slice 6): until `package_outcome` exists, `score_run` stops on a missing `session.json` (`benchmark/scorecard.py:489`). `test_committable_paths.py` gates slice 2 but for its scrub test, which needs `benchmark/scrub.py` (slice 6); its offline run runs in slice 2 without `--max-answer-turns`, which lane A3 adds in slice 3 with the option. `test_event_lines.py` 4 lands row by row with its callers: the `score_run` row in slice 3, the report's and `read_recording`'s rows and the structural "no other reader under `benchmark/`" in slice 5, where `recording._events` (`benchmark/recording.py:194-212`) goes, the `attempt_facts` row in slice 6. Slice 5's gate also runs `tests/integration/test_replay_recorded_runs.py` on the development machine (88 reproduced and 11 not replayable, `:152-155`), since `replay_events` is stricter than `_events`. Why: three gates named tests that could not pass before a later slice. Alternatives: reorder the slices (the order is 12A's).
- **RF-60** (`benchmark-run.md` 3 row 3 against `tests/unit/test_cli.py:163-185, 2438-2485`). Decision: the run-start check builds one adapter through `provider_factory` and maps the effort, and that adapter is the run's first attempt's: `benchmark_run` hands it over through a one-shot holder bound into the review function it passes `run_benchmark`, and every later attempt builds its own; `benchmark resume` does the same for its first re-run attempt. A run builds exactly one adapter per attempt, as today, so both one-package `test_cli` tests read one adapter unchanged; `test_the_run_start_adapter_is_the_first_attempts` pins it. Why: the `fake_provider` fixture records every adapter built, and two tests assert exactly one for a one-package run; an unused adapter holds no per-turn state, so handing it over keeps "a fresh adapter per package" (`cli.py:2204-2205`). Alternatives: a check building no adapter (the key rule and each adapter's effort table restated outside the adapters, and a key check outside the patched factory refuses `--provider gemini` in `test_cli` on a machine without a key); keep the extra build and rewrite both tests to read `built[1:]`. Refined by RF-81: its test lives in `tests/unit/test_cli.py`.
- **RF-61** (RF-4's root rule against `tests/unit/test_answer_key_isolation.py:35-179`). Decision: in slice 2 `run_benchmark` gains `root: Path = REPO_ROOT`, forwarded to `load_set`; the test file's three `load_set` calls (`:48`, `:71`, `:105`) and its `run_benchmark` call at `:130` pass `root=tmp_path`, whose `benchmarks/sets/` holds their sets; the smuggle test's stub (`:166`) takes the keyword, so its `run_benchmark` call (`:170`), which reads the set through the stub, needs no `root`. In slice 3 the `PreflightedSet` argument replaces the set path and the root, and the smuggle test builds a `PreflightedSet` holding the smuggled entry directly; the runner keeps its own check on every entry (`benchmark/runner.py:237-239`), which that test pins. Why: under the root rule the file's sets are refused by row 3 before the guard, so the leaky and junction tests would see a `SetError` where they pin `AnswerKeyAccessError`, and the stub's one-argument lambda raises `TypeError`; with the root each keeps its meaning. Alternatives: move `PreflightedSet` into slice 2 (lane A3's work in P2's slice).
- **RF-62** (RF-39 against `tests/support/studies.py:366-410`). Decision: compare refuses a scored package the run's saved set does not hold, naming run and package, and runs both set checks only where the saved copy exists, as `_check_saved_set` does (`benchmark/compare.py:400-415`). `write_run` (lane P2) writes one saved-set entry per `PackageSpec`, `held_out` agreeing with its `graded_items`, where it writes `"packages": []` today, and records `set_digest` over those bytes. Pinned by `test_compare_refuses_a_scored_package_missing_from_the_saved_set`. Why: every study fixture writes an empty saved set, so an undefined case would either refuse every compare test or pass silently on the fixtures meant to pin RF-39. Alternatives: skip a missing entry (the check passes on exactly the fixtures that should exercise it).
- **RF-63** (RF-18's test). Decision: the round-level pin of lever 7 through the stop wrapper is `test_the_stop_wrapper_keeps_the_withdrawal` in `tests/integration/test_coverage_stop.py`, on that file's `respx` replay of the OpenAI Responses endpoint with the run's tools wrapped in `agent.runner.StoppableTools`, as an attempt and as the pane wrap them, asserting `tool_choice: "none"` on the round after the checklist closes; `test_benchmark_attempts.py` keeps a unit pin that `providers.tools_withdrawn` sees through the wrapper. Why: `FakeProvider` never reads `tools_withdrawn`, so the drafted scripted test could not fail; the file's own docstring (`:3-17`) says why the fake cannot tell the mechanisms apart. Alternatives: a scripted-provider affordance recording `tools_withdrawn(tools)` per round (a test-only seam in the fake).
- **RF-64** (25A; §9.7 question 2's file ownership). Decision: `Stamp` (`benchmark/answer_key_model.py`) and `held_out_against` gain `tools`; `answer_key.tool_descriptions_digest()` is `canonical_sha256` of `{name: {"description": spec.full_description, "schema": spec.schema}}` over `TOOL_FUNCTIONS`, `COMPACT_QUERY_TOOL_FUNCTIONS`, `FINDING_DETAIL_TOOL_FUNCTIONS`, `BRIDGE_TOOL_FUNCTIONS`, `standards_tools()` and `drawing_tools()` (`tools/registry.py:203-274`), each through `spec_for` (`:444-450`), remodel's tools excluded; `current_stamp()` names it with the two files. It lands in slice 1 (lane S1 owns both key modules), so no later slice edits `Stamp`; no run records it, since a run's `commit` fixes the docstrings. `test_held_out_stamps.py` pins a one-character change to a docstring body, to its Notes and to one `Args:` entry each failing the stamp check, and the digested set's completeness over every lever combination. Why: 25A; the full description holds the half lever 2's on arm sends, but not the `Args:` entries: each reaches the model as its parameter's `description` in the tool schema, on both arms of every lever (`canonical_schema`, `agent/providers/schema.py:208-236`; `RecordedTool.schema`, `tools/registry.py:523-524`), and review policy sits there on purpose (`request_evidence`'s guidance, put in its argument descriptions "and not in the Notes", `tools/session.py:95-99`), so a digest of the full description alone would leave an item held out after a tuning edit to an `Args:` entry, which 25A exists to prevent. The whole schema is taken, not its parameter descriptions picked out: nested models carry descriptions the model also reads (a `SourceRef`'s `persist_ref`), and one text per tool needs no selection rule. Python reads docstrings with universal newlines and the one pydantic of `reviewer/uv.lock` builds the schema, so the digest is one number on every checkout. Alternatives: a provenance field for the digest (a must-match field nothing reads); the wire description per arm (two digests for one policy text); the full description and each parameter's `description` only (a selection rule that leaves nested descriptions out). Its first draft digested the full description alone, which missed the `Args:` entries; corrected in this round's second pass.
- **RF-65** (VD-11's `sizing_digest`). Decision: `sizing_digest` covers every block but `approval`: identity, `pilot_runs`, `measured` and `sizing`; C-6 adds an edited `pilot_runs`. Why: the approval rule lets the owner change nothing else, and compare matches a measurement by the study key in `identity`. Alternatives: digest `measured` and `sizing` only (an edited identity or run list goes unseen).
- **RF-66** (RF-40's refusal 9). Decision: a growth unit is counted as a package outside `pilot_packages` whose `graded_items` hold a held-out **known defect**; C-9 adds one core case and its twin where two units are approved. Why: a twin's conditions carry its seeds' held-out tags, so counting any held-out item counted each unit twice. Alternatives: exclude entries carrying `twin_of` (a second reading of what a unit is).
- **RF-67** (DRY across `generator.md` 7, `scoring.md` 6, `cases.md` 4.5 and 8). Decision: the data-only `AnswerKey` gains `holds_held_out()` and `held_out_model_defect_ids()`; the generator's set flag, `score`'s flag check and held-out count, and the set summary's check and count call them, pinned by `test_answer_key.py` 15. Why: each number was derived in up to four places. Alternatives: a helper in the loader (the generator may not import it, RF-1).
- **RF-68** (SR-12's `CONFIGURED_CHECK_TOOLS`). Decision: dropped; a configured check is rule 4's "otherwise", and S-5.4 holds that every check tool outside `CODE_ONLY_TOOLS` has a required parameter and is not a pre-run tool. Why: only the structural test read the literal set (Principle V). Alternatives: keep it (a production constant for one test).
- **RF-69** (SR-19's merge-base check). Decision: the version record's check of the record at `HEAD` or at the merge base with `main` is dropped; SC-013 rests on the in-file checks (the last row's version, the digest, consecutive versions, `goldens_unchanged`). Why: it depended on git history and a branch name, skipped in shallow clones and worktrees without `main`, to catch an edited committed row, which review sees in the diff. Alternatives: keep it with a Complexity Tracking row.
- **RF-70** (`scoring.md` 9.3, RF-43; `questions.md` 8). Decision: `not_assessed` is declared by every probe that moves a column between `null` and assessed: a new probe, the event stream unreadable from line N (the withdrawn column to `null`, its reason added), and the answer-log probes, which also declare the other log column. `MATCHING_RULES` leaves out the recess kind and the folded joint-map finding, which `test_benchmark_matching.py` 4 and 5 build from the real checks. Why: `not_assessed` is in the golden view and had no declaring probe, and a probe must start from a committed case, none of which keys a recess or folds joints. Alternatives: add a recess-keyed condition to a core design (case work for one probe).
- **RF-71** (RUN-06's client close at W). Decision: nothing closes the adapter's client; a read blocked in a silent stream is bounded by G and the daemon worker (RF-50). Why: no adapter has a close seam (the SDK client is private and shared with `for_presentation`, `openai_provider.py:290, 316-331`; `gemini_provider.py:312`), and the scripted `hang` could not show a close breaking a read. Alternatives: a `TransportCloseable.close_transport()` extension on both adapters and the fake.
- **RF-72** (RUN-09's `ProviderError` base against `openai_provider.py:137-150`, `gemini_provider.py:152-190, 853-864`). Decision: the base carries class-level defaults: `retryable` a class attribute an instance may override, `error_class` the given value or the class name, `stop_reason` `None`; it adds no `__init__`, so both families construct unchanged, pinned in the provider tests. Why: OpenAI's error takes keywords per instance, Gemini's set `retryable` per class and are built from a message alone. Alternatives: one required constructor (every Gemini construction site moves).
- **RF-73** (RF-41's reach). Decision: the claim is narrowed to what `without_local_paths` reaches: `run-status.json`'s messages and the runner's generic error body (`emit_error`, `agent/events.py:195-216`). An adapter's own error body (key redaction only, `openai_provider.py:731-740`, `gemini_provider.py:591-600`) and a tool's failure text (`tools/registry.py:421-431`) are not scrubbed; the scanner finds them before commit, pinned by `test_an_adapter_error_naming_a_local_path_is_found_by_the_scanner`. Why: the drafted claim was broader than the mechanism. Alternatives: pass the composed redactor into the adapters and the registry (a change to the pane's error path for a benchmark need).
- **RF-74** (RF-31 against Principle V and Governance). Decision: the product's three atomic-write copies (`report/attention_record.py:126-140`, `tokens.py:221-226`, `ir/loader.py:179-192`) get a Complexity Tracking row, and row V reads "one atomic writer for this feature's records". Why: a DRY violation left in place must be justified there; `attention_record.py` writes in text mode, so migrating it would move a pane record's bytes on Windows. Alternatives: migrate `ir/loader.py` and `tokens.py` in slice 2 (product writers this feature does not otherwise touch). *Superseded 2026-10-06 by owner decision 34A (RF-88):* the three copies migrate, the attention record's bytes LF on Windows; the Complexity Tracking row is removed, and row V reads "one atomic writer, the product's three older copies moved onto it".
- **RF-75** (the generator's identity; `generator.md` 1, 5.5; `cases.md` 1, 2). Decision: `design.design_id` is the case id, so `test_validate_reads_the_committed_benchmark_package` (`tests/unit/test_cli.py:346-355`) passes on the regenerated `cover-blind-tap`; the IR `package_id` (a uuid5) is named apart from the set's and key's `package_id` (the case id); the stale-file scan and the regeneration test skip `benchmarks/packages/bracket-assy/`, the suite's native-package slot (`reviewer/tests/conftest.py:27`), which git does not ignore. Why: the builders write `dsn:fict` and `dsn:1` (`tests/support/mechanical.py:1015`, `tests/support/packages.py:93`); two fields shared a name; the scan would exit 1 on every machine holding the native package. Alternatives: move the test to slice 4's moved list; define stale over tracked files (git needed to generate).
- **RF-76** (FR-040 against `openai_provider.py:708-729`; `benchmark-run.md` 6 and 8). Decision: a `response.failed` whose `response.error.code` is neither `server_error` nor `rate_limit_exceeded` (a policy code, `invalid_prompt`, `bio_policy`, `misalignment_policy_violation` or `image_content_policy_violation`, or any other code), and a 400 whose `APIError.code` is one of those four policy codes, set `stop_reason` to the code: a behavioural `provider_stop`, never retried. `_failed` reads the code; the retryable failed response narrows to `server_error`, `rate_limit_exceeded` or no code; any other 400 stays a non-retryable `provider_error`; each error's `retryable`, what the pane is offered, is unchanged, and the retry loop retries no error carrying `stop_reason`. Pinned by `test_an_openai_policy_failure_is_a_provider_stop_and_never_retried` and the provider tests' section 8 rows. Why: FR-040 makes any stop the provider reports behavioural; the locked `openai` 3.13.0 (`reviewer/uv.lock:934`) lists these codes on `ResponseError` (`types/responses/response_error.py`); `_failed` made every failed response retryable, so a policy stop was re-sent up to five times, paid, then excluded and re-run as `provider_error`, the survivorship bias D2 and 26A exist to prevent. Alternatives: the policy codes only, other failed-response codes staying retryable (against FR-040's "any other" reason); a new `retryable` value (it moves what the pane offers).
- **RF-77** (26A against the one Gemini overflow pattern; `benchmark-run.md` 8 and 9; RK-7). Decision: a `GeminiRequestError` without `stop_reason` that escapes an attempt after at least one of its rounds was answered is `provider_stop` with the reason `input_token_limit_inferred`, kept and graded; one before any answered round stays `provider_error`. The reason names the inference, and `run-status.json` keeps the service's message for the owner to read when the run is scored. Pinned by `test_an_unrecognised_gemini_request_error_after_an_answered_round_is_an_inferred_overflow` and its before-any-round counterpart. The error is recognised by its type and the scripted provider raises that type for the tests (§9.12's corrections). Why: the pattern is pinned on the documented message, unverified without a key; a miss would be re-run by resume, likely overflow again, and drop the package from its arm, against 26A; a request the service had accepted, grown only by the review's own history, is refused for that history, whatever the wording, while a refusal before any answered round (a key, a model, a request shape) would meet every package. Alternatives: re-run once and infer only when the refusal recurs (26A's declined option C for every miss, at one more paid review); resume refusing to re-run an unrecognised 4xx (a study stuck until a code change its one commit forbids); a broader message match (a reworded message misses again); recording that 26A covers recognised overflows only (the owner's call, not needed).
- **RF-78** (`provenance.md` 6.6 against `.gitignore:77`; FR-051, US1-11, US6). Decision: the parity slice adds `!benchmarks/studies/**/events.jsonl` and `!benchmarks/pilots/**/events.jsonl` after the `**/events.jsonl` rule, a lane-P2 row; `test_committable_paths.py` checks through `git check-ignore -q --no-index` that neither tree's streams are ignored while one elsewhere under `benchmarks/` still is, skipped without git. Why: at `be01974` `git check-ignore -v` names `.gitignore:77` for a study's, an attempt folder's and a pilot's `events.jsonl`, while every other run-folder file is committable, so a committed study would lack its streams: on another checkout a re-score after a version bump would read withdrawn hits `null`, `attempt_facts` cost and served models unknown, the noise floor's `runs_digest` stale, and the report could not be rebuilt from the repository. The cases are fictional, and 6.6's scanner reads the streams. Alternatives: keep the streams out, and say in 6.6 and quickstart P4 what re-scoring and the report lose on another checkout. **Size** (recorded 2026-09-29 with 28A). The three replay fixtures, reviews recorded end to end, hold 104,092, 113,314 and 546,061 bytes of `events.jsonl` as git stores them at `be01974` (`reviewer/tests/fixtures/replay/`: the two small assemblies, 36 and 32 steps; the big assembly, 38 steps). A study at the cap keeps 47 × 10 × 2 = 940 streams and the pilot 47 × 3 = 141, failed attempts' aside: at the small assemblies' mean, about 102 MB and 15 MB of plain streams in every checkout's working tree; at the big assembly's size, a bound, since every committed design is a small assembly, about 513 MB and 77 MB; and again per provider studied. `gzip.compress(..., compresslevel=9, mtime=0)` shrinks the three to 12,443, 12,614 and 41,491 bytes (8.4, 9.0 and 13.2 times): about 12 MB and 2 MB per study and pilot at the mean, 39 MB and 6 MB at the bound. Git deflates every object it stores, so the history and a clone's transfer are of one order either way (the three streams, committed plain, packed to 61 KiB, and gzip-compressed to 65 KiB, after `git gc`); the plain form's cost is the working tree of every checkout, the pilot workstation's included. Owner decision 28A answers it: the committed streams are compressed, and this entry's `.gitignore` rules target the compressed form (RF-84), which supersedes the two plain-stream exceptions above and their test.
- **RF-79** (the quickstart's price check against SC-008; `benchmark-run.md` 3 row 6 and 13). Decision: from the robustness slice, a `pane_defaults` or `pane_defaults_private` run refuses at start, exit 1, when the price table cannot price its requested model by section 13's lookup, naming provider and model; `test_a_pane_study_refuses_a_model_the_price_table_cannot_price` pins it, and the quickstart names the refusal. Why: `test_prices.py` checks only the default models (`agent/settings.py:103-107`), so a paid pilot run with another `--model` would read "cost unknown" on every package and fail SC-008 after it was paid for. Alternatives: reword the quickstart so the pilot uses a default model or the operator checks the entry (a manual check before a paid run).
- **RF-80** (FR-029 and 15A against `cases.md` 9's committed-set test; `generator.md` 2 and 10). Decision: the key-only spec entries gain an optional `represents`, as a seed has; spec check 8 also refuses a seed or entry whose `represents` is an anchor (R-1 to R-8) unless its tag is `TunedOn`; the committed-set test reads these tags from the case specs, which tests may import, as the validation does. Why: `represents` is recorded per set entry and never in a key, and three set entries name an anchor beside a held-out analyst-found item (`kalo-contact-positive`, `kalo-empty-positive`, whose one item is AF-1's, and `tessa-groups`), so the test could not tell an anchor's items from the others, and nothing enforced 15A there. Alternatives: rule that every key-only entry stands for its case's anchors (implicit, and it would misfile one that stands for an analyst-found item).
- **RF-81** (`benchmark-run.md` 15 against `tests/unit/test_cli.py:109-138, 162-185`). Decision: `test_the_run_start_adapter_is_the_first_attempts` lives in `tests/unit/test_cli.py` beside the two adapter-count tests (`:2438-2485`). Why: the `fake_provider` fixture and its `RecordingProvider` are that module's and in no conftest, so `test_benchmark_run_options.py` could not use them as written. Alternatives: move both into `tests/unit/conftest.py` in slice 2 (a shared fixture for one test).
