The contracts that said they refined those sentences now cite them.

### 9.12 Owner decisions 28A and 29A, and three corrections

Taken on 2026-09-29 after the third round. 28A answers RF-78's size, whose arithmetic RF-78 now records; 29A has the tasks generated now, the owner reviewing the plan and the tasks together before implementation (§1). RF-82 to RF-84 apply 28A; each is a reversible plan-level default under it, and every plan file it touches was edited to match. Line numbers are at `be01974`.

- **RF-82** (28A: who compresses the streams, when, and where the digests live; `provenance.md` 6.7). Decision: a finished run folder is packed by an explicit command, `swreview benchmark pack <run dirs...>` (`benchmark/streams.py` `pack_run(run_dir)`, lane B6, the robustness slice), run by the operator after a run's last resume and before its folder is committed (quickstart P3, P4). Every named folder's refusals are checked before any folder is packed: no `run-provenance.json`; no `run-status.json`; an attempt whose `kind` is `null` (still running, or interrupted: resume first); a recorded kept attempt still under `attempts/` (the incomplete promotion `package_outcome` names); an `events.jsonl.gz` with no plain stream beside it and no row in the record; a folder holding both forms whose packed stream decompresses to bytes other than the plain one's; a plain stream whose row, left by a pack stopped before its deletions, names another digest. Then each `<run>/<package>/events.jsonl` and `<run>/attempts/<package>/<n>/events.jsonl` is read, compressed with `gzip.compress(data, compresslevel=9, mtime=0)` (no file name and no time in the header, so one stream packs to one set of bytes on one zlib), decompressed again in memory and required equal to what was read, and written beside it as `events.jsonl.gz` through `write_atomic`; then `<run>/event-streams.json` is written through `write_atomic` (`{"event_streams_version": 1, "streams": {<run-relative path of each packed stream>: <sha256 of its plain bytes>}}`, sorted keys, an earlier pack's rows kept and never changed); the plain streams are deleted last. A folder already holding both forms (a pack stopped after its write) is verified and finished; a run with nothing left to pack prints "already packed" and exits 0; a resumed run's new attempts write plain streams, which packing the run again packs. The digest is `benchmark/digest.sha256_of_bytes` of the plain bytes, the one raw-bytes digest (`sha256_of` then calls it; lane B6 adds it in this slice), so it names the stream as the run wrote it, whatever compressed it. Why: 28A keeps the plain stream the working form while a run writes, and a run may still write after its last attempt is recorded (an abandoned daemon worker writes into its folder until the process exits, RF-50; resume adds attempts), so packing is a step taken once the run is final, by the operator who commits it; one record per run folder puts every digest where the committed-folder test reads it (RF-84). Alternatives: pack in `benchmark score` (a reader rewriting the folder it reads, every scored temporary run changing shape, and a score before a resume leaving the new attempts plain anyway); pack each attempt when the driver records its outcome (an abandoned worker may still be writing); a digest file beside each stream (twice the files); the digest of the compressed bytes (it names the compressor's output, which another zlib may change, not the stream).
- **RF-83** (28A: how the readers read a packed stream; RF-29's one reader). Decision: `agent/events.py` gains `PACKED_EVENTS_FILE_NAME = "events.jsonl.gz"` and `event_stream(folder) -> Path` beside `EVENTS_FILE_NAME` and the moved `replay_events` (lane S3, the key-extensions slice): the plain `events.jsonl` when it exists, else the packed one when it exists, else the plain path, which `replay_events` reads as an empty stream, as today; a folder holding both raises `ValueError` naming it: "{folder} holds events.jsonl and events.jsonl.gz: a pack did not finish; run swreview benchmark pack on its run again". `replay_events` opens a path whose name ends `.gz` with `gzip.open(path, "rt", encoding="utf-8")` (refined: research 9.14, implementation follow-ups), and a decompression error, a truncated or corrupt archive (`EOFError`, `gzip.BadGzipFile`, `zlib.error`), ends the replay as a malformed line does, reported through `on_unreadable` with the number of the line it was reading. Every reader of a run's streams finds each through `event_stream`: the scorer's one pass (`scoring.md` 1), `attempt_facts` (`benchmark-run.md` 10) and the report (`report.md` 2); `read_recording` and the pane keep reading `EVENTS_FILE_NAME`, since recordings and live sessions are never packed; `test_event_lines.py` 4's structural test names `benchmark/streams.py`'s packing, which copies bytes and parses no event, as its one exception. No reader checks the digest: the pack verifies what it writes, and `test_committable_paths.py` verifies every committed stream (RF-84), so a re-score or a report decompresses each stream once. Pinned by `test_event_lines.py` 5 and 6 and `report.md`'s R-19. Why: RF-29's rule, one reader beside the writer's file name, with the one resolver every reader needs beside it; refusing both forms keeps an unfinished pack from being read as either (Principle I). Alternatives: the resolver in `benchmark/streams.py` (apart from the reader and the name it resolves, and the scorer and the report importing the packing module); readers verifying the digest on every read (a second decompression of every stream, inside the report's budget, RF-46); reading the plain stream when both exist (an unfinished pack read silently).
- **RF-84** (28A: the git rules and their tests; supersedes RF-78's two plain-stream exceptions and its test). Decision: the parity slice's `.gitignore` adds `**/events.jsonl.gz` after `**/events.jsonl` (`:77`), then `!benchmarks/studies/**/events.jsonl.gz` and `!benchmarks/pilots/**/events.jsonl.gz`: a plain stream stays ignored everywhere but the replay fixtures (`:80`), the two trees included, and a packed one is committable in those two trees only. `.gitattributes` adds `*.gz binary` after `provenance.md` 6.5's `benchmarks/** eol=lf`, the later line winning, so no checkout converts a compressed byte. The committable-folder scanner (`reviewer/tests/support/local_paths.py`, lane P2) reads a file whose name ends `.gz` by its decompressed text. `test_committable_paths.py` gains, in the parity slice, `test_packed_streams_are_committable_only_in_the_study_and_pilot_trees` in place of RF-78's test, through `git check-ignore -q --no-index` and `git check-attr`, and the scanner's packed-stream row; and in the robustness slice `test_committed_run_folders_hold_packed_streams_matching_their_digests`, over every run folder under the two trees whose `run-provenance.json` git tracks: a tracked `event-streams.json`; a tracked `events.jsonl.gz` beside every tracked `session.json`; every tracked `events.jsonl.gz` decompressing to bytes whose `sha256_of_bytes` is its row; every row naming a tracked `events.jsonl.gz`. A folder git does not track (a study still running, RK-15) is not checked; both tests are skipped without git, never passed. Why: at `be01974` no rule ignores `events.jsonl.gz` (`git check-ignore -v --no-index` names none for one under `benchmarks/studies/`), so a packed stream of a run written anywhere else in the checkout would be committable, against `.gitignore:74-75`'s reason (model output and reviewed document text); ignoring the plain form in the two trees keeps a working stream out of any commit; under `* text=auto` (`.gitattributes:2`) git would leave a gzip file unconverted only by its binary guess, which `binary` makes a rule. Alternatives: rely on the absence of a rule (a packed private run committable anywhere in the checkout); check the committed folders only at the paid slices' gates (a checkout whose stream was lost or edited would pass the default suite).

Corrections with no decision of their own, made in the same pass:
- `provenance.md` 3.2's run-time refusal table, the list `benchmark-run.md` 3 row 6 points to, gains RF-79's price entry as a "both" row from the robustness slice and holds its message; row 6 points to it rather than restating it. The resume sentence under the table names the two rows it re-applies (a clean tracked tree, no carriage return in a digested input): the price row needs no second reading, since `benchmark/prices.yaml` is committed and resume refuses another commit and a pane study's dirty tracked tree.
- `benchmark-run.md` 8: RF-77's inference recognises the Gemini request error by its type, `isinstance(error, GeminiRequestError)` on the exception that escaped, which is the adapter's own object (`agent/runner.py:1434-1442` records the failure and re-raises it), never by its message, its `error_class` text or the run's provider; `benchmark/attempt.py` imports the class inside that branch, so a run that never reaches it does not load `google-genai` (the adapters' lazy import, `agent/providers/__init__.py:661-670`). The scripted provider builds the case through section 15's `ScriptedTurn.fails`, now the `ProviderError` instance a turn raises after playing its scripted `rounds`: one round with usage, then `fails=GeminiRequestError("gemini 400 ...")`, built from its message alone as `_map_error` builds it (`gemini_provider.py:853-864`; RF-72), for the inferred overflow, and the same turn with no rounds for the `provider_error`; `test_the_inferred_overflow_is_recognised_by_type` pins "by type" both ways.
- RF-78 records the repository-size arithmetic, and that 28A answers it.

### 9.13 Owner decision 30A and two task defaults (2026-09-30)

Taken on 2026-09-30 while the tasks were revised. 30A answers owner question 8 (§9.7) with its option B: held-out core cases take no answer turn; every verdict names dropped reasoning as untested by it; the anchors show it with paired intervals (FR-059). CS-9 and RF-14 stand as the plan's rule, no longer an interim, and slice 4's tasks stand as written (tasks.md T103, done). RF-85 and RF-86 settle the two points the tasks had left to the owner; each is a reversible task-level default. Line numbers are at `be01974`.

- **RF-85** (tasks.md T120, T121: `benchmark score` over a keyed saved set given `--answer-keys`; `cases.md` 4.1, RF-4). Decision: refused before any key is read, a usage error (exit 2) with one line, "a keyed set names its own answer keys; drop --answer-keys", checked in `cli.py` beside the keyless set's usage error (`cli.py:2367-2379`); T121 writes the sentence into `cases.md` 4.1's `key` row with the code. Why: a keyed set names every entry's key (row 6), so a directory given beside it is either ignored in silence, which hides a mistyped command, or read, which scores against keys the set does not name; the refusal costs one retyped command and leaves no doubt which keys scored the run. Alternatives: the entries' keys win and the directory is ignored (`sets.key_path`'s reading, which `benchmark set-summary` keeps, since it scores nothing); a usage error naming both the set's keys and the directory.
- **RF-86** (tasks.md T248 to T251: growth cases added after the pilot under `scoring.md` 9.4's version rule; FR-028, FR-049, FR-055, SC-013). Decision: growth units added after the approval keep `SCORING_RULES_VERSION`. Their oracle and null golden scorecards move `goldens_sha256`, so the record gains one row at the same version declaring `cases_added` (every added case id, twins included) with a non-blank `reason`. `version_record_failures` accepts such a row when its digest differs from the previous row's, and refuses a same-version row with a changed digest and no `cases_added`, a `cases_added` row with a blank or absent reason, one whose digest is the previous row's, and one declaring both `cases_added` and `goldens_unchanged`. So that such a row cannot carry a scoring change, the goldens other than its cases' must be the previous row's: while it is the record's last row, `goldens_sha256(folder, exclude_cases=<its cases>)`, which leaves out every `<case>--*.yml` file of those cases, must equal the previous row's `goldens_sha256`, and every listed case must have its oracle and null golden, both checked in the version-record test's record half over the committed golden folder (the base digest is by this rule the previous row's, so the row need record none of its own). A failing-first test task (T249) precedes the rule; T250 implements it in `tests/support/scoring_probes.py` and in that commit writes it into `scoring.md` 9.4, `scoring.md` 8's version sentence and `data-model.md`'s version record (a same-version row declaring `cases_added` under this rule, beside consecutive versions); the regeneration task, T251, alone appends the row. Why: adding cases changes no scoring rule, and a bump would refuse the paid pilot's committed scorecards at compare (`read_scorecard`) and disagree with the approved measurement's scorer version, forcing a re-score and a second approval of a size the owner has already approved; SC-013 still holds for every golden that existed before the row, since only the added cases' own goldens may change at the same version. Alternatives: bump the version, re-score the three pilot folders (reads saved sessions, pays nothing) and re-run `benchmark pilot` over them for a new measurement to approve; add the cases with no record row (the goldens' digest then fails the record's check).

### 9.14 Owner decisions 33A to 36A, applied (2026-10-06)

Taken on 2026-10-06 in the owner's review of the plan, done in CLAUDE.md's SMALL CHANGE form (31A, 32A; §1). RF-87 to RF-91 apply them; each is a reversible plan-level default under its decision, and every plan file it touches was edited to match. tasks.md gained seven tasks (T010, T011, T171, T205, T206, T221 and T244) and was renumbered by script, every reference moving with its task. Line numbers are at `be01974`.

- **RF-87** (33A: what the three gates run, what "unchanged" means, and the seat task). Decision: the gates of slices 1, 2 and 6 (tasks.md T041, T072, T220) run, from the repository root on Windows, `dotnet build extractor/SwReview.sln -c Release --nologo -v q` with zero warnings and `dotnet test extractor/SwReview.sln -c Release --no-build --nologo` against the baseline's counts (T001 records them), and a replay of the recorded pane sessions: the three committed replay fixtures through the replay gate's steps 2 and 3 (013 `contracts/tokens.md` section 5: `swreview benchmark replay` under every configuration of 008 `contracts/replay.md` section 9's latest row, with the example profile; no recorded finding lost or not replayable, the contacts reproduced, pass A within 1%), on the development machine. "Unchanged but for the evidence fields the plan allows" reads, slice by slice: in slice 1 every figure of that row unchanged, the pane's records differing only by one `"origin_marks": 1` in each session (RF-27); in slice 2 unchanged but for what D12's profile stamps move, which T062 re-measures (RF-28); in slice 6 unchanged, the pane's live stream gaining only `served_model` and `stop_reason` (RF-32), which its unit tests pin, since the replay's provider reports neither. The seat task, T221 (`[W]`, lane I), follows slice 6's gate: a smoke review of the small assembly in the Task Pane as the workstation test plan's step 4.1 runs it, passing when the review finishes and renders and its run folder holds exactly the plan's evidence (`served_model` on each `usage` body and no `retries` field, `"origin_marks": 1`, profile stamps with no folder, an LF `attention.json`, no `prompts.json`). It is written into the workstation handover, test plan and results sheet of its sitting as `014 T221`, and `reviewer/tests/support/seat_tasks.py`'s `PACKAGES` gains `014-design-review-eval`, so the existing handover and test-plan tests hold those documents to it (013 T138's form). Every paid run (P0, the pilot, the study, the private slice) waits for it. Why: the replay plays the pane's own review path over recorded sessions, so it sees a finding moved or lost, or a request changed, that a unit test of one module would not; the add-in's tests are the page's and the host's; the seat shows, once and before money is spent, what only a licensed SOLIDWORKS and the real pane can. Alternatives: the replay only where a slice regenerates the fixtures (the plan as it stood); a seat review after each of the three slices (three sittings for one question, the pane's live path being slice 6's).
- **RF-88** (34A: the migration's shape). Decision: tasks.md T010 writes one failing-first test per writer in its own test file (`test_attention_record.py`, `test_tokens.py`, `test_ir_loader_append.py`), each through a spy on `swreview.atomic.write_atomic` and with `os.replace` patched through `swreview.atomic.os`: one call with the exact bytes, an existing file replaced whole, the old bytes kept and no temporary left when the replace fails. T011 makes each writer one `atomic.write_atomic` call (`from swreview import atomic`, so one spy on the module attribute sees every call), removes the imports that leaves unused, and moves the three existing failure tests (`test_attention_record.py:168-188`, `test_ir_loader_append.py:182-198`, `test_checks_first_interference.py:306-331`) to patch `swreview.atomic.os`, what they assert unchanged. `write_atomic` names its temporary `.{name}.<random>.tmp` in the target's folder, the attention record writer's naming (`report/attention_record.py:128-135`), so that file's glob holds (T008, T009). Bytes: the vocabulary's are the source's and the package's are `merged_text` encoded UTF-8 (its text write used `newline="\n"`), both unchanged on every platform; the attention record's, written in text mode with no newline argument, were CRLF on Windows and are LF after it. That one change is benign: its readers parse JSON (`read_attention_record`, the chat server's attention route and the Review page through it), the handoff export copies the file whole, no committed file holds one, and the seat task reads one written so. Why: the rule existed three times before the eval; one helper with one test file is the repetition removed, and a byte pin per writer catches a writer the migration would otherwise move silently. Alternatives: migrate each in the slice that first touches its module (slice 3 for `ir/loader.py`), one change spread over three slices; keep the record's text mode through a `newline` keyword on `write_atomic` (a second spelling of one rule).
- **RF-89** (35A: P0's set, its folders and the live test). Decision: P0 runs a committed, generated two-package set, `benchmarks/sets/pane-smoke.json`: `committed.json`'s entries for `kalo-1` (a core case, two held-out model-produced defects) and `kalo-fit-1` (R-3's anchor, with a case file, so the answer driver runs), each equal to its `committed.json` entry and in that file's order, written by the generator from one constant, `PANE_SMOKE_CASES`, and pinned by `test_committed_benchmark_set.py` (tasks.md T126, T147, T151). P0 is the pane study's two arms at one repetition each, with the pilot's provider, model, effort, explanation setting and profile and the `--jobs` the owner plans, into `benchmarks/pilots/<study folder>/smoke-off` and `smoke-on`, so it meets every run-time refusal the pilot would; its folders are scored, reported under `$TMP` and read row by row, then packed and committed with the pilot's, since `benchmark pilot --smoke` reads their review times (RF-91). A wiring fault is fixed by a task of its own, failing test first, and P0 runs again from the fix's commit into fresh folders, the failed ones never committed; the pilot runs from the commit of the P0 that passed. The live test (`reviewer/tests/live/test_benchmark_live_attempt.py`, T171) drives one real attempt of `kalo-fit-1` through `cli.app`, from a one-entry set under `tmp_path` naming the committed files by absolute path (a set outside the repository names absolute paths, `cases.md` 4.2), then `score` and `report`, and asserts the row's fields present and non-trivial; it is skipped without a key, skips on an authentication, rate-limit or 5xx refusal as the other live tests treat one, and counts as green only on `1 passed, 0 skipped`; it imports the slice's new modules inside the test, so it collects from the slice's first commit. Why: a committed set makes P0 one command on any machine and its saved set the same everywhere; `benchmarks/sets/pilot.json` is the lever studies' smoke set already and holds no held-out case, which P-3 and the pane study's rules need; the live test is the one assertion of P0 that a machine, not a reader, checks. Alternatives: a set file written under `$TMP` with absolute paths (a hand-made input to a paid, committed run); a `--packages` option on `benchmark run` (a new option, and a saved set that differs from its set file); P0's folders kept out of the repository (the projection's on-arm input then not reproducible).
- **RF-90** (36A: `benchmark run --jobs N`). Decision: one package scheduler in `benchmark/runner.py`, which `run_benchmark` and `resume_benchmark` both call, runs on the calling thread only: it re-hashes the profile and starts an attempt for the next package in set order while fewer than N are in flight, polls every attempt in flight in slices of `WAIT_SLICE_S`, and, as each ends, records it, promotes a kept attempt and prints its line. `benchmark/attempt.py`'s `run_attempt` splits into `start_attempt` (the daemon worker started, RF-50) and `RunningAttempt.poll(now)` (W, A and G applied without blocking), `run_attempt` becoming the two in a loop, so its tests stand. Every write of `run-status.json` is that thread's, packages in set order whatever order they end in, so no two writes interleave and the record needs no lock; the one value two attempt bodies share, the run-start adapter's one-shot holder (RF-60), is taken under a `threading.Lock`. Each package keeps its own attempt folders, stream, listener, adapter, retry loop and daemon worker. A moved profile starts no further attempt and lets those in flight end; Ctrl-C reaches the scheduling thread, which sets every stop in flight, records each attempt `interrupted` and propagates (RF-22). Lines print whole as packages end; `--json` keeps set order. `--jobs` is an integer of at least 1, default 1, a usage error below; `RunProvenance.jobs` records it from slice 6, exempt from must-match by name in compare (`provenance.md` 4), in the handoff allowlist, and no ledger column (the header regenerates once, in slice 2); `benchmark resume` runs at the recorded N and refuses `--jobs` typed, since every setting of a resumed run comes from its records. Why: one waiting thread keeps RF-22's interrupt and RF-50's exit guarantee as they are and makes the shared record single-writer by construction, the guarantee "under a lock or per package" asks for; N changes no review's inputs, only when each starts, so it measures nothing and its exemption is safe; the tests pin N = 1 to the sequential driver's lines, payload and record bytes. Alternatives: a driver thread per package running `run_attempt` (Ctrl-C reaches only the main thread, so a second interrupt path, and non-daemon drivers would hold the process at exit); a process per package (loses the in-process scripted tests and the one status record, as RF-50 weighed); resume taking `--jobs` (a run whose record no longer says how it ran).
- **RF-91** (36A: the projected wall clock). Decision: `benchmark pilot` takes `--smoke <folder>` twice, P0's off and on runs, refused unless they are one of each arm of the pilot's pane study at the pilot runs' provider, model, effort and commit, over a saved set every pilot run's saved set holds, each scored with every package kept (`verdict.md` P-14); and `--jobs N` (default 1), recorded as `sizing.jobs`. The projection, `pilot.project_wall_clock`: a package's off-arm time is its mean `wall_clock_s` over its counted pilot runs; its on-arm time that times the smoke ratio, P0's on-arm package times summed over its off-arm ones; a growth package takes the committed core cases' and twins' mean off-arm time; one run's wall clock is the list schedule of its packages' times in set order on N slots, each package starting on the first slot to free, which is the schedule `--jobs` runs; the study's is R times one off run plus one on run, the runs one at a time; a package with no counted run makes it unknown, never 0. It is printed with the cost gate, beside the mean tokens per minute of one off-arm review (so N times it can be held under the account's limit, plan RK-18), and stored in `sizing.projected_wall_clock`; `smoke_runs` joins the measurement's computed blocks under `sizing_digest`, and no rate, fit, cost or ledger row reads it. The owner chooses N at the cost gate and may project at another N as at another cap, by deleting the unapproved file. Why: the pilot measures the off arm only, and P0 is the one paid measurement of the on arm's speed; the list schedule is what the scheduler does, so the projection is the runner's own arithmetic, deterministic and checkable by hand. Alternatives: the total time over N (blind to imbalance, optimistic at small N); the off arm's times for both arms (the plan's upper estimate, with no P0 input); a projection in compare at the verdict (too late for the gate).

Corrections with no decision of their own, made in the same pass:
- RF-31's and RF-74's line ranges, `report/attention_record.py:126-140` and `ir/loader.py:179-192`, read `:126-145` and `:177-195` at `be01974`, each the whole block from the temporary's declaration to its clean-up; the plan, `benchmark-run.md` 4 and tasks.md cite the corrected ranges.
- `cases.md` 6.1 calls `benchmarks/sets/pilot.json` the lever studies' smoke set; P0's set is named `pane-smoke.json` so that the two cannot be confused.
- FR-047 and SC-012 except the paid review calls; the seat task is one, a paid review of the pane itself on the licensed seat, so neither changes, and the plan's "SOLIDWORKS is never started" now reads "by the eval or its tests".

#### Implementation follow-ups (2026-10-07)

Found by the verification of Phase 2's new and moved code. Each refines its task within that task's own contract, test first, and moves no byte that a review, `benchmark run`, `score` or `compare` writes.

- `atomic.write_atomic` (T009): the temporary's removal in its `finally` runs under `contextlib.suppress(OSError)`, so a removal that fails beside a failing `os.replace` never masks the replace's error (`test_atomic.py::test_a_clean_up_that_fails_too_never_hides_the_replace_s_error`).
- `agent.events.replay_events` (T013): a line cut inside a multibyte UTF-8 character (a truncated write of non-ASCII text such as a tolerance sign) ends the replay at that line and is reported through `on_unreadable` with its number, as a line that is not JSON is, in the plain and the packed form, never raising `UnicodeDecodeError` and never losing an earlier line that shares its read: the stream is read in text mode, as T013 opened it, with `errors="surrogateescape"`, so each byte that is not UTF-8 reads as a lone surrogate, and a line holding one is unreadable. The follow-up first read the stream as bytes, which stopped a lone carriage return from ending a line; owner decision 38A (§1) restored the universal newlines of `be01974`. `test_event_lines.py` pins both forms, a later event appended to the cut line, a lone carriage return ending a line and counted in the line's number, and a damaged packed stream read with `on_unreadable=None` returning its prefix without raising.
- `tools.registry.read_stored_result` (T015): a file nested past the interpreter's limit (`json.loads` raising `RecursionError`) and an envelope whose `status` is missing or not a string are `Unstored("unreadable")`, so `StoredResult.status` is always a string; `_store` has written `status` since feature 008, so the replay classes every recorded call as before (`test_stored_result.py`).
- `test_stored_result.py` imports `stored_file` from `test_replay_accounting.py` beside the names it already imports, in place of its own copy.
- `agent/events.py`'s docstrings put the benchmark's callers in the future tense: the benchmark's readers move onto `replay_events` in US3, US6 and US7, and `swreview benchmark pack` is US7's.
- Owner decision 40A's measured consequence (§1; `tools/checks_interference.py`). Unlike the follow-ups above, 40A moves bytes a review writes: each interference finding now cites its step. The big-assembly replay fixture, regenerated with 008 `contracts/replay.md` section 8's command and compared with the ids, timestamps and elapsed times each generation mints set aside, changed in its three interference findings' `tool_result_ids` (steps 14, 17 and 18, in `session.json` and in their `finding` events) and otherwise only where the model reads that one more id per interference finding: (a) those three steps' `result_bytes` and `result_tokens`, each up by 2; (b) every later recorded round's usage, up by 2, 4 or 6 tokens, since the generator sizes those calls from the recorded growth (008 `contracts/replay.md` section 8): turn 0 by 130 and the follow-up round by 6, the recorded total from 10,582,573 to 10,582,709; (c) the session's `finding_explanation_fingerprint`. 40A named a small token change ("slice 1's replay gate allows the tiny token change"), and (a) to (c) are taken as its direct consequence. The two small fixtures record no interference finding and do not move. The pane fixture moves by the three step ids and its resume cost, 346,319 input tokens from 346,313; `test_replay_fixtures.py`'s recorded total and follow-up round and `test_model_reads_the_recorded_title.py`'s full-view digest of `check_interference_group` are re-pinned with the reason. The replay gate's figures are 008 `contracts/replay.md` section 9's row "Feature 014 owner decision 40A": no requested figure moves.

Found by the verification of slice 1 (tasks.md T020 to T041), in the same terms: each refines its task within that task's own contract, test first where it is code, and moves no byte that a review, `benchmark run`, `score` or `compare` writes.

- `answer_key.load_answer_key` (T037): a key file that is missing, that cannot be read, or that is not UTF-8 is refused as an `AnswerKeyError` whose message starts "answer key {file name}:", "cannot be read ({error})" for an `OSError` (its `strerror`, refined below) and "not UTF-8 ({error})" for a `UnicodeDecodeError`, before any row runs, as a file that is not JSON is; neither error escapes the loader any longer (`test_answer_key.py`, one test each).
- `answer_key_model.AnswerKey` (T021): an explicit JSON `null` for `key_only` is refused naming the field, as `answer-key.md` 2.1's "optional, `true` only" reads, and `tests/support/answer_keys.write_key` states `key_only` only when its caller does. The model's docstring says that an explicit `null` for `references`, on a check that is not reference-keyed, and for `held_out_against`, on a tuned-on entry, reads as absent; `test_answer_key.py` pins both.

Found by the verification of slice 2 (tasks.md T042 to T072), in the same terms: each refines its task within that task's own contract, test first where it is code, and moves no byte that a review, `benchmark run`, `score` or `compare` writes but the two the bullets name (a record compare now refuses, and a handoff manifest's records).

- `test_efficiency_settings.py` (T043): `test_a_plain_baseline_refuses_a_model_view_switch` checked that the refusal named the switch typed, which passed whatever happened, since `check_study_arm` is told that a switch was typed, not which (`model_view_typed: bool`, T043), and names all three. - One inside-the-checkout rule (T051, T054, T068): `benchmark/sets.py`'s `checkout_relative(path, *, root) -> Path | None` (both resolved, then `is_relative_to`, the path relative to the root or `None`) replaces the five places that wrote it: the verifiers' three, `sets._path_form`, `cli._private_packages` and `runner.committable`, and two more found beside them, `compare._ledger_dir` and the pane study's carriage-return row in `cli._refuse_a_pane_study_breaking_its_file_rules`. `runner` imports the name, `cli` and `compare` call `sets.checkout_relative`, and `committable` asks `git check-ignore` about the path relative to the checkout. `runner.committable`, `tree_dirty` and `profile_is_example` take one signature, `root` by keyword with no default, as `profile_is_example` already did and `checkout_relative` does: `benchmark run` and `compare` read `sets.REPO_ROOT` once per invocation and pass it on (T051), and a default is bound once, at import, so a call that left the root out would ask about the import-time checkout, not the one the command read, while with no default it fails; every caller already passed it (`tree_dirty(root)` becomes `tree_dirty(root=root)`). `test_benchmark_sets.py` pins the helper and, through a spy patched where each caller looks the name up, that every reading goes through it; `test_run_provenance.py` pins the signature. `provenance.md` 6.4 states the rule.
- The committable-path scanner (T055, T056; `provenance.md` 6.6): pinned that a `.jsonl` file and a decompressed `events.jsonl.gz` are read line by line, a `\`-escaped path found decoded on each line and a line that is not JSON read as its text. Changed: an HTML end tag (`</td>`, `</TABLE>`, `</p >`) and a self-closing tag's `/>` start no rooted path, while a path written straight after `<` (`</srv/x>`) still does, since slice 5 may commit report pages; a path that starts at a fictional root and climbs out of it with `..` is flagged, the root checked again once the `..` are applied (`posixpath.normpath`), while one whose `..` stays inside passes; a drive letter and its backslash start a path glued to a word character too (plain text quoting an escaped line, `...\nE:\...`), through a second use of `scramble.WINDOWS_ANCHOR` kept only when it ends in `:\`, so the anchor is still imported, never restated, while a UNC anchor's two backslashes and a drive letter with `/` (a URL's scheme) still start a path only at a token boundary. `provenance.md` 6.6 and the scanner's docstring state these rules.

Found by the verification of the key extensions (tasks.md T079 to T087), in the same terms: each refines its task within that task's own contract, test first where it is code, and moves no byte that a review, `benchmark run`, `score` or `compare` wrote before T087.

- `compare`'s run row (T087), an implementation decision approved by the orchestrator, not by the owner, who may reverse it. T087 has `finding_origins` also name each withdrawn forbidden hit (`scoring.md` 5.5) while the aggregate's two counts by origin keep reading the final state, through `scorecard.final_state_origins`; the ledger's run row read `by_origin(score.finding_origins.values())`, so its "findings by origin" cell would have counted withdrawn hits as findings and disagreed with the aggregate of the same run. T087's "`benchmark/compare.py` and the ledger do not move" is read as "the ledger's output does not move": `compare._row` reads its findings by origin through `final_state_origins`, one line, which tasks.md T087 records after its task text. No committed key holds a forbidden entry, so no committed ledger byte could move either way, and the ledger check exits 0. `test_benchmark_compare.py` pins a run row whose scorecard holds a withdrawn hit, and `test_scorecard.py` the aggregate over the real regrade's stream; each fails when `final_state_origins` returns every origin. Reversing it, by reading `finding_origins` whole, makes the ledger's cell count withdrawn hits that the scorecard's aggregate does not.
- `tests/support/scoring_probes.stand_in_package` (T085): the stand-in also holds the ids a key's extension lists name, a forbidden entry's parts and documents, an unanswerable request's documents and a question-count rule's ids, a rule's id a document where the key names it one and a component instance otherwise (`questions.md` 5.3 allows either), so the oracle of a key-only key whose rule with `min` 1 names ids asks its one question, where it met an error envelope and read a miss (`test_scoring_probes.py`).
- `scorecard._score_package` (T087) takes `words: Words` by keyword with no default, the words `score_run` loads once, in place of loading them per package for the coverage reading; `scoring_probes` passes `load_words()`, as every later direct caller (tasks.md T131, T153, T209, T210) will (`test_scorecard.py`: one load for a run of two packages that each grade coverage).
- `test_scorecard.py` (T086) pins the aggregate's question lines of `scorecard.md` (`questions.md` 6): each reads "not assessed" when no package assesses its column and "{total} ({k} of {n} packages)" where some do, re-asks split into seen and refused, and a column assessed with nothing in it reads 0.

- `test_scorecard.py` (T086, T087): `scoring.md` 3.3's three conditions that no test reached, since every withdrawn finding in the real regrade's stream is crediting, announced once and absent from the final state, are pinned on that stream edited: a withdrawn finding last announced `unresolved` or `checked_within_scope` is no hit (each recast body still validates as a `Finding`); an id announced again after its withdrawal and held by the final session is a forbidden hit and a false alarm, never a withdrawn hit; an id announced on the pin and then on the plate's instance alone before its withdrawal is judged by that last body; and a last body that is no `Finding` is skipped, the column still assessed. Each fails under the verifier's mutation (the status test dropped, the final-state test dropped, the first body kept, the validation failure raised).
- `test_benchmark_questions.py` (T081, T082): `questions.md` 5.2's two counting rules that no test reached: a `part_roles` rule counts the part-roles question as 1 beside the drawing candidate question, also written by code; and R-3's refused follow-up (the plate's hole alone, `already_answered`) counts for Q-2 over `cmp:0001` and `cmp:0002` with max 1, giving 2, a miss. - `runner.committable` (T054): git is asked `check-ignore -q -- <path>`, so a folder named like an option is asked about as a path, where `-x` made git refuse the command and `--stdin` read standard input, both reading committable; `test_committable_paths.py` pins an ignored `-x/` and `--stdin/` reading not committable. `provenance.md` 6.4 says so.
- The glued drive rule (T056; `provenance.md` 6.6), kept as the contract words it, by decision: since a drive letter and its backslash start a path wherever they stand, plain text such as `regex:\d+` or `note:\n then` reads as a drive path (`x:\d+`, `e:\n`), a false alarm on the refusing side. Narrowing it to a drive letter straight after a backslash escape (`\n`, `\t`) would pass a path typed against a word (`the run wrote{path}`, `x_9{path}`), which `test_a_drive_path_glued_to_a_word_character_is_flagged` pins as flagged, and a path the scanner misses is a silent leak into a public repository where a false alarm is a loud test failure, met and fixed before a commit. `test_text_like_an_escape_after_a_colon_reads_as_a_glued_drive_path` pins the choice, so changing it is a change to 6.6.

Found by the verification of the generator's machinery and seed kinds (tasks.md T124 to T129), in the same terms: each refines its task within that task's own contract, test first where it is code, and moves no byte that a review, `benchmark run`, `score` or `compare` writes. The three points that changed a contract went to the owner, who settled them on 2026-10-08 as 54A (the Resilient Modeling group words in the case vocabulary), 55A (`coaxiality_undrawn`'s twin carries no correct call) and 56A (`DimensionPlace.nominal_text`), each option A (§1).

- `generator.md` 3's `joint_absent` row (T127): "nothing in the holes" is not enough. The joint map pairs two adjacent coaxial holes whether or not anything sits in them, and judges an empty pair's alignment with the fastener size their Hole Wizard size names (`checks/joint_alignment.py` `fastener_size`), so a hole-pair seed's planted offset on adjacent holes would be a code-produced `hole.nominal_alignment`. The row now adds that the two holes' faces stand more than the joint rules' 1 mm adjacency apart along their axis (`checks/joint_rules.yaml` `adjacency_gap_mm`), which `check_hole_alignment`, reading the two axes, does not need. `seeds.py`'s docstring and the test design (`quill`'s empty pairs stop 2 mm short of the joint face) already did so; tasks.md T127 and the design rules' rule 6 now say it. T131's leg (a) proves it on every hole-pair case.
- `SeedKind`'s signatures (T126): T126 wrote `references(at)`, `correct_call(at)` and `decoys(at)`. The kind's callables take `(at, places)`, since its forms and calls read the design's records with every form applied, and the correct calls are plural, `bottoming_thread_depth_unknown` making one call per screw into its hole; `parts`, `documents` and `optional_roles` sit beside them. The T126 line records the landed names, which T131 builds against.
- The group commit's message said each seed kind was verified against the real tools on the test design. Only the R-7 tree flags (`test_tree_specs_lays_a_compliant_tree_and_each_flag_fails_only_its_rule`) and, with 55A, `coaxiality_undrawn`'s unresolved clean form go through real tools in the committed tests; the other kinds' statuses are T131's validation to prove, on every committed case. The amended message says so.
- `callout_drill_only` on `cover-blind-tap`'s hole row (T128, for T144): `generator.md` 3's planted cell said the model hole carries a thread depth, and `seeds.py` refused every callout on a hole without one, so T144's D-2 could not sit on the row D-1 (`bottoming_thread_depth_unknown`) leaves with no thread depth, a row `cases.md` 6.4 says never had one. A callout needs the model hole's thread depth only where it states one: every drawing kind's clean form, and the thread and the count that differ, which refuse a hole with none (`seeds._thread_depth`, a `ValueError` the render turns into a refusal naming the case); the drill-only plant and every drawing kind's correct call read only the hole's thread, count and drill depth. A core case still states its hole's thread depth, through its twin. The kind's words, which are D-2's key description, now say only that the callout states the drill depth, and the row's planted cell says which holes carry a thread depth. `test_a_drill_only_callout_sits_on_a_hole_row_with_no_thread_depth` lays D-1 then D-2 on a row like `cover-blind-tap`'s and renders "4X M6x1.0 - 6H DRILL ↧ 14" with D-2's `record_drawing_finding` call resolved; three tests pin the refusals.
- Row 5 (T126): `_row_5_drawing_documents` reads each seed's references on the design's records, and a kind that cannot read them (a `bottoming_thread_depth_unknown` seed on a hole no screw goes into) let a bare `ValueError` out. It now refuses as a `CaseSpecError` naming the case, as `render._laid` does, since section 10 starts every refusal "case spec {case id}:"; `test_row_5_a_kind_refusing_the_design_s_records_names_the_case` pins it.
- The R-7 tree's standards half (T125, T129): `test_tree_specs_lays_a_compliant_tree_and_each_flag_fails_only_its_rule` ran the RMS tools only, so `standards.part.sketches_fully_defined`, the fourth kind on the `sketches_defined` flag, went unchecked. Run over the example profile, it was unresolved on every tree, planted or clean: `tree_specs`' sketches carried no text-segment count, and the standards check cannot rule out its sketch-text exemption without one (`checks/standards/part.py`). `tree_specs` now reads every sketch with no text (`text_segments=0`), so the check fails on the under-defined Core sketch and passes the compliant tree, as `mir-sitting` and `mir-sitting-positive` (T143) need; the test runs the standards part checks beside the RMS tools.
- The tree's global variable (T126) was named "Width", the one generated name outside the case vocabulary, passing only because the names check reads no equation (`generator.md` 8 lists none). It is "Size" now, and `test_a_treed_case_and_its_twin_pass_the_names_check` holds every global variable's name to the vocabulary.
- Read and left as it is: `render_all()` renders each case twice, once through `render_spec` and again inside `set_entries`. It is correct and cheap at the test design's size; T151 may revisit it if the committed set's render grows slow.

Found by the implementation of `kalo`'s R-4 to R-6 anchor cases (tasks.md T134), in the same terms: each stays inside its task's contract, and no committed output moves, since nothing under `benchmarks/` is rendered before T151.

- `kalo-contact-positive`'s pair (T134; `cases.md` 6.3), a reading decided by the orchestrator on 2026-10-08, not by the owner, who may reverse it. T134 and `cases.md` 6.3 said the second pin, 0.750 mm off the axis of a 3.1 mm hole, overlaps "the plate"; in `kalo` the plate holds the pins' Ø3.0 holes, line to line, and the cover the Ø3.1 ones. Taken: `pin_b` stays line to line in the plate's `dowel_b`, the cover's `cdowel_b` moves 0.750 mm off its axis (`alignment_offset_placed`), and `interference_positive` is keyed on `pin_b` and the cover. The fixture row the case copies is the offset pin's with the part holding the Ø3.1 hole (`generate_fixtures.py:679`, `cmp:0025` with `cmp:0004`, whose hole is `:444-445`), and T129's `alignment_offset_placed` docstring sets the pin 0.750 mm off the axis of that 3.1 mm clearance hole; §3 R-4 names the part the plate because its one-part design holds both holes. T134's line and `cases.md` 6.3 now read "overlapping the cover", citing this bullet. Leg (a) reports `interference.static` and `hole.nominal_alignment` on the case, both code-produced and demonstrated, as T129 decided, so the stop point T129 and T134 set did not fire.
- Six implementation choices made in T134, kept by the same decision of the orchestrator, not of the owner, who may reverse any of them:
  1. The dowel holes are one-instance Hole Wizard rows, one per pin: `dowel_a` and `dowel_b` in the plate (Ø3.0) under `cdowel_a` and `cdowel_b` in the cover (Ø3.1), where T132 laid two two-instance rows, `dowels` and `cdowels`. `seeds._offset_hole` moves its hole record whole (`lay` adds `offset_mm` to every instance) and the kind keys `#1` of the seat and the hole, so a two-instance row would have moved both pins' holes. T132's and T133's cases still pass `check_design('kalo')`.
  2. R-4's three interference rows are places of the base, laid in every variant: `pin_a_plate` and `pin_b_plate` at zero volume, and `pin_b_cover`, the possible row, with no volume and stating the 1.237 mm³ overlap `interference_positive` plants. The core and R-3 cases carry the contacts too, which the interference check reads as contacts, no finding (leg (a)); the possible row lies between `pin_b` and the cover so that the seed turns that one row into the measured overlap (`seeds._contact_between` takes the one contact place between the pair).
  3. The variant `UNDRAWN` ingests no drawing, for `kalo-contact`, `kalo-contact-positive` and `kalo-lightweight`: §3 R-5 grades no fit defect because "the case carries no drawing", and names R-4's package its twin. Each of the three also passes on the drawn base; the variant follows the recorded anchors, it is not forced by the validation.
  4. The variant `SCREWS_ELSEWHERE` (`kalo-empty`) lays no screw and adds, on the plate alone, `mounts`, M8 counterbored through holes (an ISO 4762 M8's: a Ø9.0 medium-fit through hole under a Ø14.0 counterbore 8.6 mm deep; two instances where §3 R-6 laid five), and `tap6`, M6x1.0 with a 10 mm thread in a 12 mm hole; no cover hole lies over either, so the joint map pairs neither. The base's `taps` stay, empty, under the cover's `clrs`. `HoleShape` gains `counterbore_mm` and `faces` for `mounts`. `kalo-empty-positive` reads the base, its screws laid, so its known defect sits in a package of its own beside R-6's forbidden entries (FR-009).
  5. A document place's `opened` reaches `PackageBuilder.document`, and every instance of an unopened document is laid `suppression="lightweight"` with no hole row, face, mesh or interference row on it (`kalo._opened`; `tests/support/mechanical.py:471-577`), so `component_lightweight`, which sets `opened=False` on the pin's document, leaves both pins lightweight and the package with no interference row, as §3 R-5's seed lays it.
  6. A contact is laid `is_possible` only while it carries no volume: `seeds._overlap` replaces only the row's volume, so once `interference_positive` plants the overlap the row is a measured overlap, not SOLIDWORKS's possible-interference flag for coincident or touching faces (`ir/models.py`'s `Interference.is_possible`); the interference check judges it by its volume (`checks/interference.py` `_contact`).
- Read and left as it is: 1.237 mm³ is the volume the fixture row states, not the crescent a 3.0 mm pin 0.750 mm off a 3.1 mm hole cuts through the 4 mm cover (about 8 mm³; the fixture's 6 mm face, about 12 mm³). The interference check reads the row's volume and never the faces, and T134 and `cases.md` 6.3 pin the number, so the case keeps it.


## Integration checkpoint: accepted fine-pitch baseline

Integrated `010-fine-pitch` through its final gate commit `a5b4ed8` into the slice-4
baseline `ee6571c`. The fixture helper keeps both branches’ fastener builders, cosmetic
threads and feature rows, with one fastener collection and one constructor argument.
The parser and joint-reference tests keep both behaviours. Both historical replay rows
are retained, and the combined code’s measured row is in feature 008’s replay contract.

Held-out items cited by this integration: **none**. The fine-pitch implementation and
its acceptance fixtures were established independently of the held-out corpus; its
existing `cover-blind-tap` replay anchor is already tuned-on. No held-out item was used
to change the thread rules, prompt or tool description. The integration adds the
`check_threads` description/schema to the offered-tool digest, so `stamps.CURRENT`
records the new digest and the generated held-out keys are resealed without changing
case tags. The prompt and checklist digests are unchanged.

Validation: the initial focused run had one expected stale-stamp failure and 250 passes;
after updating the generator stamp and regenerating, held-out stamps, case reachability,
validation, generator reproducibility, replay, checks-first and pane-fixture tests passed
(591 passed, one skipped). The ten replay configurations and lever-14-off pair were
measured over all three committed fixtures: no finding lost or not replayable, and the
62, 7 and 5 additional checks-first findings remain. No replay fixture or real recording
was regenerated or read. The combined summary still meets the held-out floor; its
exact counts are recorded with the slice-4 gate.
